# V1-056 plan

Written 2026-10-03 by Claude Code under ops/V1_056_TAKEOVER.md section 1. Status: APPROVED by the owner on 2026-10-03 with the changed order; owner answers are in section 6.

Scope: Final Settlement, deposits, refunds and permanent correction history. Rules: DL-068, DL-069 and the docs 001, 002 and 003 amendments in commit 6732b8e. Read with AGENTS.md, ops/V1_055_RUN_RULES.md and ops/V1_056_TAKEOVER.md. The owner asked for this plan file in the repo; that request overrides the run-rules line that forbids tools from saving plan files.

## 1. What exists today (read-only survey)

- Capabilities are code strings checked by the application (for example `deposits.dispose`, `refunds.create`, `invoices.void`, `invoices.adjust`, `credits.apply`). The database checks the active actor and Property lists. There is no capability catalog table; access administration answers "unavailable". Adding `financial.approve` is therefore a code constant plus database function checks, not a catalog row.
- Refunds: `app.refunds` (Reservation Deposit, migration 21) and the Credit/Pet Refund services (migration 41, `refund.py`). Refunds run at once; there is no draft step and no Refund method column.
- Deposit dispositions run at once through `deposits.dispose` for three grounds only: `BR-DEP-022` (pet noncompliance), `BR-DEP-023` (voluntary early), `BR-LEASE-005` (unpaid Initial obligation). Termination bases are `VOLUNTARY_EARLY`, `CUSTOMER_VIOLATION`, `SIGNED_LEASE_OTHER` and `UNPAID_INITIAL_OBLIGATION`.
- Payment Allocation reversal exists (`payment_allocation.py`, `reverse_allocation`).
- Move-out inspection findings carry `pet_related` (migration 60). One-time charges and Damage charges exist (migration 65). The final Utility Invoice exists (migration 72). The Property due-days setting exists (9b).
- Units have no cleaning rate. Payment Methods are a global table `app.payment_methods`.
- Lease `end_date` is optional in the Lease draft and is checked only as later than the Start Date.
- Final Settlement has no code, no table and no traceability entry. docs/004 (DB-SETTLE-001 to 006) and docs/005 (API-070 to 072) already describe the design.
- Last migration: `20261010_75`. New migrations start at `20261011_76`.
- Traceability has 2445 entries, all `implemented`. 12 of the 13 new DL-069 rule IDs have no test (only BR-CREDIT-014 is mapped).

## 2. Order changes and why

- Refund methods and the joint-Signer recipient move from position 7 to position 3. Reason: slices 6 (Pet Deposit rest refunded) and 9 (Settlement Refunds) create Refunds, and every new Refund must carry a method and a reference note. Building the method list first avoids a later rework of those slices.
- Final Settlement (old 8) is split into two slices (draft and preview, then confirm and close). One slice would be too big for one checkpoint.
- HTTP and screens (old 10) are split into five slices, one per area, each with its own commit and security review.
- I recommend a security review also for slice 5 (hand-back VOID and credit Adjustment) and slice 10 (post-settlement corrections), because both change what a Customer owes. The takeover list does not require it; the owner decides (Q17).

## 3. Slices

Each slice: one local checkpoint commit, explicit paths, secret and diff scan, independent review (fresh subagent) before the commit, review saved under `ops/reviews/`, one line in ops/NOW.md after the commit. "Sec" means an extra security review.

### Slice 1 — Lease End Date is a month end (BR-LEASE-031, BR-LEASE-013)
- Change: Lease draft, amend and sign paths reject an End Date that is not the last day of its calendar month. Service validation in `lease.py` plus the same check inside the database draft/sign functions.
- Migration: `20261011_76` replaces the Lease draft and sign function bodies with the extra check. This rejects input that was accepted before, so it is a tightening change (Q1). No table-level CHECK, so existing rows are untouched.
- Tests: rejection of 30 May, acceptance of 31 May and 29 Feb 2028; database function rejects a direct call; no Rent Invoice for a month after the End Date (billing run over the End Date month and the next month). Existing fixtures with non-month-end End Dates (about 19 test files have dated fixtures; one known `2028-01-09`) need narrowing with docs/010 notes.
- Sec: no.

### Slice 2 — `financial.approve` and the draft-then-confirm model (BR-AUTH-010, BR-AUTH-008, BR-AUTH-009)
- Change: new capability constant `financial.approve`. New generic tables `app.financial_drafts` (id, Property, kind, payload JSON with fixed schema per kind, prepared_by, prepared_at, reason, expected financial revision) and `app.financial_draft_events` (append-only: CONFIRMED, REJECTED, WITHDRAWN, with actor, time, reason). A draft has no financial effect. Confirm runs the kind's existing command function in the same transaction, with the confirming actor, and fails if the source changed since the draft (financial revision check). Each draft is confirmed at most once (unique partial index on CONFIRMED).
- Migration: `20261012_77` additive (two tables, guard triggers that forbid UPDATE and DELETE, SECURITY DEFINER functions `app.record_financial_draft`, `app.confirm_financial_draft`, `app.close_financial_draft`, EXECUTE to the runtime role only).
- Tests: Staff without `financial.approve` cannot confirm (service and database); draft has no effect on balances, ledger or Deposit; confirm is idempotent and once only; stale draft rejected; reject and withdraw need a reason; history is append-only (UPDATE and DELETE refused).
- Sec: yes.

### Slice 3 — Refund methods and joint-Signer recipient (BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015)
- Change: configured Refund methods list (seeded with Cash and Bank transfer), managed by `property.manage`. New Refunds record a method and a reference note. Deposit Refund recipient is the Primary Tenant unless a recorded written agreement of all Signers names another Signer; the named person must be a Signer of that Lease. All amounts USD cents. No deadline.
- Migration: `20261013_78` additive: `app.refund_methods`, nullable `refund_method_id` and `reference_note` on new Refund rows (old rows keep null), a Refund draft kind, a recipient-agreement table.
- Tests: method required for every new Refund; inactive method rejected; note length bounds; recipient is the Primary Tenant by default; another Signer only with agreement text; a non-Signer rejected; USD only; old Refund rows unchanged.
- Sec: yes (old slice 7).

### Slice 4 — Unit cleaning rate and move-out cleaning charge (BR-UNIT-014, BR-MOVEOUT-013, BR-SVC-001, BR-MOVEOUT-006)
- Change: Unit cleaning rate in USD cents, set with `property.manage`, history kept. At move-out an authorized user issues the cleaning charge as a one-time charge from the Unit's rate at that moment (rate snapshot on the line). Due Date = issue date + Property due-days, with the existing BR-INV-018 override. Reduction only by a linked credit Adjustment with a reason. Refused when the Customer never moved in. Billed at most once per Lease.
- Migration: `20261016_81` additive: Unit cleaning rate history table, a link table that marks the cleaning charge (rate snapshot, Lease, Unit, move-out event, at most one per Lease), and the charge command. The charge uses the existing `MISCELLANEOUS_CHARGES` one-time charge category (owner answer to Q-2026-10-04-7, Option B, 2026-10-04).
- Tests: charge equals the rate at issue; later rate change leaves the charge unchanged; never-moved-in refused; second charge refused; Due Date default and override; reduction by credit Adjustment only.
- Sec: no.

### Slice 5 — Hand-back month (BR-RENT-030, BR-RENT-024, BR-RENT-026, BR-LEASE-021, BR-MOVEOUT-010)
- Change: a service that, for an early-terminated Lease with a hand-back date before the 5th, lets an authorized user (a) VOID the whole unpaid hand-back-month Rent Invoice with a reason, including fixed Service lines, and the next month's Invoice generated on the 25th when the hand-back is between the 25th and the month end; (b) keep a fully paid Invoice (no action); (c) remove the unpaid rest of a partly paid Invoice with a linked credit Adjustment and a reason. It checks the conditions and refuses otherwise. Nothing runs automatically. Uses existing `invoices.void` and `invoices.adjust` (Q8).
- Migration: `20261017_82` additive (eligibility read function and a guarded wrapper over the existing VOID and Adjustment functions). Scope per owner answers to Q-2026-10-04-8 and Q-2026-10-04-9 (docs/010).
- Tests: hand-back on the 4th unpaid gives whole VOID with Trash line; on the 5th refused (BR-RENT-024 applies); fully paid refused; partly paid gives credit Adjustment equal to the unpaid rest; hand-back on the 26th covers the next month's Invoice; notice date ignored; original Invoice and Payment rows unchanged.
- Sec: recommended (Q17).

### Slice 6 — Deposit forfeiture and Pet Deposit cases (BR-DEP-018, 019, 022, 023, 026, 027)
- Scope per owner answers to Q-2026-10-04-11 (2026-10-04): forfeiture drafts only; the BR-DEP-027 Pet Deposit outcome moved to slice 9; the 30-day default ground needs `BR-LEASE-028` and a recorded Serious Default event; the direct Lease disposition route answers 409; the unpaid-Initial-Rent termination command stops forfeiting (new relaxing migration). Also in this slice (Q-2026-10-04-10): the migration 70 two-line credit Adjustment bug (test first) and the refusal of a move-out correction that a hand-back resolution relies on.
- Change: forfeiture and Pet Deposit use become draft kinds confirmed by `financial.approve`. Forfeiture is always the whole Security Deposit and is never applied to what the Customer owes. New grounds: non-pet violation termination and 30-day Rent nonpayment default termination (Q10). Pet Deposit at those terminations and at unpaid-Initial termination pays valid pet damage first (findings with `pet_related`) and the rest is refunded through a Refund draft (slice 3). Pet noncompliance keeps both deposits (BR-DEP-022 unchanged).
- Migration: `20261016_81` additive: new disposition ground values, draft kinds, a guard that a forfeited Security Deposit cannot be the source of any application.
- Tests: each ground; forfeiture equals the full held amount; an application from a forfeited deposit is refused at the database; Pet Deposit pays pet damage first and the Refund draft gets the rest; pet noncompliance keeps both; draft has no effect until confirmed; audit fields per BR-DEP-019.
- Sec: yes.

### Slice 6b — Lease Deposit forfeiture reversal draft (BR-AUTH-010, BR-DEP-019; owner answers to Q-2026-10-04-13 and Q-2026-10-04-14)
- Scope (owner answer to Q-2026-10-04-13, 2026-10-04): the reversal of a confirmed Lease Deposit forfeiture becomes a draft that Staff prepare and a `financial.approve` holder confirms; until then (slice 6) the one-call reversal answers 409. The Deposit receipt reversal stays without a draft, like a Payment reversal.
- Change: a reversal draft recorded with a reason and an evidence reference, confirmed by running the existing reversal function with the approver as actor, so every earlier reversal rule applies; stale and replay rules as for the forfeiture draft. Tests in slices 6 that reverse a Lease disposition as the owner and whose subject is the reversal move to this draft path (docs/010 note per test).
- Moved here from slice 6 round 3 (owner answer to Q-2026-10-04-14):
  - a new Lease forfeiture draft must also refuse a disposition id that a Reservation Deposit draft, in any state, holds as its `outcome_id` (`payload->>'outcome_id'`), not only Lease drafts' `disposition_id`;
  - a test for a disposition id used only by a recorded disposition (no draft holds it);
  - the stale status lines (the "Known gap" line in ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-06-lease-deposit-forfeiture.md and older ops/NOW.md lines);
  - the draft read filter: a reader without `financial.approve` sees only drafts of the capability they may prepare (`prepare_capability`, not only the kind), so a holder of only `reservation_deposits.dispose` cannot read Lease forfeiture drafts and the reverse; needed before the slice 11b and 11c draft routes.
- Migration: `20261019_84` additive and relaxing (draft variant prepared under `deposits.correct`, record and confirm functions, capability-scoped draft reads, the disposition-id check change).
- Sec: yes (deposits, approvals).

### Slice 7 — Wrong-Unit Payment move (BR-PAY-042)
- Change: a draft records the source allocation, target Invoice and the confirmation text (both Customers, who, date, how). Confirm by `financial.approve` reverses the wrong allocation and allocates the same Payment to the target Invoice in one transaction. Blocked if the released value already became Customer Credit or a Refund that is not reversed. Payment amount, date and Receipt never change. No Credit moves between Customers.
- Migration: next free number, additive (draft kind, move link table, confirm function).
- Tests: move succeeds and both Invoices show correct balances; first Invoice becomes unpaid and later late-payment/nonpayment evaluation counts from the move, earlier records unchanged; blocked when Credit or Refund exists; Payment row and Receipt unchanged; Staff cannot confirm.
- Sec: yes.

### Slice 8 — Final Settlement draft and preview (BR-SETTLE-001 to 007, 016, 017, DL-032)
- Change: `final_settlements` (one per terminated Lease), obligation snapshots (Invoices, Late-payment Charges, cleaning charge), preview in DL-032 order: obligations, Customer Credit first, Pet Deposit to pet-related lines, explicit Security Deposit applications (not when forfeited), explicit remaining Pet Deposit (Customer choice or staff with reason), remaining balance, Refunds. Blocking issues listed (for example final Utility not issued, cleaning charge missing).
- Migration: next free number, additive (DB-SETTLE-001, 002, 004, 006 plus the draft kind).
- Tests: order is exactly DL-032; no inferred FIFO between Deposit types; forfeited Security Deposit never offered; normal Lease end offers Security Deposit then refund of the rest; preview changes nothing.
- Note (slice 4 review round 2): "cleaning charge missing" must check that the linked cleaning charge (`app.unit_cleaning_charges`) is on an issued, non-VOID Invoice, not only that the link row exists; a draft correction can swap the source and a VOID releases it. It must not report the charge as missing when the Customer never moved in or when the Unit's rate was zero at move-out (slice 4 refuses the charge in both cases, owner answers to Q13 and Q14).
- Note (slice 7 review round 3): Credit applications at Final Settlement must keep a Credit released from a moved (`WRONG_UNIT_MOVE`) allocation with its own Customer.
- Sec: yes.

### Slice 9 — Final Settlement confirm and close (BR-SETTLE-008 to 015, 016)
- Moved here (owner answer to Q-2026-10-04-11, 2026-10-04): the record that pays an Invoice from a Deposit (built once, used by DL-032 steps 3 to 5) and the BR-DEP-027 Pet Deposit outcome: valid pet damage first, the rest refunded through a Refund draft, at unpaid-Initial-Rent, non-pet violation and 30-day default terminations as well as at Final Settlement; pet noncompliance keeps both deposits (BR-DEP-022). Tests: Pet Deposit pays only pet-related Damage charges first; the rest becomes a Refund draft; nothing is applied before confirmation; outstanding amounts, Invoice status and the Customer Ledger count the Deposit application as paid; a forfeited Security Deposit can never be applied; unpaid-Initial-Rent and violation terminations follow the same Pet Deposit rule.
- Added (owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-19, 2026-10-05): a Refund of a Customer Credit released by a credit Adjustment to the Invoice Customer who is not the Payment's payer (BR-CREDIT-014; for example a Credit released from a wrong-Unit move to another Customer, or from a third-party Payment since migration 67). Today's Refund rules pay a Credit Refund only to the payer. It must be a Refund draft confirmed by `financial.approve`. Built in sub-slice 8f (migration `20261030_95`).
- Note (slice 7 extra review): today a Credit Refund to a recipient who is not the payer fails at confirmation with SQLSTATE 23514, which the Refund service reports as "outcome unresolved; replay"; map it to a conflict or refuse it when the draft is recorded. Built in sub-slice 8f: mapped to a conflict at confirmation.
- Change: confirm by `financial.approve` executes the chosen Credit applications, Deposit applications and Refunds in DL-032 order and closes the Settlement in one transaction, revalidating every source. The unpaid rest stays as debt on the Primary Tenant's Customer Ledger; no write-off. A closed Settlement is never reopened.
- Migration: next free number, additive (close function, closure snapshot, guards forbidding UPDATE of closed rows).
- Tests: atomic close; stale draft rejected; debt shows on the Primary Tenant's ledger for joint Signers; no reopen path exists at the database; every step is a separate linked record.
- Sec: yes.
- Merged into slice 8 (owner instruction, 2026-10-05): slice 8 now covers the whole Final Settlement, including the Deposit application record and the confirm. Every item of this slice 9 entry is placed in a slice 8 sub-slice below.

### Slice 8 design note (2026-10-05)

Written before any code, at the owner's request. Status: the owner answered Q-2026-10-05-20 (docs/010, 2026-10-05) and this note follows the answers; the owner answered Q-2026-10-05-21 the same day (BR-SETTLE-016, BR-DEP-014, PR-DEP-014 and BR-DEP-027 amended; at the BR-DEP-027 terminations a Pet Deposit rest without the Customer's choice is refunded and the debt stays; the DL-013 staff step stays at a normal end and at voluntary early leaving). Sub-slice 8a started. The read-only path search is recorded in Q-2026-10-05-20. Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-05-25): the DL-013 staff step stays at every termination except the three BR-DEP-027 terminations.

**Reading of the rules (checked against docs/010 DL-012, DL-013, DL-032, DL-068, DL-069 and the later notes, docs/002 BR-SETTLE-001 to 017, BR-DEP-022 to 027, BR-CREDIT-005 to 008 and 014, BR-REFUND-007 and 015, BR-AUTH-008 to 010):**

- One Final Settlement per TERMINATED Lease (BR-SETTLE-001). Staff start and prepare it under `final_settlements.prepare`; only a `financial.approve` holder confirms it; confirmation closes it; no function reopens or updates a closed Settlement (BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-AUTH-010).
- Obligations: every issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Lease, whoever of its Signers is named on it, with its open lines, and the separate Late-payment Charges of those Invoices (BR-SETTLE-002, BR-SETTLE-003, DL-069; owner answer 5). Nothing new is invented for the Settlement.
- Strict order (owner answer 3): Credit before Deposits, pet damage before any Pet Deposit Refund, and where DL-032 applies the Security Deposit pays open debt before any Security Deposit Refund; no Refund while this Lease still has open debt that a Deposit could pay. Each Customer's Credit pays only that Customer's obligations (BR-CREDIT-008); the Deposits may pay any open debt of the Lease (owner answer 5).
- DL-032 order in the draft and at confirmation: (1) obligations final, (2) Customer Credit, (3) Pet Deposit to valid pet damage, (4) Security Deposit applications, only where DL-032 applies, (5) remaining Pet Deposit by the Customer's choice or by staff with a reason when the Customer will not pay, (6) remaining balance recorded, (7) Refunds of what remains in each Deposit. No FIFO and no inferred Deposit-type order; every step is chosen explicitly by staff (BR-SETTLE-006).
- Kept Security Deposit: when the termination record gives a Security forfeiture ground (`app.lease_deposit_forfeiture_source`, slice 6: BR-LEASE-021, BR-LEASE-005, BR-DEP-022, BR-LEASE-025, BR-LEASE-028), the Security Deposit is never offered for any application (BR-DEP-026); the Settlement cannot close while it is still held and its forfeiture draft is not confirmed. Unpaid charges stay as debt. Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answers to Q-2026-10-05-23 and Q-2026-10-05-24, sub-slice 8b): a Deposit whose every confirmed forfeiture was reversed is held again and follows DL-032; a ground with no confirmed forfeiture blocks only while the Deposit holds money (path `NOTHING_HELD` otherwise); an active forfeiture keeps the Deposit.
- Pet Deposit: pet noncompliance (BR-DEP-022) keeps both Deposits, so no Pet step. In every other case (normal end, voluntary early, unpaid Initial Rent or Security Deposit, non-pet violation, 30-day default) the Pet Deposit first pays valid pet damage, then may pay any remaining balance by the Customer's choice (recorded as text: who, the date, how, with the staff reason) or, outside the BR-DEP-027 terminations, by staff with a reason when the Customer will not pay (BR-SETTLE-005, BR-SETTLE-017, DL-013, BR-DEP-027; owner answer 4; the case without the Customer's choice is Q-2026-10-05-21 point 3), and the rest is refunded. Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answers to Q-2026-10-05-24 and Q-2026-10-05-25): a Pet Deposit whose pet-noncompliance forfeiture was reversed follows the DL-032 Pet steps, with the staff step allowed.
- Valid pet damage: Invoice lines of a Damage charge whose inspection finding is `pet_related` on the current inspection (migrations 60 and 65; plan Q12). Extraordinary cleaning caused by a pet counts when staff record it as a pet-related finding and bill it as a Damage charge.
- Cleaning charge check: a blocking issue only when the Customer moved in, a physical move-out is recorded, the Unit's rate in force at the move-out is above zero (or no rate is set), and no linked cleaning charge (`app.unit_cleaning_charges`) sits on an issued, non-VOID Invoice. Never moved in, or rate zero at move-out: no issue (slice 4, Q13, Q14). Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-05-25): the Unit's current rate decides, the same rate the slice 4 command charges, not the rate in force at the move-out.
- Remaining balance: stays open on the Customer Ledger (BR-SETTLE-007, BR-SETTLE-016). The billing functions issue the Lease's Rent, Utility, Damage, one-time, cleaning and final Utility Invoices to the Primary Tenant (migrations 30, 35, 65, 72, 81), so that debt is on the Primary Tenant's ledger. Only an ordinary Invoice draft with the Lease may name another signed Signer (migration 29); debt left on such an Invoice stays on that Signer's ledger (owner answer 5; BR-SETTLE-016 wording in Q-2026-10-05-21 point 1). No write-off.
- Refunds of remaining Deposits go to the Primary Tenant, or to another Signer with the written agreement of all Signers (BR-REFUND-015, slice 3a-2), with a Refund method and a reference note (BR-REFUND-014), in USD cents.

**Sub-slices** (each: one full backend unit run and one full PostgreSQL run, independent and security review, the standing round-3 rule, one checkpoint commit, one ops/NOW.md line):

- Split (2026-10-05, during 8a): 8a is built as 8a-1 (Settlement record, Deposit application record and every balance reader except the Customer Ledger) and 8a-2 (the Customer Ledger projection of Deposit applications in the database, the Python reader and the frontend client, and the Deposit's own history: the Deposit ledger read in `deposit_api.py` gains an application event so a lower held amount always has its event, BR-SETTLE-009), each with its own runs, reviews and commit. Until 8a-2 no application can exist outside tests, because no role can write one.
- **8a — Settlement record and Deposit application record** (BR-SETTLE-001, 009, 010, BR-DEP-026, BR-DEP-027; DB-SETTLE-001, DB-DEP-005). `app.final_settlements` (one per TERMINATED Lease, opened by `app.start_final_settlement` under `final_settlements.prepare`, no financial effect). `app.deposit_applications` with the table and column names that the existing `app.deposit_has_active_application` (migration 28) already expects; stages `PET_RELATED_FIRST`, `SECURITY_EXPLICIT`, `PET_REMAINDER_EXPLICIT`; target an Invoice line or a Late-payment Charge of the Lease, whoever is named on the Invoice (owner answers 1 and 5); no role may insert, only an internal function used by 8e. Deferred checks: stage matches the Deposit type, Deposit and target belong to the Settlement Lease, amount within the held Deposit and the open obligation, a Security Deposit with a forfeiture ground is refused, the first Pet stage only on pet-related Damage lines. The application counts as paid everywhere: `app.invoice_line_application_value_at` (so outstanding, Invoice status, the Invoice resource and `app.adjust_invoice` count it), the readers that add Payment and Credit values separately (migrations 59 and 82), the Late-payment Charge summary, the Deposit held value (`app.deposit_snapshot`, `app.deposit_held_value_at`, `app.deposit_balance_events`) and a new Customer Ledger projection version (the Deposit owner's ledger shows the Deposit applied, naming the obligation and its Customer; the Invoice Customer's ledger shows the obligation paid by a Deposit application). New-state guards (not a tightening, as no application exists before): VOID, Adjustments and Late-payment Charge corrections refuse a target with an active Deposit application; the receipt-reversal trigger (migration 80 body) also refuses while an application is active. Deposit application reversals are not built (plan Q15). Tests use an owner helper to write applications.
- **8r — Deposit receipt reversal draft** (BR-AUTH-010; owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-22, 2026-10-05; built right after 8a-1 is committed and before 8b). A Lease Deposit receipt reversal becomes a draft: Staff with `deposits.correct` prepare it, a `financial.approve` holder confirms it, like the forfeiture reversal (6b); the one-call reversal answers 409 and the financial role loses EXECUTE on it (approved tightening, replaces the Q-2026-10-04-13 answer); a receipt reversal is refused while any draft for that Deposit is pending. A returned transfer still works: the approver confirms the receipt reversal, then the Payment is reversed as today. The Reservation Deposit receipt reversal (slice 3b) is checked in 8r and closed the same way if it can free money for a one-call allocation or Credit recognition; otherwise the 8r report says why. Own runs, independent and security review, standing round-3 rule, one commit.
- Notes from the 8a-1 review round 2 (2026-10-05), for the named sub-slices: 8a-2 or 11e decides whether Late-payment readers may see the `deposit_id` of a `DEPOSIT_APPLICATION` item in `app.late_payment_charge_application_summary` (granted to the runtime role, no Property check, UUIDs only) and adds `deposit_total` to the Late-payment Charge resource; 8e records the current physical move-out in the closure `source_snapshot`, because `move_out_date_snapshot` is fixed when the Settlement starts and may be stale or NULL; 8b and 8c show the owner what happens to a Security Deposit whose forfeiture was reversed (6b): `app.lease_deposit_kept_rule` reads only the termination record, so that Deposit stays kept and is never applied (the stricter reading; owner question before 8c); 8e or slice 10 adds `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals` to `app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change` when a writer of that table exists; slice 10 checks Deposit applications before the P4608 check in `app.correct_late_payment_charge`, so a Charge correction after a Deposit application never says "Active applications must be reversed explicitly" (today it is still a conflict).
- Notes from the 8r review round 1 (2026-10-05): 8c and 8e extend the receipt reversal pending-draft checks (the record function and `app.validate_deposit_receipt_reversal`, today `REFUND` and `DEPOSIT_FORFEITURE` only) to the Final Settlement drafts that use a Deposit, with a test, or the Settlement confirm's stale check covers receipt reversals; 11c shows a receipt reversal draft by its variant (its kind is `DEPOSIT_FORFEITURE`), shows a pending receipt reversal on the Deposit's other draft screens (a Refund or forfeiture draft can still be recorded and confirmed first; refusing them would be a tightening and needs the owner), tells the approver that the Payment's value becomes available again, and may add the draft id to the reversal's audit context; slice 12 hides the database URL from the services' `repr` (`field(repr=False)`, all services).
- Notes from the 8r review round 2 (2026-10-05): 11c adds the Deposit receipt reversal draft routes (record under `deposits.correct`, confirm under `financial.approve`, withdraw, reject) and updates docs/006 UI-073, which still describes a direct receipt reversal action; until then a returned transfer is processed only through the service. Slice 12 tightens the least-privilege tests to exact grantee sets (`aclexplode` on the functions and the table, column-level INSERT with `has_any_column_privilege`, and the other login roles `prm_human_admin`, `prm_restore`, `prm_backup`), and may add tests for an 8r confirm given a 6b reversal draft id and for a pending Lease forfeiture draft refusing a receipt reversal.
- Owner answers to Q-2026-10-05-23 (2026-10-05) for the named sub-slices: 8a-2 also refuses the confirmation of a Refund or forfeiture draft for a Deposit while a receipt reversal draft for it is pending (approved tightening, built in 8a-2 with tests); 8c and 8e extend both directions of the receipt reversal check to the Final Settlement drafts; a Security Deposit whose forfeiture was reversed is held again and follows DL-032 at settlement unless a new forfeiture draft is confirmed, which needs a relaxing change of the 8a-1 kept-Deposit check in 8b, 8c and 8e; Q-2026-10-05-24 (wording of BR-DEP-023 and BR-DEP-026) must be answered before 8b builds that path and before 8c. 8a-2 leaves the Deposit identifiers visible to Late-payment readers (`app.late_payment_charge_application_summary`, `app.deposit_has_active_application`) to 11e.
- **8b — Settlement preview** (BR-SETTLE-002, 003, 011, DL-032; plan slice 8 notes). One read function: obligations per Customer, available Credit per Customer (a Credit released from a moved allocation stays with its own Customer), both Deposits with held amount and path (kept with its ground, or DL-032), the DL-032 steps with what each may use, and the blocking issues: final Utility Invoice not issued, cleaning charge missing (rule above), Late-payment Decision not recorded (Q-20 answer 6), kept Deposit still held without a confirmed forfeiture, a pending forfeiture, reversal, Refund or Payment move draft on a Settlement source, an Invoice draft of the Lease not issued. The preview writes nothing. Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answers to Q-2026-10-05-25, sub-slice 8b as built): also `UTILITY_PERIOD_NOT_BILLED`, `ONE_TIME_CHARGE_NOT_INVOICED`, Late-payment Decisions on PARTIALLY_PAID Invoices, the Deposit path `NOTHING_HELD`, and a pending draft's identity shown only to `financial.approve` or the draft's prepare capability. Amended 2026-10-05 (owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-27): `ONE_TIME_CHARGE_NOT_INVOICED` flags only a charge still waiting to be billed (not one whose Invoice was VOIDed, not one a draft correction replaced); the preview's `information` key lists VOIDed charges not billed again, without blocking.
- **8c — Settlement draft** (BR-AUTH-010, BR-SETTLE-004 to 008, 017). Draft kind `FINAL_SETTLEMENT` under `final_settlements.prepare`: the ordered steps, amounts in cents, for the remaining Pet Deposit the Customer's choice text (who, the date, how) with the staff reason, or, outside the BR-DEP-027 terminations, the staff reason when the Customer will not pay, each Deposit Refund with method, reference note and recipient (and the Signers' agreement when needed), the expected closing balance and the source revision of every included Invoice, Charge, Credit and Deposit. Refused while a blocking issue exists. Strict order rules (owner answer 3) checked at record and at confirmation. One pending draft per Settlement; withdraw and reject use the slice 2 functions. No financial effect before confirmation. As built 2026-10-05 (migration `20261025_90`): the plan checker `app.final_settlement_plan_result` is internal so 8e calls it again; every DL-032 Deposit must end with nothing held; the source revision is the digest of `app.final_settlement_source_state`; the receipt reversal checks see the Settlement draft's `deposit_ids` in both directions; replaced one-time charges are listed under the preview's `information` (owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-28). Choices are listed in docs/010.
- Notes from the 8c review round 1 (2026-10-05), for 8e: the confirm takes the Settlement advisory lock (`final-settlement-draft:<settlement>`, as the recorder does), then the per-Deposit advisory lock and the Deposit row lock for every Deposit of the Lease in id order, before it writes its decision (the decision trigger's receipt reversal wait takes no lock itself); it calls `app.final_settlement_plan_result` again, compares its `source_revision` with the draft's and also compares the recomputed result (closing balances, `deposit_ids`) with the stored one, because the digest does not cover pet-damage classification, Signer status or Refund method state; a Credit step on an Invoice with Rent or Service lines is applied with the basis `RENT_THEN_RECURRING_SERVICES_PROPOSAL` (no designation) or `CUSTOMER_DESIGNATED` with the step's text as the designation reference. Also from the 8c review round 2: before calling the plan check, 8e serializes with Late-payment Decision finalization (for example locks the Lease's UNPAID and PARTIALLY_PAID Invoices), or `app.late_payment_review_snapshot` is made STABLE, because that earlier VOLATILE function reads its own snapshot inside the preview; a Credit step on an Invoice without Rent or Service lines needs an internal writer for the existing `OTHER_APPLICABLE_OBLIGATION` basis with `OTHER_CONFIRMED` details (no writer produces it today), or the confirm refuses drafts that 8c accepts; step ids are stored as sent, so 8e casts them to uuid before any comparison. Slice 12: the 16,000-byte draft payload allows about 90 short steps (owner to know); a GIN index on `payload->'deposit_ids'` for `FINAL_SETTLEMENT` drafts and splitting the receipt-guard `OR` into two `EXISTS` if drafts grow; a two-connection test of the recorder lock order; a test that an inactive Refund method is refused in a Settlement draft (today only an unknown method is tested); a charge both VOIDed and replaced is listed twice under the preview's `information` (8c review round 3 notes).
- **8d — Settlement Deposit Refund source** (BR-DEP-025, BR-REFUND-007, 014, 015, BR-SETTLE-008). A relaxing change of the Refund source checks so that a Refund of a Lease Security or Pet Deposit linked to a Final Settlement is accepted, through an internal function only (no grant); the ledger already books Deposit Refunds on the Deposit owner's ledger (slice 3a-2). As built 2026-10-05 (migration `20261026_91`): the owner answer to choice (4a) first (the plan check refuses a remaining Pet Deposit step while a `DL_032` Security Deposit still holds money); the relaxed Refund source-context check, `app.final_settlement_refund_details` (the Settlement link, method, note, agreement, draft; one Refund per Deposit per Settlement; not a new `refunds` column, docs/010), the internal writer `app.post_final_settlement_deposit_refund` and the source check `app.assert_final_settlement_refund_source` (docs/004 DB-REFUND-001); the one-call Refund corrections refuse a Settlement Refund; a Deposit application after a Settlement Refund is refused. Choices are listed in docs/010.
- Owner rule (answer 3 to Q-2026-10-05-31, 2026-10-05): when a design differs from docs/004 or this plan, ask the owner first.
- Notes from sub-slice 8d, for 8e: call `app.post_final_settlement_deposit_refund` after every Credit step and Deposit application and before the closure; the source check accepts the draft while it is pending or once it is confirmed by the same approver, but a deferred check refuses the commit unless the same approver's CONFIRMED decision exists, so 8e writes its decision in the same transaction, before the Refund writer or with nothing between them that sets constraints IMMEDIATE (the writer sets its own deferred checks back to DEFERRED, so an earlier `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`, for example from the Credit steps, does not stop it, but a later one fires the confirmation check before the decision exists and refuses a valid draft with 42501); 8e refunds the Deposits in id order; the source check refuses a Refund while a receipt reversal or forfeiture draft for the Deposit waits (owner's 8r rule, Q-2026-10-05-31); lock order: 8e takes the Settlement advisory lock first and the Settlement row before the Deposit rows (the writers lock the Settlement row, then the Deposit's draft lock and row), or it may deadlock with them; 8e serializes with every writer that can add or reopen Lease debt (new charges, Payment or allocation reversals, Credit application reversals), for example by locking every Lease Invoice target, because the Refund's open-debt check reads that debt without locks; 8e keeps READ COMMITTED and re-runs the plan check (with the (4a) rule) before any Deposit application or Refund, because the Deposit application trigger does not check (4a); pass a Refund id derived from the draft and the Deposit so an exact repeat replays; map 55000 (closed Settlement, inactive method, a waiting receipt reversal or forfeiture draft, open debt) to a conflict, 42501 to an authorization error and 23514 to a stale or invalid draft. For slice 10: a returned Settlement Refund (verified return) has no correction path yet; the one-call Refund correction refuses it. For 11e: the Deposit history read (`deposit_api.py`) shows a Settlement Refund with no governing source; label it `FINAL_SETTLEMENT` with the Settlement id, as the Deposit applications are; the frontend's financial action context (`financialActionContext.ts`) refuses a Deposit Refund with neither a Reservation event nor a Pet Refund review, so it must accept a Settlement Refund (from `final_settlement_refund_details`) before any screen shows one.
- **8e — Confirm and close** (BR-SETTLE-007, 008, 009, 012, 015, 016; DB-SETTLE-002, 004, 006). `financial.approve` only. In one transaction: lock, re-run the preview and the stale check, apply the Credit steps through the existing Credit application function (its `final_settlement_id` check relaxed to accept the Settlement; amended by owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-32: through a new internal writer with the same Credit rules, sub-slice 8e-2), the Deposit applications (8a), the Refunds (8d), write the closure snapshots and the closing balance, close once, record the decision and an audit event; an exact repeat replays. Approved tightening (owner answer 7): after closure, VOID, Adjustments (including the hand-back and unpaid-Initial resolutions), Late-payment Decisions and Charge corrections on the Settlement's obligations, and new charges on the Lease, are refused until the slice 10 correction drafts exist; Payments, allocations, Credit applications and their ordinary reversals stay open.
- **8e pre-code review (2026-10-06, owner request; read only, no code).**
  - Every 8e note in this plan and how 8e meets it (8e-1, 8e-2 and 8e-3 are the split below):
    1. (8a-1 round 2) The current physical move-out goes into the closure `source_snapshot`: 8e-3 reads the current (not superseded) physical move-out record at the confirm and stores its id and date there; more than one current record refuses the confirm as a conflict, as `app.start_final_settlement` does.
    2. (8a-1 round 2) `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals` joins `app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change` once a writer exists: the path search below finds no writer, so that addition stays with slice 10; 8e-1 already refuses an Adjustment reversal on an obligation of a closed Settlement at the table, so a later writer inherits that refusal.
    3. (8a-1 round 3) The closure guard checks that `closed_by` is an active approver and bounds `closed_at` to the clock; an inactive approver is an authorization error: 8e-3 extends `app.reject_final_settlement_change` (a human actor active on the Property, `closed_at` not before `started_at` and not later than the clock plus 5 seconds; it refuses only closures no writer could make before), and the service maps the inactive-approver refusal to an authorization error.
    4. (8r round 1; owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-05-23) The receipt reversal checks see Final Settlement drafts in both directions: built in 8c; 8e-3 records its decision through `app.confirm_financial_draft_record`, so the decision trigger refuses while a receipt reversal draft of a Deposit in the draft's `deposit_ids` waits; 8e-3 adds a test at the confirm.
    5. (Q-2026-10-05-23 answer 3, Q-2026-10-05-24) The three cases of a Deposit with a forfeiture ground: the plan check that 8e-3 runs again needs a ready preview, so a ground with no confirmed forfeiture (`FORFEITURE_REQUIRED`) and a pending new forfeiture draft block the confirm; a `KEPT` Deposit is never in a plan (only `DL_032` Deposits that hold money are); a reversed forfeiture follows DL-032. 8e-3 tests each case at the confirm.
    6. (8b round 1) Every preview blocking issue, pending drafts included, is checked again under the Settlement and Deposit locks: 8e-3 calls `app.final_settlement_plan_result` (which needs a ready preview) after taking the locks of note 7 and before any write.
    7. (8c round 1; 8d) Lock order: 8e-3 takes the Settlement advisory lock (`final-settlement-draft:<settlement>`), then locks every Invoice and Late-payment Charge of the Lease in id order (the targets the allocation, Credit, Deposit and Late-payment Decision writers lock first), then the Settlement row `FOR UPDATE`, then for each Deposit of the Lease in id order the per-Deposit advisory lock and the Deposit row, and only then checks and writes. This keeps the writers' order (Settlement row, then the Deposit's draft lock and row, then the target) for everything taken after the targets.
    8. (8c round 1) Compare the recomputed source revision and result (closing balances, `deposit_ids`) with the stored ones: 8e-3 refuses the draft as stale, and leaves it pending, when any of them differs.
    9. (8c rounds 1 and 2) Credit steps: on an Invoice with Rent or Service lines the basis `RENT_THEN_RECURRING_SERVICES_PROPOSAL`, or `CUSTOMER_DESIGNATED` with the step's text; an internal writer for `OTHER_APPLICABLE_OBLIGATION` with `OTHER_CONFIRMED` details for an Invoice without Rent or Service lines: 8e-2, after the owner answer to Q-2026-10-06-32 (the existing Credit application function cannot record the Settlement link, see that question).
    10. (8c round 2) Serialize with Late-payment Decision finalization before the plan check: `app.finalize_late_payment_decision` locks its Invoice `FOR UPDATE`, and 8e-3 locks every Lease Invoice before the plan check (note 7); 8e-1's refusal also waits for a running confirm (note 14).
    11. (8c round 2) Step ids are stored as sent: 8e-3 reads every id through `app.final_settlement_plan_id` (canonical uuid text) and casts it to uuid before any use or comparison.
    12. (8d) The Refund writer runs after every Credit step and Deposit application and before the closure; the decision is written first, with no `SET CONSTRAINTS ... IMMEDIATE` between the decision and the writer; Refunds in Deposit id order; a Refund id derived from the draft and the Deposit: 8e-3 order is locks, plan check, decision, Credit steps, Deposit applications (`app.post_deposit_application`, stage by step kind, order 1 to n), Refunds (`app.post_final_settlement_deposit_refund`, Deposit id order, id derived from the draft id and the Deposit id), closure snapshots, closure, audit event.
    13. (8d) The Refund source check refuses while a receipt reversal or forfeiture draft for the Deposit waits: inherited through the writer; 8e-3 maps it (55000) to a conflict.
    14. (8d) Serialize with every writer that can add or reopen Lease debt: allocation and Credit application reversals lock their Invoice or Charge target, which 8e-3 locks before the plan check (note 7); new charges, VOID, Adjustments, Late-payment Decisions and Charge corrections meet 8e-1's refusal, which locks the Lease's Settlement row `FOR SHARE`, so it waits for a running confirm and then sees the closure, and a confirm that starts after it sees its change and refuses the draft as stale.
    15. (8d) Keep READ COMMITTED and run the plan check, with the (4a) rule, before any Deposit application or Refund: 8e-3 refuses any other isolation level and runs the plan check once, under every lock, before its first write.
    16. (8d; 8a-1 round 3) Error mapping: 55000 to a conflict, 42501 and an inactive approver to an authorization error, 23514 to a stale or invalid draft; 8e-3 service with unit tests.
    17. (8d round 3) A later `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` before the decision fails safe, and the writer stays inside the confirm transaction: met by note 12.
    18. (8e entry) Closure snapshots (DB-SETTLE-002, 004, 006), closing balance, close once, decision, audit event, exact repeat replays: 8e-3; the key of DB-SETTLE-006 is point 4 of Q-2026-10-06-32.
    19. (8b round 3) The preview's `information` for the approver: a screen item (11e); 8e changes nothing there. The owner item on replaced charges was answered by Q-2026-10-05-28 and built in 8c.
  - Path search (2026-10-06, read only): one-call paths that can change a Lease's obligations after closure (owner answer 7 to Q-2026-10-05-20 refuses them until the slice 10 correction drafts exist):
    1. VOID: `POST /invoices/{invoice_id}/void`, `invoice.py`, `app.void_invoice` (financial role, `invoices.void`), writes `app.invoice_void_events`.
    2. Adjustments: `POST /invoices/{invoice_id}/adjustments`, `invoice.py`, `app.adjust_invoice` (financial role, `invoices.adjust`), writes `app.invoice_adjustments`; a credit Adjustment on a paid line also releases Credit (`app.repost_line_value_after_credit_excess`, internal).
    3. Hand-back resolution: `app.resolve_hand_back_rent_invoice` (financial role, service in `invoice.py`, route in 11a), through VOID or credit Adjustments.
    4. Unpaid-Initial resolutions: `POST /properties/{property_id}/lease-terminations/{termination_event_id}/unpaid-initial-never-moved-in` and `.../unpaid-initial-moved-in-credit`, `unpaid_initial_termination.py`, `app.resolve_unpaid_initial_never_moved_in` and `app.credit_initial_rent_after_termination` (financial role), through VOID or credit Adjustments.
    5. Late-payment Decisions: `POST /invoices/{invoice_id}/late-payment-decision`, `late_payment.py`, `app.finalize_late_payment_decision` (financial role, `late_payment.final_decision`), writes `app.late_payment_decisions` and `app.late_payment_charges`.
    6. Late-payment Charge corrections: `POST /late-payment-charges/{charge_id}/corrections`, `late_payment.py`, `app.correct_late_payment_charge` (financial role, `late_payment.charge_correction`), writes `app.late_payment_charge_corrections`.
    7. Adjustment reversals: `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals` has no writer today.
    8. New charges on the Lease, each writing `app.authorized_nonrecurring_obligations` with the Lease or `app.invoices` with the Lease: one-time and Damage charges (`POST /properties/{property_id}/one-time-charges`, `app.record_one_time_charge`, `invoices.create`, Damage with `damage_charges.bill`); the cleaning charge (`app.record_unit_cleaning_charge`, service only, route in 11a); Invoice drafts with the Lease, any Signer named (`POST /properties/{property_id}/invoices`, `app.create_invoice_draft`), their correction and issue (`PATCH /invoices/{invoice_id}`, `POST /invoices/{invoice_id}/issue`); the final Utility Invoice and the Utility catch-up Invoice (`POST /properties/{property_id}/leases/{lease_id}/final-utility-invoice` and `.../utility-catch-up-invoice`, `final_utility_invoice.py`, `app.utility_final_invoice_create`, `app.utility_catch_up_invoice_create`); Rent Invoices (`app.generate_rent_service_invoice`, financial role, `billing.py`, and the scheduled Rent and Utility run items `app.record_scheduled_rent_item` and `app.record_scheduled_utility_item`, `POST /properties/{property_id}/billing-runs/rent` and `.../utilities`, `billing_run.py`; a terminated Lease is still billable for a period inside its coverage and for the early final month). A run item that fails is recorded as an `ERROR` item with its SQLSTATE and the run goes on.
    9. Not refused (owner answer 7 or outside it): Payments, Payment allocations and their reversals, Payment reversals, Credit recognition, Credit applications and their reversals (but see Q-2026-10-06-32 point 3 for a Credit application the Settlement itself makes), Credit and Payment Refund drafts, Payment move drafts (a draft confirmed by `financial.approve`; it moves allocations, which stay open), move-out inspection findings (not a charge until billed), the Initial Rent Due Date override (`app.set_initial_rent_due_date`, SIGNED Leases only). A credit Adjustment that would release Credit is refused with the Adjustments.
    No service writes these tables directly; the services write only audit and recovery-code rows.
  - Split (2026-10-06; a split needs no owner answer): 8e is three sub-slices, each with its own runs, reviews, standing round-3 rule and commit. The order differs from the owner's example (confirm first) for two reasons: 8e-1 does not depend on the open question Q-2026-10-06-32, and with 8e-1 first no closed Settlement can ever exist without its refusals (only 8e-3 can close one), which also gives 8e-3 the serialization of note 14.
    - **8e-1 — Refusals after closure** (owner answer 7 to Q-2026-10-05-20; owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-33; BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013). One trigger function, BEFORE INSERT on `app.invoice_void_events`, `app.invoice_adjustments`, `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals`, `app.late_payment_decisions`, `app.late_payment_charges`, `app.late_payment_charge_corrections`, `app.invoices` (a new Invoice with the Lease) and `app.authorized_nonrecurring_obligations` (a new charge with the Lease), and BEFORE UPDATE OF `lease_id`, `status` on `app.invoices` (a draft corrected onto a Lease charge, or a DRAFT with the Lease issued; review round 1, blocker B1): it finds the Lease's Settlement, locks its row `FOR SHARE` and refuses with one dedicated SQLSTATE when the Settlement is closed; each affected service maps that code to a conflict. Owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-33: a scheduled Rent or Utility run records a closed Lease as `SKIPPED` with `FINAL_SETTLEMENT_CLOSED` and can succeed. Owner answer 3: a VOID of a draft with the closed Lease stays refused. No closed Settlement exists before 8e-3, so nothing changes today. Tests close a Settlement through an owner test helper (the 8a-1 closure guard allows exactly one closure) and check through the real commands: VOID, credit and debit Adjustments, a one-time charge and an Invoice draft with the Lease, a draft corrected onto a Lease charge and then issued, the VOID of that draft, Late-payment Decisions and Charge corrections, the unpaid-Initial resolution, the final Utility Invoice, a scheduled Rent run with a closed and an open Lease (and the VOID of the open Lease's Invoice), a scheduled Utility run with a closed and a started-but-open Settlement (and the VOID of the billed Invoice; review round 2); every guarded table at its own trigger; the open paths (Payment allocation and its reversal, Payment reversal, Credit application and its reversal); VOID, Adjustment and draft correction while the Settlement is open; a two-connection wait. The other writers (Rent generation outside a run, catch-up Utility, hand-back resolution, moved-in credit, cleaning and Damage charges, a draft naming another Signer) only reach the guarded tables, so the table-level tests and the service mappings prove them; they are not tested one by one.
    - **8e-2 — Settlement Credit application writer** (BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-SETTLE-004, BR-SETTLE-009; owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-32). An internal writer (no grant), not `app.post_credit_application`: the same header and detail rows the existing function writes (the Rent-first proposal with its trace, the Customer's designation, a direct Late-payment Charge) plus `OTHER_APPLICABLE_OBLIGATION` with `OTHER_CONFIRMED` details on an Invoice without Rent or Service lines; `final_settlement_id` set (its NULL-only CHECK becomes a foreign key); `authorization_reference` `financial.approve` on such rows only; every existing Credit trigger unchanged; a deferred check that it commits only with its draft confirmed by the same approver (as 8d); the one-call Credit application reversal refuses a Credit application with a `final_settlement_id` (answer 3; no such row exists before).
      - As built (2026-10-06, migration `20261028_93`): `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application(property, approver, settlement, draft, step)` reads the step at that 1-based position of the draft, derives the application id from the draft and the step (`app.final_settlement_credit_application_id`), takes the locks of `app.post_credit_application` after the Settlement row, writes the header and one detail (the detail's `calculation_trace` names the draft and the step) and refreshes the Invoice status; the source check (`a0_customer_credit_applications_final_settlement`) runs before the existing Credit checks; the deferred check `customer_credit_applications_final_settlement_confirmed`; the reversal refusal `a0_customer_credit_application_reversals_final_settlement` (`P5S01`, mapped to a conflict in `credit.py`); no Settlement Credit application once the Settlement has a Deposit application or a Refund (DL-032 step 2). Choices are listed in docs/010.
      - Notes for 8e-3 from building 8e-2 (with the review round 1 findings): call the writer once per Customer Credit step, in the draft's order, after the decision and before the first Deposit application; map its refusals: 23514 (a step that no longer matches, a Credit or target that no longer allows it, the Rent-first refusal), 23505 (a step already written) and the proposal's P5101, P5102 and P5104 to a stale draft, 55000 to a conflict, 42501 to an authorization error, P0002 and 22023 to a stale or invalid draft; an inactive approver reaches the writer through `app.assert_active_financial_actor` as 55000, so 8e-3 checks the approver first (plan note 16 makes it an authorization error); the writer sets only its own four deferred checks DEFERRED and never sets anything IMMEDIATE, so an 8e-3 that wants the deferred checks inside its function sets `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` only after its decision and after every Settlement writer, and maps errors raised at commit as well. Lock order (amends note 7; security review round 1): the writer, the existing Credit check and the 8a Deposit application check all lock their target Invoice (and a Charge's source Invoice) `FOR UPDATE` and the Credit writer also its lines, after the Settlement row; so before the Settlement row 8e-3 locks, in id order, the source Payment (`app.serialize_payment_state`, then the row) and the Credit of every Customer Credit step (the order of the one-call Credit and allocation writers: Payment before Invoice), then every Late-payment Charge and Invoice of the Lease (Charges before their source Invoices): `FOR UPDATE` for a Late-payment Charge that a Credit step or a Deposit application targets and for its source Invoice (`app.lock_payment_allocation_target` locks the Charge, then that Invoice, `FOR UPDATE`; review round 2) and for every Invoice that a Credit step or a Deposit application targets (with its lines, for a Credit step), and `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` for the others; no lock taken after the Settlement row is then stronger than one 8e-3 already holds, and the writers' re-locks are no-ops. One inversion remains and cannot be removed by 8e-3 (security review round 2): `app.adjust_invoice` locks its Invoice before the credit-excess reversal locks a Payment, the reverse of the Payment-before-Invoice order, so a credit Adjustment that reverses an ordinary Credit application from the same Payment as a Settlement Credit step can meet 8e-3 in a detected deadlock (40P01, mapped to a conflict; the same inversion already exists between `app.post_credit_application` and `app.adjust_invoice`); 8e-3 may add a two-connection test. 8e-3's closure checks that the Settlement has exactly one Credit application for each Customer Credit step of the confirmed draft (the id derived from the draft and the step), because the database refuses a missing step only until the first Deposit application (security note); 8e-3's own audit event names the Settlement's Credit applications and the draft, because the existing `credit.applied` event records `financial.approve` and the approver but not the Settlement (both reviewers; the existing Credit triggers stay unchanged).
      - Notes for the 8e-3 pre-code review from the 8e-2 review round 3: place the Lease row lock (8e-1 round-2 note: `FOR UPDATE` before the plan check) at an explicit position in the amended order above and check it against Lease-first writers such as the unpaid-Initial resolution (migration 70 locks the Lease, then `app.adjust_invoice`, whose excess path can lock a Payment), keeping 40P01 mapped to a conflict (security); add a confirm test of a Customer Credit step with the Customer's designation on a Late-payment Charge (`CUSTOMER_DESIGNATED` on the header and the detail; the writer handles it, no 8e-2 test) (independent); optional: an owner-written row with a non-human approver for the other half of the 42501 check (independent).
      - For the owner's information (security review round 1, from owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-06-32 and the existing rules): while a Settlement Credit application stands, its Customer Credit cannot be reversed (a Credit reversal needs all its applications reversed) and its source Payment cannot be reversed either (a Payment reversal lists the application as needing reversal, which is refused); for example a Payment that bounces after its Credit paid Settlement debt waits for the post-settlement corrections (slice 10), as a Payment whose Deposit a Deposit application used already does (8a-1). Slice 10 opens the reversal only through a linked correction row checked in the same transaction, never a session setting.
      - Notes for 11e from building 8e-2: `frontend/src/financialActionContext.ts` refuses a Credit application whose `authorization_reference` is not `credits.apply`, so it must accept a Settlement Credit application (`final_settlement_id` set, `financial.approve`) before any screen shows one; the Credit application reversal action is shown as unavailable for such an application (the database answers `P5S01`, a 409) and points to the post-settlement corrections.
    - **8e-3 — Confirm and close** (the rest of the 8e entry and notes 1 to 18; DB-SETTLE-006 refers to a new `id` column of `settlement_invoice_items`, owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-06-32). After 8e-2. Also: the preview gains the blocking issue `RENT_PERIOD_NOT_BILLED` for a full month inside the Lease's coverage with no active Rent Invoice (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-06-33, an approved tightening that also refuses such Settlement drafts), and the confirm checks `INVOICE_DRAFT_NOT_ISSUED` again under its locks (owner answer 3).
      - As built (2026-10-06, migration `20261029_94`): `app.confirm_final_settlement_draft(property, approver, draft, authorization)` (financial role; `FinalSettlementService.confirm` checks `financial.approve`), `app.final_settlement_unbilled_rent_months` (owner answer to Q-2026-10-06-34: a month VOIDed by a BR-RENT-030 hand-back resolution does not block), the snapshot tables `app.settlement_invoice_items` (with `id`), `app.settlement_late_payment_charge_items`, `app.settlement_invoice_line_items` with insert and deferred reconciliation checks, the closure guard's approver and clock checks; lock order: the Settlement draft lock, the Lease row, the Credit steps' Payments then Credits, the Lease's Charges then Invoices in id order, the Settlement row, each Deposit's draft lock and row; a stale draft is `P5S02`. Choices are listed in docs/010.
      - Notes from the 8e-3 reviews (2026-10-06). For 11e: the confirm route answers a stale draft (`P5S02`, `FinalSettlementStaleDraftError`) with a 409 that tells the preparer to prepare the draft again; the confirm screen shows the draft's preparer and the approver separately (the closure snapshot keeps `prepared_by`); the Customer's step 5 choice text stays in the draft (the Deposit application records the staff reason), so the Settlement screen shows it from the draft named in `source_snapshot`. For slice 12 (owner items first): (owner) the confirm is one statement under the shared 3-second statement timeout of the Deposit services, and each Invoice or Charge snapshot row re-runs the whole closure reconciliation at commit, so measure with a long Lease and then choose one check per Settlement or a confirm timeout; (owner) a deferred check tying every closure and every Deposit application to a CONFIRMED decision would refuse the owner-written closures of the 8a-1 and 8e-1 tests (their helpers would need the confirm); tests only: the snapshot-insert test asserts the insert check's own message, failing cases for the line-snapshot insert check, the line CHECK and the Charge-snapshot amount check, a Charge snapshot's `included_by` under a second approver, an optional two-connection test of the Lease row position (new charge waiting on the Lease key lock; the Lease forfeiture confirm pair ends in 40P01); hardening: a distinct SQLSTATE for a server whose default isolation is not READ COMMITTED; the confirm's internal invariant refusals (23514) could get their own SQLSTATE instead of "stale".
      - Notes from the review of the Q-2026-10-06-34 follow-up tests (2026-10-06), for slice 12 (tests only): assert that next month's Invoice is VOID after the hand-back resolution; assert the hand-back coverage of each Invoice before the plain VOID; pin the `lease_id` and `action='VOID'` filters of the hand-back exclusion with a second Lease whose hand-back resolution VOIDs the same month.
  - Notes from the 8e-1 review round 1 (2026-10-06; added with the owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-33): 8e-3 locks every Late-payment Charge before its source Invoice and the Lease's Invoices `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (so the foreign-key and overlap checks of a new-Invoice writer are not blocked), and maps 40P01 and 55P03 to a conflict, because `app.correct_late_payment_charge` locks the Charge before its Invoice and `invoice.py`, `late_payment.py` and `billing.py` map 40P01 (and the last two 55P03) to "unavailable" today; slice 10's correction writers pass the 8e-1 guard only through a linked correction row checked in the same transaction, never through a session setting (any role can call `set_config`); 11e shows a stable reason for `P5S01` (the final Utility service gives no reason and says "sources changed or are in use"; the Late-payment 409 returns a review that still says `eligible: true`) and may label `FINAL_SETTLEMENT_CLOSED` on the run diagnostics screen; for the owner's information, one-time charges with no Lease for the former tenant stay open after closure (outside answer 7).
  - Notes from the 8e-1 review round 2 (2026-10-06): the scheduled Rent run item takes the Settlement row `FOR SHARE` before `app.generate_rent_service_invoice` locks an overlapping active Rent Invoice of the Lease (Settlement, then Invoice), the reverse of the guard's and the 8e-3 confirm's order (Invoices, then Settlement); PostgreSQL detects the deadlock (40P01) inside the item's exception block (at the generator's Invoice lock, or at a new Invoice's key-share lock on the Lease row against a confirm's Lease `FOR UPDATE`): the item is recorded as `ERROR` (`RENT_PERSISTENCE_40P01` or its Utility equivalent) and the next attempt skips the Lease; the Rent item's `FOR SHARE` itself, outside that block, can only wait, and a lock timeout there (55P03) ends the call as a conflict and the attempt resumes on the next call (security review round 3), so 8e-3 maps 40P01 to a conflict (already planned) and keeps every lock it takes after the Settlement row free of Rent Invoice locks; 8e-3 also locks the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` before its plan check, so a writer whose guard ran before `app.start_final_settlement` (no Settlement then, so no lock) cannot commit a charge after the closure; open item for 11e or slice 12 (pre-existing): a one-time-charge draft has no Due Date until it is issued, so it can never be VOIDed, and with owner answer 3 the closure then waits until such a draft is issued (and VOIDed if unwanted) or corrected onto a charge with no Lease; the closure screens should explain that way out; optional hardening: the run item history trigger could check that a `FINAL_SETTLEMENT_CLOSED` item's Lease really has a closed Settlement (as for `SOURCE_NO_LONGER_ELIGIBLE`; `NOT_MOVED_IN` has no such check either).
- **8f — Credit Refund to the Invoice Customer who is not the payer** (BR-CREDIT-014; owner answer to Q-2026-10-05-19). A Refund draft variant for a Credit released by a credit Adjustment, paid to the Credit's Customer, prepared under `refunds.create` and confirmed by `financial.approve`; the confirmation refusal SQLSTATE 23514 becomes a conflict.
  - Pre-code design (2026-10-06, read only; within the plan and docs/004, so no owner question):
    - Which Credits: a Customer Credit that a credit Adjustment released (`app.invoice_credit_excess_releases.customer_credit_id`) and whose Customer is not the payer of its source Payment: a third-party Payment (since migration 67) or a wrong-Unit move to another Customer (slice 7). These are the only Credits whose Customer differs from the payer. A Credit whose Customer is the payer keeps the ordinary `USD` Credit Refund draft, which already pays that Customer.
    - Record: new draft variant `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` of kind `REFUND` under `refunds.create`, through a new function `app.record_credit_customer_refund_draft` (financial role), with a Refund method, a reference note and the Credit's Customer as `recipient_customer_id`. The payload keeps `source_kind` `CREDIT` and `source_id`, so the Settlement preview's pending-draft check and the draft reads see it unchanged. A Credit the variant does not cover is refused with a new SQLSTATE `P5206`, reported as a conflict. Withdraw and reject use the slice 2 functions.
    - Confirm: the existing `app.confirm_refund_draft` gains the variant (same `financial.approve` check, method check, key-reuse check and replay), and calls a new internal writer `app.post_credit_customer_refund` (no grant): Payment, then Credit lock (as every Credit Refund), the Credit check again, the amount within the available Credit (otherwise stale, `P5202`, pending, as the `USD` variant), the Refund row paid to the Credit's Customer with `processed_by` the approver and `authorization_reference` `refunds.create` (as every confirmed Refund draft, docs/010 slice 3a-1), and the replay row; the method and note go to `app.refund_draft_details` as today.
    - Refund source trigger (relaxing): the recipient of a Credit Refund must still be the Credit's Customer; the payer rule is lifted only for a Credit the variant covers, with a matching `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` draft pending or confirmed by the same approver; a deferred check lets such a Refund commit only with that draft CONFIRMED under `financial.approve` by the Refund's approver and its `refund_draft_details` row (as 8d and 8e-2).
    - Error mapping: at confirmation every 23514 is a conflict (the plan), also for the `USD` and `PET` variants, because the transaction rolled back and the draft stays pending (no lost outcome to replay); `P5206` is a conflict; recording keeps today's mapping.
    - Unchanged: the Customer Ledger books a Credit Refund on its recipient in the `CREDIT` bucket, so the Credit's Customer's ledger shows it; Refund corrections (verified returns and their reversals) work as for any Credit Refund; the one-call Credit reversal refusal of slice 7 stays. No HTTP route or screen (11c).
    - Affected tests (read-only search): the head-revision assertions (26 in 14 modules); two concurrency tests whose loser now gets a conflict instead of "unavailable" (`test_refund_service_migration.py::test_refund_service_concurrent_commands_replay_once_or_fail_closed`, `test_credit_refund_api_migration.py::test_credit_refund_http_real_competing_refunds_cannot_double_consume_credit`), narrowed one expectation each with a docs/010 note; no other test observes a 23514 at a Refund confirmation (the slice 7 refusal test already accepts a conflict, and `test_refund.py` maps 23514 at recording, which is unchanged).
  - Notes from the 8f review round 1 (2026-10-06). For 11c: the Refund source read (`app.usd_refund_source`, `GET /refund-sources/CREDIT/{id}`) names the payer as recipient and `eligible: true` for a Credit only a `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` draft can pay, and `POST /refund-drafts` records an ordinary draft for it that is refused at confirmation (409; it also shows as `PENDING_DRAFT` in a Final Settlement preview until withdrawn): the 11c source read names the Credit's Customer and the variant, and the draft route records that variant (or refuses the ordinary one for such a Credit; refusing it is a tightening and needs the owner); the draft route and an HTTP confirmation test of the variant come with it; the Refund screens must not show the payer's source Payment memo or channel reference on a Refund or voucher for the Credit's Customer (`app.refund_resource` embeds the source Payment); the stale Refund draft refusals (`P5202`) become conflicts with the other draft routes (owner answer, plan 11c). For 11c and 11e: `frontend/src/financialActionContext.ts` requires a Credit's Customer to be its Payment's payer (`credit()`) and a Payment or Credit Refund's recipient to be the payer (`refundSummary()`), so it must accept a Credit or Credit Refund of a Customer other than the payer when a credit Adjustment released the Credit, before any screen shows one (verified returns and their reversals work in the backend). For slice 12 (tests and hardening only): a test that a pending `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` draft on a Lease Customer's Credit shows as `PENDING_DRAFT` (variant `CREDIT_CUSTOMER`) in the Final Settlement preview; the `refund.created` audit event of a Credit Refund paid to a Customer other than the payer takes `lease_id` from the source Payment (the payer's Lease after a wrong-Unit move), and could take the Lease of the releasing Invoice; `app.credit_customer_refund_draft` scans the Property's REFUND drafts (measure with the other draft lookups); the different-key case of `test_refund_service_migration.py::test_refund_service_concurrent_commands_replay_once_or_fail_closed` can also end with `RefundInvalidError` (P5202) when the loser's confirmation starts after the winner committed (true before 8f); the derived Refund ids of 8d, 8e and 8f (md5 of the draft; 8f: `app.credit_customer_refund_id`) can meet an id or `REF-` number clash with a Reservation Refund draft's client-chosen `outcome_id` or `refund_number` (denial of service only; security notes of rounds 1 and 2; the hardening, for example server-generated Reservation Refund ids and numbers, should cover all three); at the Refund draft confirmation 8f maps 23505 to a conflict, tested only with a mock (no database test of a taken derived id yet). From the 8f review round 3 (tests and hardening only): direct tests of the draft-match clauses of `app.credit_customer_refund_draft` and the release clause of `app.credit_customer_refund_basis` (with a pending 1,000 draft, a direct insert with the derived id but another amount, another reason, or a random id, each 23514 with the source check's message); assert the refusal messages in `test_a_credit_customer_refund_is_written_only_through_its_confirmed_draft` too; the test's "another approver" is the draft's preparer (any other person meets the clause); the deferred check's refusal (42501) reaches the service as an authorization error (404 over HTTP), not a conflict, if a later change ever broke the confirmation's write order (unreachable today; a dedicated SQLSTATE in 11c or slice 12).
- **8g — One-call reversal refused for a Credit released to another Customer** (BR-CREDIT-014, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer to Q-2026-10-06-35, approved tightening). Migration `20261031_96`: BEFORE INSERT trigger `customer_credit_reversals_other_customer` on `app.customer_credit_reversals` refuses, for every writer, the reversal of a Credit for which `app.credit_customer_refund_basis` is not NULL (the Credits 8f pays), SQLSTATE `P4904` as slice 7, a conflict in `CustomerCreditService`; it fires after the slice 7 refusal and before the Credit reversal checks. Choices in docs/010.
  - Consequence (slice 12 item below): a third-party Payment whose credit Adjustment released such a Credit can no longer be reversed; after 8g no path reverses such a Credit, so the Payment stays blocked until a controlled path exists.
  - Mistaken credit Adjustment (owner request): no draft-based fix exists; one way of ordinary commands works in general (a Miscellaneous Charge for the amount, then the Credit applied) and a second only in part (docs/010 8g entry); a reversal draft is added to slice 10.
  - For 11c and 11e: the Credit reversal action (`POST /customer-credits/{credit_id}/reverse`) is shown as unavailable for such a Credit (the database answers `P4904`, a 409), as 11d already notes for moved Credits, and points to applying the Credit or to the `CREDIT_CUSTOMER` Refund draft.

HTTP routes and screens stay in slice 11e. Post-settlement corrections stay in slice 10.

### Slice 10 — Post-settlement corrections (BR-SETTLE-013)
- Change: correction draft linked to the closed Settlement with a reason; kinds per Q15; confirm by `financial.approve`; no time limit; the closed Settlement row is never changed.
- Migration: next free number, additive (DB-SETTLE-003, 005).
- Tests: correction years after closure accepted; closed Settlement row unchanged; correction effect linked by exactly one typed foreign key; Staff cannot confirm.
- Added (owner answer to the 8e-2 report, 2026-10-06): the correction draft must handle a bounced Payment whose Customer Credit or Deposit a Final Settlement used (a Settlement Credit application or a Deposit application), with a test. Until slice 10 such a Payment cannot be reversed (accepted by the owner).
- Added (owner answer to the 8f report, 2026-10-06, sub-slice 8g): a draft that reverses a mistaken credit Adjustment. Today no draft-based way exists: `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals` has no writer; a debit Adjustment is refused on a fully paid line (BR-INV-044, migration 66); the one-call Credit reversal is refused for a Credit released to a Customer other than the payer (8g) and for a Credit released from a moved allocation unless the Payment is a normal Customer Payment and the Credit is its payer's (slice 7); where it is allowed, it returns the value to the Payment but leaves the line reduced. The draft (prepared by an `invoices.adjust` holder, confirmed by `financial.approve`) writes the Adjustment reversal with a reason and, in the same transaction, puts the released value back on the line, for every release of the Adjustment: a release from a Payment allocation made a new Credit, and the part of that Credit still available is applied to the line for the same Customer (BR-CREDIT-008 holds); a release from a Credit application returned the value to an existing Credit, and the part still available is applied again; any part already applied elsewhere or refunded stays as debt on the line. After the Lease's Final Settlement is closed it runs only as a linked post-settlement correction.
- Sec: recommended (Q17).

### Slice 10 design note (2026-10-06)

Written before any code, at the owner's request. Status: owner answers to Q-2026-10-06-36 received and built into this note (docs/010, 2026-10-06); follow-up points that do not block 10a are in Q-2026-10-06-37.

**Owner rules and how the design meets them (DL-068, DL-069, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013 as amended, plan Q15 and Q17 answers):**

1. A Settlement is never reopened: no correction updates `app.final_settlements`, its closure snapshots (DB-SETTLE-002, 004, 006) or any record the confirm wrote; the 8a-1 closure guard stays as it is. Every correction is new rows: a header (DB-SETTLE-003) and typed effect links (DB-SETTLE-005), each pointing to exactly one ordinary financial record that the correction wrote in the same transaction.
2. A later fix is a new linked correction with a reason: the header carries the reason, the Settlement, the draft and a correlation id; every effect record names the correction in its audit context.
3. The same approvers as the Settlement: Staff prepare under `final_settlements.prepare` (plan Q3: the Settlement preparer capability also prepares corrections); only a `financial.approve` holder confirms (draft kind `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION`, slice 2 functions for withdraw and reject; the same person may prepare and confirm only with `financial.approve`, plan Q4).
4. No time limit: no check on the age of the Settlement; only the chronology checks every writer has (not before the closure, not later than the clock plus 5 seconds).
5. Corrections pass the 8e-1 refusal (`app.refuse_closed_final_settlement_change`) and the 8e-2 refusal (`P5S01`) only through a linked correction row checked in the same transaction (deferred check), never through a session setting (8e-1 and 8e-2 security notes).

**Correction kinds** (owner answer 1 to Q-2026-10-06-36; each a variant of the draft kind `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION`, prepared under `final_settlements.prepare` with a reason, confirmed only by `financial.approve`):

- `EXTRA_CHARGE`: a new one-time charge (an existing category, for example Miscellaneous or Damage, BR-INV-044) on a new issued Invoice with the Lease, to the Customer of the obligation it corrects, or to the Primary Tenant when it corrects nothing on an Invoice (owner answer 6: a Signer's own debt stays on that Signer's ledger, other Lease debt goes to the Primary Tenant, BR-SETTLE-016); Due Date = issue date + Property due-days, with the existing override; no Late-payment Charge on a correction Invoice (the 8e-1 refusal of Late-payment Decisions after closure stays). Debt stays on that Customer's ledger.
- `CHARGE_REDUCTION` (the fifth kind, owner answer 1: a clear fix for a wrong charge that is still unpaid): a linked credit Adjustment that removes the wrong unpaid amount; refused when it would release paid value (that is a `REFUND`).
- `REFUND` (a wrong charge that was paid, or a returned Settlement Refund): a linked credit Adjustment on the Settlement obligation; the part paid by a Payment allocation or a Credit application is released as today (migration 67) and the part paid by a Deposit application is released through a new Deposit application reversal (owner answer 2), so the Deposit holds it again; a linked Refund pays the released value to its owner (Credit Refund to the Credit's Customer, Deposit Refund to the Primary Tenant or the agreed Signer, BR-REFUND-015).
