A `REFUND` always pays a Refund; an unpaid part in the same Adjustment is removed with it. A returned Settlement Refund (8d note) records the verified return and a new Refund in one correction.
- `RETURNED_PAYMENT` (owner item and owner answer 5): for a Payment whose Credit or Deposit the Settlement used, the correction reverses, in one transaction, the Settlement's Credit applications sourced from that Payment's Credits, the Deposit applications of a Deposit that Payment funded, the Payment's other allocations and Credit applications, then the Credit reversals, the Deposit receipt reversal and the Payment reversal; the debt comes back on the original obligations and stays on their Customers' ledgers (BR-SETTLE-016). A Settlement Refund already paid from a Deposit that the returned Payment funded cannot be undone (money left the Property): it becomes an `EXTRA_CHARGE` to the Deposit owner in the same correction. The same returned-Payment draft also works for a Payment with no closed Settlement (owner answer 5): it passes the slice 7 and 8g refusals only through its own linked record, so it ends both lasting blocks; tested with a returned third-party Payment whose adjustment Credit was released to another Customer. Its record shape and preparer capability are Q-2026-10-06-37 points 2 and 3.
- `ADJUSTMENT_REVERSAL` (owner request in 8g, owner answer 7): the mistaken credit Adjustment reversal of the slice 10 entry above; before closure an ordinary draft prepared under `invoices.adjust`, after closure only inside a correction prepared under `final_settlements.prepare`; `financial.approve` confirms both.
- `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` (owner answer 4): the reversal of a kept Deposit's forfeiture after closure runs only inside a correction, which follows DL-032 (owner answer 3 to Q-2026-10-05-23, Q-2026-10-05-24): the Deposit first pays this Lease's debt that is still open, through Deposit applications linked to the correction (DB-SETTLE-005 `deposit_application_id`, owner answer 4), and only the rest is refunded (a linked Deposit Refund). Its name as a kind is Q-2026-10-06-37 point 1.

**A real wrong-Unit case after closure** (owner answer 4): the one-call move draft of an allocation that existed at closure on a Settlement obligation is refused (sub-slice 10h below). Staff fix it with a correction of the closed Settlement whose obligation the wrong allocation paid: the same slice 7 move (the confirmation text of both Customers: who, the date, how; the source allocation reversed and the same Payment allocated to the correct Invoice, `WRONG_UNIT_MOVE`), linked to the correction (`payment_allocation_reversal_id`, `payment_allocation_id`) and confirmed by `financial.approve`; the debt comes back on the Settlement obligation and stays on its Customer's ledger (BR-SETTLE-016); the correct Invoice may belong to any Lease, open or settled (allocations to a closed Settlement's obligations stay open, owner answer 7 to Q-2026-10-05-20). Proposed as a seventh variant `PAYMENT_MOVE` (Q-2026-10-06-37 point 1); a move whose source allocation was made after closure stays the ordinary move draft.

**Read-only path search (2026-10-06): one-call or draft paths that can change a closed Settlement's money without a correction.** Method: a throwaway database at head `20261031_96`; every `app` function the financial or runtime role may execute; its writes followed through called functions and the triggers of each written table; no role has a direct write grant on any money table. Grouped by what happens after closure:

- Refused after closure (8e-1, 8e-2): `void_invoice`, `adjust_invoice`, `resolve_hand_back_rent_invoice`, `resolve_unpaid_initial_never_moved_in`, `credit_initial_rent_after_termination`, `finalize_late_payment_decision`, `correct_late_payment_charge`, `record_one_time_charge` and `record_unit_cleaning_charge` with the Lease, `create_invoice_draft`, `correct_invoice_draft` and `issue_invoice` with the Lease, `generate_rent_service_invoice`, `record_scheduled_rent_item` and `record_scheduled_utility_item` (skipped), `utility_final_invoice_create`, `utility_catch_up_invoice_create`; the reversal of a Settlement Credit application (`reverse_credit_application`, P5S01); a receipt reversal while a Deposit application is active (8a-1); the one-call correction of a Settlement Refund (8d); `start_final_settlement` (one per Lease).
- Open, one call, no draft, no approver (they change the closed Settlement's money):
  1. `reverse_payment_allocation_command` (`payments.allocations.reverse`, `POST /payment-allocations/{allocation_id}/reverse`): an allocation that paid a Settlement obligation before closure is reversed; the debt comes back and the value returns to the Payment, from where it can be recognized as Credit, allocated elsewhere or refunded (a Refund needs a draft). Moved allocations are refused (slice 7).
  2. `reverse_credit_application` (`credits.applications.reverse`, `POST /credit-applications/{application_id}/reverse`): an ordinary Credit application that paid a Settlement obligation before closure is reversed; the debt comes back and the Credit is available again.
  3. `reverse_payment_command` (`payments.reverse`, `POST /payments/{payment_id}/reverse`) after 1 and 2: the Payment is reversed (a returned transfer) and the debt stays open.
  4. `recognize_customer_credit` (`credits.recognize`) and `reverse_customer_credit` (`credits.reverse`) on value freed by 1: no direct effect on the obligations, but they move the freed value.
- Open with a draft and `financial.approve`, but not linked to the Settlement:
  5. `confirm_payment_move_draft`: a wrong-Unit move of an allocation that paid a Settlement obligation before closure reopens that debt.
  6. `confirm_lease_deposit_reversal_draft`: the reversal of a confirmed forfeiture of a kept Deposit after closure makes that Deposit hold money again, which the closed Settlement can neither apply nor refund.
  7. `confirm_refund_draft` of a Payment or Credit Refund of value freed by 1 or 2 (money leaves only with an approver).
- Open and intended (owner answer 7 to Q-2026-10-05-20: the Customer can still pay the debt): `post_payment_allocations`, `post_credit_application`, new Payments (`confirm_usd_payment`, `confirm_khr_payment`). They lower the debt the closure left and do not touch the closed records.
- Not affecting a Lease Settlement: `create_reservation` (Reservation Deposit receipts), `confirm_reservation_deposit_draft`, `verify_usd_refund_return` and `reverse_refund_correction` (refused for a Settlement Refund; other Refunds give value back to their own source).

Owner answers 3 and 4 to Q-2026-10-06-36 (approved tightenings): after closure 1, 2 and 5 are refused for the allocations and Credit applications that existed at closure on the Settlement's obligations, and 6 runs only inside a `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` correction; allocations and Credit applications made after closure stay reversible (sub-slice 10h).

**Sub-slices** (each: one full backend unit run and one full PostgreSQL run, independent and security review (Q17), the standing round-3 rule, one checkpoint commit, one ops/NOW.md line):

- **10a — Correction record** (BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-AUTH-010; DB-SETTLE-003, DB-SETTLE-005 as amended by owner answer 2). The header and effect tables, the shared internal confirm step every variant uses (the decision, then the header), the deferred checks, and the passage through the 8e-1 and 8e-2 refusals for a record linked to a correction of the same Settlement. No variant writer yet.
  - Pre-code design (2026-10-06, read only; within the design note, DB-SETTLE-003 and DB-SETTLE-005 as amended by owner answer 2). Migration `20261101_97`:
    - `app.settlement_corrections` exactly as DB-SETTLE-003 (`correlation_id` is the `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft id, unique, a foreign key to the draft; `authorization_reference` is `financial.approve`). BEFORE INSERT check: the Settlement is closed; `occurred_at` not before the closure and not later than the clock plus 5 seconds (no other time check: no time limit); the draft is a `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft of the same Property under `final_settlements.prepare` naming this Settlement, with the same reason; the actor is an active financial actor on the Property. Deferred check at commit: the draft is CONFIRMED by that actor under `financial.approve` (42501 otherwise, as 8d, 8e-2 and 8f) and the correction has at least one effect.
    - `app.settlement_correction_effects` as DB-SETTLE-005 with the approved links (`invoice_id`, `payment_reversal_id`, `customer_credit_reversal_id`, `deposit_receipt_reversal_id`, `deposit_application_id`; `deposit_application_reversal_id` comes with its table in 10c-2): every link a deferrable foreign key (so the link can be written before its record) and unique (a record is the effect of at most one correction); exactly one link per row; an effect only for a correction confirmed in the same transaction (as built after review rounds 1 and 2: the CONFIRMED decision's result carries the top-level transaction id, `pg_current_xact_id()`, every check compares it with the current one and needs the decision written after the current transaction started; a time comparison alone was not exact under READ COMMITTED, and a transaction id alone can be handed out again after a logical restore).
    - Both tables append-only; no application role may read or write them.
    - Internal functions (no grant): `app.begin_settlement_correction(property, approver, draft, authorization)` (checks the approver and `financial.approve`, takes the draft lock and then the Settlement's draft lock, locks the pending draft, needs the Settlement closed (55000 otherwise, a conflict), records the CONFIRMED decision, then writes the header with an id derived from the draft) and `app.link_settlement_correction_effect(correction, link, record)` (the next effect sequence); `app.settlement_correction_linked(settlement, table, record)` answers whether a record is linked to a correction of that Settlement confirmed in this transaction (never NULL; the refusals also test `IS NOT TRUE`).
    - Relaxing patches: the 8e-1 refusal (`app.refuse_closed_final_settlement_change`) lets through an Invoice, an Adjustment, an Adjustment reversal or a Late-payment Charge correction linked that way; the 8e-2 refusal lets through the reversal of a Settlement Credit application linked that way. VOID, Late-payment Decisions and Charges, and one-time charge records have no link, so they stay refused (owner answer 6: no Late-payment Charge on a correction Invoice; Q-2026-10-06-37 point 4 for the one-time charge record).
    - Notes for every variant writer: replay an exact repeat (the same draft confirmed by the same approver) before anything else, and refuse a stale draft (its sources changed since it was recorded: compare a source revision stored in the draft, as the Settlement draft does) and leave it pending; then call `app.begin_settlement_correction`; link only records the writer itself writes, and link and write each of them in the same call; write every linked record in its final state before the writer returns (no linked Invoice left DRAFT), and check that at the end, because a linked record id lets any later statement of the same transaction through the refusals (security review round 1); set no constraint IMMEDIATE before every linked record is written (the effect links are deferred foreign keys, and the header's deferred check needs one effect); several existing functions (for example `app.reverse_credit_application`) set `ALL IMMEDIATE`, so a writer writes its own records instead of calling them. The service and the 11e route check `financial.approve` before calling the writer (the database checks only the reference and an active person, as for every draft since slice 2). The first writer that links an Invoice (10b) also adds a database check that refuses the commit while an Invoice linked to the correction is still DRAFT (security review round 2 note: it moves the writer rule into the database). The 11e routes leave the decision result's `transaction_id` out of draft responses (security review round 2 note). 10b, which rebuilds `app.settlement_correction_linked` for its new link, adds a test of the `decided_at` bound in the three checks (a decision with the current transaction id but an older `decided_at`: no effect, no link, 42501 at commit) (round 3 notes, both reviewers).
    - Tests (new module, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): an open Settlement refuses a correction; a correction commits only with its draft confirmed by the same approver and at least one effect; exactly one link per effect, unique links; an Adjustment and an Adjustment reversal pass the 8e-1 refusal only when linked in the same transaction to a correction of the same Settlement (not another Settlement's, not a committed one); a Settlement Credit application reversal passes the 8e-2 refusal the same way; VOID stays refused; the Settlement row and its snapshots unchanged; append-only; least privilege; no time check other than the two bounds; forward-only.
- **10b — `EXTRA_CHARGE`**. Tests: the charge, its Invoice, Customer, Due Date and ledger; no Late-payment Decision on it; the 8e-1 refusal still holds for an unlinked charge.
  - Pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; within the design note, owner answer 6 to Q-2026-10-06-36 and owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-06-37). Migration `20261102_98`:
    - DB-SETTLE-005 gains the link `authorized_nonrecurring_obligation_id` (deferrable, unique, as the other links); the one-link check covers it; `app.settlement_correction_linked` and `app.link_settlement_correction_effect` are rebuilt with it, and the 8e-1 refusal lets a one-time charge record through when it is linked that way (the same rule as the Invoice).
    - The commit check from the writer notes: a deferred check on each Invoice link refuses the commit while that Invoice is still DRAFT (23514). Consequence: the 10a test of the Invoice UPDATE path committed a correction with a linked DRAFT; that test changes its expectations (the committed-link half now expects this refusal; the same-transaction half issues the moved draft before commit); docs/010 note. As built: the same-transaction half expects this check's refusal instead, because `app.correct_invoice_draft` runs it at once while the draft is still DRAFT (full run P1); the check is positive (it refuses an Invoice that is not issued or not yet written, security review round 1).
    - Recorder `app.record_settlement_extra_charge_draft(property, actor, draft, settlement, charge, reason, authorization)` (`final_settlements.prepare`; EXECUTE to the financial role, as the Settlement recorder): the Settlement is closed (55000 otherwise); the charge is a Miscellaneous or Damage one-time charge (description, amount in cents, evidence reference; a Damage charge names a current, unbilled inspection finding of the Lease, BR-INV-044); its Customer is the Customer of the corrected Invoice when the draft names one (an issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Settlement's Lease), otherwise the Primary Tenant (owner answer 6; BR-SETTLE-016); a Damage charge always goes to the Primary Tenant (the existing Damage rule); an optional Due Date override with its reason (the existing override). The reason is at most 500 characters, because the one-time charge record keeps it (choice). The payload stores the variant, the Settlement, the Lease, the Customer and the charge; the source revision is the digest of the Settlement closure, the Primary Tenant, the corrected Invoice's Customer and state, and the finding's state. An exact repeat replays; a reused draft id is P5205.
    - Confirm `app.confirm_settlement_extra_charge(property, approver, draft, authorization)` (`financial.approve`; a human approver; EXECUTE to the financial role): replay of an exact repeat first; then the draft lock, the Settlement draft lock and the `invoice-lease` lock of the one-time charge recorder; a draft whose sources changed is stale (P5S02) and stays pending; then `app.begin_settlement_correction`; then, each linked before it is written, the one-time charge record (authorized by the approver under `financial.approve`, Due Date rule issue date plus Property days) and its Invoice with the Lease and one line, issued in the same call (Due Date = the business date plus the Property's one-time charge days, or the override, never before the issue date, 55000), with `invoice.one_time_charge.recorded`, `invoice.created` and `invoice.issued` audit events whose correlation is the correction; it checks at its end that the Invoice is issued. No Late-payment Charge: the 8e-1 refusal of Late-payment Decisions after closure stays. Ids are derived from the draft, so an exact repeat replays. As built after review round 1: the confirm takes the draft lock before the replay (the safer order, as the Settlement confirm); the records get fresh ids (a client chooses one-time charge, Invoice and audit ids elsewhere and could take a derived id first) and a repeat finds them through the correction's links; the confirm runs only at READ COMMITTED, locks the Lease row (FOR SHARE) after the Settlement draft lock, stamps the records after the approval, and never returns the decision's transaction id.
    - Service: new module `property_rental/settlement_correction.py` (`final_settlements.prepare` records, `financial.approve` confirms, checked before any connection; the Final Settlement error mapping; P5S02 at confirm is stale; 23505 stays a conflict after review round 1, because the locks serialize two confirms on one Lease). Routes stay in 11e.
    - Note for every later writer (full run P1 of 10b): once every linked record is written, the writer runs `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` itself, as the existing Invoice writers do; otherwise the deferred Invoice validators, which are not definer functions, run at COMMIT under the financial role and fail with 42501. A function that sets `ALL IMMEDIATE` while a linked Invoice is still DRAFT (`app.create_invoice_draft`, `app.correct_invoice_draft`) is refused by the new commit check, so a writer writes its own Invoice. Review round 1 notes for later writers: a variant writer is the only statement of its transaction (its `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` runs every pending deferred check with the function owner's rights; the service runs one call per transaction); its record ids are fresh, never derived from the draft; and when 10e needs an extra charge inside a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction, the linked obligation, Invoice and issue writes of `app.confirm_settlement_extra_charge` move into an internal helper with no begin and no `ALL IMMEDIATE`; that helper then makes `source_reference` unique per charge (`'settlement-correction:'||correction||':'||obligation`), because `(customer_id, category, source_reference)` is unique and one correction may then write two charges of one category for one Customer (security review round 2); `app.settlement_extra_charge_result` then returns arrays, because its scalar reads assume one charge and one Invoice per correction (review round 3). The general draft reads (`app.financial_draft_resource_by_capability`, `app.list_financial_drafts_by_capability`) still return the decision's transaction id of a `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft; 11e strips it there too (security review round 2).
    - Tests (new module, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): the charge, its Invoice, Customer (Primary Tenant; another Signer's corrected Invoice), Due Date (Property days and the override) and ledger; Damage charge with a finding; replay and reused id; stale draft stays pending; Staff cannot confirm; no Late-payment Decision on the correction Invoice; the 8e-1 refusal still holds for an unlinked charge and for a charge linked to another Settlement's correction; the DRAFT commit check; the `decided_at` bound in the three checks (round 3 notes of 10a); the Settlement row and snapshots unchanged; least privilege and forward-only.
- **10c — `CHARGE_REDUCTION` and `REFUND`**: 10c-1 obligations that are unpaid or paid by Payments or Credits (the migration 67 release, Credit and Payment Refunds); 10c-2 obligations paid by a Deposit application (the new `deposit_application_reversals` table and its readers, `deposit_application_reversal_id`), the Deposit Refund, a returned Settlement Refund (8d note). Also the 8a-1 notes: `app.invoice_adjustment_reversals` joins `app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change` once 10f writes it; the Late-payment Charge correction checks Deposit applications before `P4608` if Charge corrections become a correction effect.
  - Split (2026-10-07; a split needs no owner answer): **10c-1a** `CHARGE_REDUCTION` on Invoice lines; **10c-1b** `REFUND` of Invoice lines paid by Payment allocations or Credit applications (the released value becomes or returns to Customer Credit, as migration 67 does, and is refunded as a Credit Refund to the Credit's Customer); **10c-2** as above. A wrong Late-payment Charge after closure is Q-2026-10-07-38 (a sub-slice 10c-3 if the owner agrees); it does not block 10c-1a or 10c-1b.
  - 10c-1a pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; within the design note). Migration `20261103_99`:
    - Recorder `app.record_settlement_charge_reduction_draft(property, actor, draft, settlement, reduction, reason, authorization)` (`final_settlements.prepare`; EXECUTE to the financial role): the Settlement is closed (55000); the Invoice is an issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Settlement's Lease (P0002, or 55000 when not issued; 10b choice 2); the line belongs to it (P0002); the amount, in cents, is at most the line's unpaid rest (`app.invoice_line_outstanding`), otherwise 55000 "would release paid value" (design note: that is a `REFUND`); an Invoice with a Deposit application is refused with the 8a-1 refusal's message (55000; 10c-2 relaxes it for linked records only); the reason is at most 500 characters (the Adjustment keeps it, as `app.adjust_invoice` allows). Payload: the variant, the Settlement, the Lease, the Invoice's Customer (an Adjustment follows its Invoice, BR-INV-045) and the reduction. Source revision: the digest of the Settlement closure, the Invoice (Lease, Customer, issue state; as built the issue state, not the status, so a Payment on another line of the Invoice does not make the draft stale, review round 2), the line's adjusted obligation and paid value, and the Invoice's Deposit applications. An exact repeat replays; a reused draft id is P5205.
    - Confirm `app.confirm_settlement_charge_reduction(property, approver, draft, authorization)` (`financial.approve`; a human approver; EXECUTE to the financial role), as 10b: the draft lock, then the replay of an exact repeat; READ COMMITTED only; the Settlement's draft lock, then the Invoice `FOR UPDATE` (the 8e-1 refusal's order: Invoice, then the Settlement row); a draft whose sources changed is stale (P5S02) and stays pending; the stored reduction passes the shape check again; then `app.begin_settlement_correction`; then the credit Adjustment (fresh id), linked before it is written: the amount, `source_reference` `settlement-correction:<correction>`, the draft's reason, the approver, `financial.approve` (the capability that authorized it, as 10b choice 3); the Invoice status, version and time refreshed as `app.adjust_invoice` does; `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`; audit events `invoice.adjusted` and `final_settlement.correction.confirmed` with the correction as correlation. Nothing is released: the amount stays within the unpaid rest under the locks. As built: the shape check of the stored reduction runs before the Settlement's draft lock (it gives the Invoice to lock), then the Invoice lock, then the stale check; at the confirm a line that changed since the draft is always P5S02, so the recorder's 55000 refusals (release of paid value, Deposit application) are not reached there (review round 1). For 11e (security review round 1): at a correction confirm a 23514 from the Adjustment chronology check (for example a Payment allocation stamped a few seconds ahead) leaves the draft pending and a retry succeeds; the route should word it as "try again", not "invalid".
    - Service: `SettlementCorrectionService.record_charge_reduction` and `confirm_charge_reduction` (the 10b mapping).
    - Tests (new module `test_settlement_charge_reduction_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): the reduction, its Adjustment, link, header, audit and ledger; a partly paid line reduced within its unpaid rest; a reduction that would release paid value refused at record, and a draft that becomes so after a later Payment is stale at confirm and stays pending; an Invoice of another Lease, a DRAFT Invoice, a line of another Invoice and an Invoice with a Deposit application refused; Staff cannot confirm; exact repeat and reused key; READ COMMITTED; the 8e-1 refusal still holds for an unlinked Adjustment; the Settlement row unchanged; least privilege, no time limit, forward-only.
  - 10c-1b pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only). Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-39 received (docs/010): (1) the new append-only table `settlement_correction_refund_details`; (2) the approved tightening on the remainder; both are built into this design below. The parts that did not depend on the answer:
    - Recorder (`final_settlements.prepare`): an issued, non-VOID Invoice of the Settlement's Lease, one line, the amount to remove (at most the line's adjusted obligation) and a Refund method (active) and reference note (BR-REFUND-014). The released part (the amount less the line's unpaid rest) must be above zero (otherwise it is a `CHARGE_REDUCTION`, 55000); an Invoice that a Deposit application paid stays refused until 10c-2. The draft stores the released sources the confirm will reverse, in the order of migration 67 (newest first: Payment allocations and Credit applications on the line), each with its released amount, the Credit it becomes or returns to, that Credit's Customer (the Refund recipient) and whether that Customer is the payer. The source revision covers the line, those sources and their Credits; any change is stale (P5S02). As built: the revision covers the line and every source that pays it (the stored sources name their Credit, Payment and Customers, not Credit balances); a Credit or Payment cannot change in a way that matters while its application or allocation is active (review round 1).
    - Confirm (`financial.approve`, human approver, READ COMMITTED): locks in the order of the Credit and allocation writers (each source Payment, then its Credit, then the Invoice), the stale check, `app.begin_settlement_correction`, then the release written by the correction itself, every record linked before it is written (the allocation and Credit application reversals, the re-posted remainders, the credit Adjustment), because the existing migration 67 functions give fresh ids inside and a Settlement Credit application's reversal passes the 8e-2 refusal only when linked first; then one Credit Refund per released source to the Credit's Customer (`refunds.create` on the Refund row as every confirmed Refund draft, `processed_by` the approver, `processed_at` after the Credit's latest event), each linked (`refund_id`); then `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`, audit events and the result.
    - Relaxing patches: the general Refund source check and the deferred `refunds_credit_customer_confirmed` check also accept a Credit Refund to a Customer other than the payer when the Refund is linked to a correction confirmed in this transaction and its Credit was released by that correction's own Adjustment (BR-CREDIT-014, as 8f).
    - As answered (migration `20261104_100`): `app.settlement_correction_refund_details` (append-only; no role may read or write it): `refund_id` (PK, deferred FK, written before its Refund so the source check reads it), `settlement_correction_id`, `draft_id` (the correction's draft), `refund_method_id`, `reference_note` (1 to 500 characters), `recorded_at`; a BEFORE INSERT check needs the correction confirmed in this transaction with that draft (as built after review round 1: and the Refund already linked to that correction; a deferred check needs the Refund processed by the correction's approver). The Refund row carries the recipient. The draft holds one Refund method and one reference note (Q-39 option (a)); the confirm writes one details row per Refund, so each Refund keeps its own method, note and recipient. Every released value goes to the Invoice Customer (a Payment allocation's released value becomes that Customer's Credit, BR-CREDIT-014; a Credit application gives its value back to its Credit, which BR-CREDIT-008 makes that Customer's), so every Refund of one correction has that recipient. The recorder refuses an unknown or inactive method (55000, as the Settlement draft); the confirm locks the method row (FOR SHARE, as 8d).
    - The relaxing check `app.settlement_correction_refund_allowed(refund)`: a details row for the Refund whose correction is that draft's and was confirmed by the Refund's approver, the Refund linked to that correction in this transaction (`app.settlement_correction_linked`), a Credit source paid to its own Customer, and an amount within what that correction's own Adjustment released to that Credit (a released allocation's new Credit, or a reversed application's Credit).
    - Approved tightening (Q-39 answer 2): a new BEFORE INSERT trigger on `app.customer_credit_application_reversals` refuses (P5S01, a conflict, the 8e-2 message) the reversal of a Credit application that re-posts, directly or through earlier re-posts, the remainder of a Settlement Credit application (walked back through `invoice_credit_excess_releases`), unless the reversal is linked to a correction of that Settlement confirmed in this transaction. Such a row exists only through a correction, so it refuses nothing staff can do today. 10h decides the same for allocations and counts every re-posted remainder of a closure-era record as existing at closure.
    - Unchanged: the Refund correction paths (a verified return and its reversal) work for a correction's Credit Refund as for any Credit Refund (the value returns to the Credit, not to the Settlement's obligations); the generic Refund, allocation and Credit audits keep their shape, and the correction's own `final_settlement.correction.confirmed` event names every released source, re-post, released Credit and Refund (design note point 2; review round 1).
    - As built (review round 1): the confirm locks every source Payment and then every source Credit, each in id order, before the Invoice (deadlock-safe with the single-Payment writers). A one-call `app.adjust_invoice` or `app.void_invoice` on the same Invoice locks the Invoice first and then Payments, so it can deadlock with a confirm; both are refused after closure anyway (8e-1), the loser gets 40P01 (a conflict) and the draft stays pending. 10h may add an early closed-Settlement check before the release in `app.adjust_invoice`.
    - Tests (new module `test_settlement_refund_correction_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): a paid line released from a Payment allocation and refunded (Adjustment, reversal, re-posted remainder, Credit, Refund, details, links, audit, ledger, Settlement row unchanged, replay, Staff cannot confirm, the one-call Adjustment still refused); one correction paying a Payment and a Settlement Credit application in two Refunds, and the one-call reversal of the re-posted remainder refused, also after a second correction; a third-party Payment refunded to the Invoice Customer, not the payer, and the relaxed check accepts nothing outside a correction; refusals (open Settlement, nothing released, more than the charge, inactive method, Deposit-applied Invoice, malformed input), a changed line is stale and stays pending, READ COMMITTED, key reuse and crossed variants; least privilege and forward-only. Added in review rounds 1 and 2: a Credit released before closure from a third-party Payment, applied to another line and refunded to the Invoice Customer; an ordinary re-posted Credit application that still reverses with one call; the relaxed checks inside a hand-built correction.
  - Added to 10c-2 (owner answer 4 to the 10c-1b review record, 2026-10-07; approved tightening; 10h at the latest): a Credit Refund paid by a post-settlement correction (a Refund with a `settlement_correction_refund_details` row) is corrected only through another post-settlement correction, as 8d made the Settlement Deposit Refunds; the one-call verified return (`app.verify_usd_refund_return`) refuses it, otherwise a one-call return could create Credit that pays debt without an approver. 10c-2's returned-Refund correction (a verified return and a new Refund in one correction, 8d note) covers such a Refund too. Test: the one-call return of a correction Refund refused, and the same return through a correction accepted. Choice 9 of the 10c-1b docs/010 entry describes 10c-1b as built until then.
  - 10c-2 pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; within the design note). STOPPED for Q-2026-10-07-41 before code. Split (a split needs no owner answer): **10c-2a** `REFUND` of an Invoice line paid by a Deposit application; **10c-2b** the returned-Refund correction (a verified return and a new Refund in one correction) for a Settlement Deposit Refund (8d) and for a correction's Credit Refund, with the approved tightening above.
    - Read-only facts: DB-DEP-006 `deposit_application_reversals` is specified in docs/004 (`id`, `deposit_application_id` unique, `reason`, `occurred_at`, `actor_id`, `authorization_reference`) but marked "not built"; DB-DER-004 already subtracts only Deposit applications not reversed by it. Migration 86 put Deposit applications into every line paid value, Invoice status, Late-payment Charge balance, Deposit held balance, the Payment reversal and action contexts (20 references), migration 88 into the Customer Ledger (`DEPOSIT_APPLIED`) and the Deposit history; each needs "not reversed" and the ledger needs a reversal event. `app.post_deposit_application` and the 8d trigger refuse any Deposit application after closure ("A closed Final Settlement takes no new Deposit application"). The 8d Deposit Refund source trigger accepts a Deposit Refund with no Reservation event and no Pet Refund review only with a `final_settlement_refund_details` row (one per Deposit per Settlement, with `signer_agreement`), refuses it while the Lease has open debt that a Deposit could pay (DL-032), and a closed Settlement takes no new Refund. `settlement_correction_refund_details` has no `deposit_id` or `signer_agreement`. The 8d one-call Refund correction refusal (23514) covers every Settlement Deposit Refund.
    - 10c-2a, apart from the open points: the recorder (`final_settlements.prepare`) takes the line, the amount, the method and note, and for a Deposit Refund the recipient (Primary Tenant, or another signed Signer with the written agreement, BR-REFUND-015, as 8d); the confirm (`financial.approve`) writes, each linked before it is written, the Deposit application reversals (new table, link `deposit_application_reversal_id`), the release of allocations and Credit applications as 10c-1b, the credit Adjustment (the 8a-1 refusal passes it only when every Deposit application of the line is reversed in the same correction), then the Refunds; the readers above are rebuilt with "not reversed"; the Customer Ledger gains `DEPOSIT_APPLICATION_REVERSED` on both components; the 10c-1a and 10c-1b recorders stop refusing a Deposit-paid Invoice only through this path.
    - Open (Q-2026-10-07-41): DL-032 for the released Deposit value; release order across Deposit applications and Payment or Credit sources; where a correction Deposit Refund keeps its Deposit and Signer agreement; the returned-Refund correction's rules. Answered 2026-10-07 (docs/010); two conflicts found while applying answers 1 and 4 are Q-2026-10-07-42, answered 2026-10-07 ((a) to all three; docs/010; see "Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-42" below).
    - Split of 10c-2a (2026-10-07; a split needs no owner answer): **10c-2a-1** the Deposit application reversal record and its readers (does not depend on Q-42; built first, as 10a was while Q-37 was open); **10c-2a-2** the shared step of answer 1 (released or held Deposit value first pays the Lease's open debt under the Settlement rules, then the rest is refunded; reused by 10d; waits for Q-42 points 1 and 2); **10c-2a-3** the `REFUND` correction of a Deposit-paid line (waits for Q-42).
    - 10c-2a-1 pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; head `20261104_100` dumped in the session scratchpad; DB-DEP-006, DB-SETTLE-005, DB-DER-004, DB-DER-010; owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-06-36). Migration `20261105_101`:
      - `app.deposit_application_reversals` exactly as DB-DEP-006 (`id`, `deposit_application_id` unique, `reason` 1 to 1,000 characters as the application's, `occurred_at`, `actor_id`, `authorization_reference` = `financial.approve`); append-only; no role may read or write it. BEFORE INSERT check (23514): the reversal is linked to a correction of the application's Settlement confirmed in this transaction (`app.settlement_correction_linked`), its actor is that correction's approver, and `occurred_at` is not before the application or the closure and not later than the clock plus 5 seconds. One reversal per application; a partial release reverses the whole application and re-posts the rest (10c-2a-2 or -3, as migration 67).
      - DB-SETTLE-005 gains `deposit_application_reversal_id` (deferrable, unique); the one-link check and the two link functions are rebuilt with it (the 10b pattern).
      - Readers count only applications not reversed: `app.invoice_line_deposit_value_at` (as of its time), `app.deposit_balance_events` (a reversal adds the amount back), `app.deposit_snapshot`, `app.late_payment_charge_application_summary`, and the 8a-1 refusal `app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change` (relaxing: an obligation whose Deposit applications are all reversed is not refused by it; after closure the 8e-1 refusal still stops every unlinked change); `app.allocation_target_latest_at` also counts the reversal time; `app.deposit_has_active_application` already reads the table when it exists (no change).
      - Customer Ledger: new event `DEPOSIT_APPLICATION_REVERSED` (order 76), the two `DEPOSIT_APPLIED` components with the opposite effect; the ledger page accepts it as a filter. Deposit history: the bounded read `app.deposit_application_reversal_history(property, deposit)` (runtime EXECUTE only) and rows `APPLICATION_REVERSAL_INVOICE_LINE` and `APPLICATION_REVERSAL_LATE_PAYMENT_CHARGE`.
      - Not changed here: the Deposit application writer and the 8d refusal of a new Deposit application after closure (10c-2a-2 lets a linked one through); the 10c-1a and 10c-1b recorders' refusal of a Deposit-paid Invoice (10c-2a-3).
      - As built after review round 1 (independent and security): the BEFORE INSERT check also locks the Deposit (`app.serialize_deposit_state`) and then the Invoice or Charge (`app.lock_payment_allocation_target`) and needs `occurred_at` later than the latest event of the target and of the Deposit and not before the correction, as the Deposit application's own check (migration 86); the reason follows the application's rule; a deferred trigger refreshes the reversed line's Invoice status and version at commit (the application's own pattern), replacing the earlier "its writer" choice. Notes for the writers (10c-2a-3, 10e): take the Deposit lock before the Invoice lock, in the order the other Deposit writers use, and date each reversal after those latest events (as 10c-1b dates its release with `app.credit_excess_instant`); once a reversal leaves a Deposit holding money after closure, a Deposit receipt reversal draft of that Deposit is no longer held back by an active application (still approver-gated, 8r), so 10c-2a-3 or 10e decides whether such a draft needs a closed-Settlement check. Review round 2: a NULL time is refused by the check itself; the writers date reversals from the clock, or add Deposit applications and reversals to the time checks of a later Adjustment, VOID or Late-payment Charge correction on the same obligation (those count only Payment and Credit records). Review round 3 notes for the writers: the check accepts a reversal linked to any correction of the Settlement (the 10a rule), so each writer makes sure its confirmed draft describes the reversals it writes; and once a Deposit holds money again after closure, besides the receipt reversal draft, the writers confirm that no other one-call outflow (for example a Pet Refund-review Refund or a Lease forfeiture) can take that amount without a correction. As-built tests: the reversal of a line and a Charge application (every reader, Invoice status refresh, both ledgers, filtered ledger read, Deposit history, second reversal refused); refusals (unlinked, another Settlement's correction, another actor or authorization, unknown application, invalid reason, before the correction, not later than the latest event of the Deposit or of the target (an allocation posted inside the correction's transaction), later than the clock plus 5 seconds, append-only); the 8a-1 relaxation for an Adjustment and a Late-payment Charge correction, and the 8e-1 refusal of an unlinked change after the reversal; least privilege and forward-only.
      - Application and frontend: the ledger event kind (`customer_ledger.py`, `customerLedgerClient.ts`), the Deposit history rows (`deposit_api.py`); docs 004, 005, 006.
      - Tests (new module `test_deposit_application_reversal_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): in a correction built by hand through the internal 10a functions (no writer yet), the reversal of a Pet Deposit application on a pet damage line and of one on a Late-payment Charge: Deposit held, line outstanding, Charge remaining and summary, latest event time, both ledgers (reconciled), the Deposit history through the service; refusals (unlinked, linked to another Settlement's correction, another actor or authorization, before the application, a second reversal, append-only); the 8a-1 refusal holds until the application is reversed; least privilege; forward-only. (Superseded by the as-built test list below; the "before the application" bound is covered by the latest-event bounds.)
    - Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-42 (2026-10-07; docs/010), built into the 10c-2 design: (1) before released or held Deposit value pays open debt, each Customer's own Credit pays that Customer's own open debt on this Lease, as the Settlement draft does (Q-29); the correction writes these Credit applications itself, linked to it and recorded as Credit applications of the Settlement (one-call reversal refused, as 8e-2), through the 8e-2 writer with a correction link (10c-2a-2). (2) Approved change of 10c-1b, built in 10c-2a-3 with tests for both cases: every `REFUND` correction first uses the Credit it releases against that Credit's own Customer's open debt on this Lease and refunds only the rest; a Customer with no open debt on this Lease gets the Refund as today. (3) A returned Deposit Refund goes back to the Deposit (DB-DER-004), not to Customer Credit: a return-only correction of a Settlement Deposit Refund (10c-2b) leaves the amount held by the Deposit; a later correction runs the 10c-2a-2 step on it (the Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order, then a Refund of the rest); the refund-only form of the returned-Refund correction is built in 10c-2b. Q-41 answer 4 (the returned amount stays as Customer Credit) applies to a returned Credit Refund only.
    - 10c-2a-2 pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; head `20261105_101` dumped in the session scratchpad; DL-032, BR-SETTLE-004, BR-SETTLE-005, BR-SETTLE-006, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-017, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-DEP-027, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-AUTH-010; owner answer 1 to Q-41, answer 3 to Q-41, answers 1 and 2 to Q-42). The shared DL-032 step after closure, reused by 10c-2a-3, 10c-2b and 10d. Migration `20261106_102`. No variant recorder or confirm, no grant, no route and no service: as 10c-2a-1, the tests build the correction by hand through the internal 10a functions.
      - Plan format: a correction draft that runs the step keeps in its payload `plan` (exactly the Settlement plan: `steps` of kinds `CUSTOMER_CREDIT`, `PET_DEPOSIT_PET_DAMAGE`, `SECURITY_DEPOSIT`, `PET_DEPOSIT_REMAINDER`, `DEPOSIT_REFUNDS` with the 8c keys, and `pet_remainder`), `plan_overlay` and `plan_result` (the check's result without its source revision). Staff choose every step; nothing is inferred between Deposit types (BR-SETTLE-006).
      - Overlay (`plan_overlay`): what the correction itself changes before the step, so a draft recorded before the release is checked against the state after it: `obligations` (an obligation key `INVOICE_LINE:<id>` or `LATE_PAYMENT_CHARGE:<id>` of this Lease and what it owes after the release), `credits` (a key with its Customer, the value added and, for value a released Payment allocation becomes (BR-CREDIT-014), `released_payment_allocation_id`; otherwise the key is the existing Credit that gets value back) and `deposits` (a Deposit of this Lease and the value it gets back). Amounts are cents, 0 to 9,000,000,000,000,000; malformed 22023; a key outside this Lease or Settlement, or a Credit of another Customer, 55000. A Deposit in the overlay counts as following DL-032 (the variant's release guarantees it; the confirm checks the real state).
      - `app.settlement_correction_plan_result(property, actor, settlement, plan, overlay)` (internal, STABLE): the Settlement must be closed (55000); the state is the Settlement preview (`app.final_settlement_preview`, the same Customers, Credits, pet damage, Deposit paths and step 5 rule) with the overlay applied; of the preview's blocking issues only `PENDING_DRAFT` refuses (55000; the 8r rule and the Settlement's pending-draft rule; the others describe the closure, which the 8e-1 refusals keep unchanged). The checks are those of the Settlement plan check (8c with the 8d (4a) rule): the DL-032 order, targets, amounts, the Credit rules (same Customer, available, the Rent-first proposal or the Customer's designation), only a DL-032 Deposit that holds money, the Deposit type of each step, pet damage first, the step 5 basis, every such Deposit ends with nothing held (BR-SETTLE-008), no Deposit Refund while open debt a Deposit could pay remains, the remaining Pet Deposit only after the Security Deposit is used, and the Refund method, recipient and agreement rules (BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015). Added for corrections: (R1, Q-42 answer 1) when any Deposit holds money after the overlay, each Customer's Credit pays that Customer's open debt, up to the lower of the two, before any Deposit pays (the Settlement rule, but only when a Deposit holds money; the Settlement draft applies it always; named for the owner after review round 1); (R2, Q-42 answer 2) a Credit in the overlay that has value left after the plan needs its Customer to owe nothing on this Lease after the plan; (Q-41 answer 3) every `DEPOSIT_REFUNDS` step of one plan has the same recipient, method, note and agreement (22023). Result: the Lease, the termination ground, the step 5 rule, `deposit_ids`, the open total, the closing balance and balances per Customer, `credit_left` for each overlay Credit key, and `source_revision` (`settlement-correction-plan:` and the digest of `app.final_settlement_source_state`, read in the same call). The 8c function is not changed.
      - `app.settlement_correction_credit_id(draft, key)` (internal): the Credit a plan key names inside that draft's correction: the Credit that a release of the correction's own credit Adjustment recognized from the overlay's `released_payment_allocation_id`, otherwise the key itself.
      - `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan(draft)` (internal): the step's locks, for a variant to call before `app.begin_settlement_correction` (the 8e-3 order; as amended by 10c-2a-3 security review round 2: after the two draft locks and the Lease row a variant takes no lock of its own except its Refund method `FOR SHARE`, last; the function is extended per variant, as for `REFUND`, so the variant's sources join its single id-ordered Payment, Credit and Invoice passes): the Payments and then the Credits of the existing Credits the Credit steps name, in id order; the Lease's Late-payment Charges and then Invoices in id order (`FOR UPDATE` where a step pays them, with the lines of a Credit step's Invoice; `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` otherwise); each Deposit of the Lease, in id order, its draft lock and its row. (As built after review round 1: the draft and Settlement draft locks first, and the Settlement row before the Deposits; the step calls it again.)
      - `app.post_settlement_correction_plan(property, approver, draft)` (internal): runs only inside the draft's correction confirmed in this transaction by that approver (23514 otherwise); after the variant's release it resolves the Credit keys, runs the plan check again on the real state (the overlay keeps only the released Credit keys, with nothing added) and compares the closing balance, the balances per Customer, `deposit_ids` and `credit_left` with the stored `plan_result` (otherwise stale, `P5S02`; the transaction rolls back and the draft stays pending); then writes, each record linked before it is written and with a fresh id (10b): the Credit steps in the plan's order (the 8e-2 writer), the Deposit applications in the plan's order (`app.post_deposit_application`, order numbers after the Settlement's last, the draft's reason or the step 5 staff reason, as 8e-3), and the Deposit Refunds in Deposit id order (new writer below). It sets nothing IMMEDIATE (the variant does at its end) and returns the ids and `credit_left`; the variant refunds the released Credit that is left (10c-2a-3) and names every record in its audit event.
      - 8e-2 writer reused with a correction link (Q-42 answer 1): `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application` also takes a `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft: the step from `plan`, the Credit through `app.settlement_correction_credit_id`, a fresh id linked to the correction (`customer_credit_application_id`) before it is written (its derived id stays for the Settlement). Its source check accepts such a row only when it is linked to a correction of this Settlement confirmed in this transaction, by that correction's approver under `financial.approve`, not before the correction, with a matching Credit step of that correction's plan, and before any Deposit application or Refund of the same correction; its deferred check pins the row to the step its detail names (once per step). Every other Credit trigger runs unchanged; the one-call reversal stays refused (8e-2).
      - Deposit applications (relaxing): the closed-Settlement refusal and the "after the Settlement Refunds" refusal of `app.validate_deposit_application` pass a row linked to a correction of this Settlement confirmed in this transaction, written by that correction's approver, not before the correction, and before any Refund of the same correction; the DL-032 stage order is checked within that correction (the order numbers still follow the Settlement's last). Every other check stays (DL-032 path, pet damage, amounts, chronology).
      - Deposit Refunds (Q-41 answer 3): `app.settlement_correction_refund_details` gains `deposit_id` and `signer_agreement` (nullable, the 8d limits; an agreement only with a Deposit); a new internal writer `app.post_settlement_correction_deposit_refund` writes the details row and the Refund (`financial.approve`, processed by the approver, as 8d); the 8d source check (`app.assert_final_settlement_refund_source`) hands a Refund with such a details row to a new check that accepts only the Refund of a correction confirmed in this transaction, linked to it, processed by its approver, matching a `DEPOSIT_REFUNDS` step of that correction's plan, at most one per Deposit per correction, with the 8d rules (DL-032 path, no waiting receipt reversal or forfeiture draft, active method, BR-REFUND-015 recipient, held amount, chronology, no open debt a Deposit could pay). `app.settlement_correction_refund_allowed` (Credit Refunds) also needs a details row without a Deposit. The one-call Refund corrections already refuse it (8d).
      - Not changed here: the 10c-1b recorder and confirm (10c-2a-3), the receipt reversal recorder (a correction draft that waits is protected by the pending-draft rule and the stale check; a tightening is not needed), the Customer Ledger and Deposit history (they already show Credit applications, Deposit applications and Deposit Refunds).
      - Tests (new module `test_settlement_correction_plan_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): in a hand-built correction after a Deposit application reversal (10c-2a-1): Credit first, a Deposit application and the Deposit Refund of the rest (every record linked, the Settlement row unchanged, the Deposit empty, both ledgers reconciled); a Refund to another Signer with the agreement; the plan check refusals (open Settlement, R1, R2, a Deposit Refund while debt remains, pet damage first, (4a), a Deposit not emptied, a Deposit that holds nothing (a kept Deposit holds nothing after closure; a kept Deposit an overlay gives back is checked on the real state at the confirm, which 10d tests), a pending draft, mixed Refund details, malformed overlay and plan); a key of released Payment value resolved after a linked credit Adjustment; a stale confirm; the relaxed checks accept nothing else (an unlinked Credit application, Deposit application or Deposit Refund after closure, another Settlement's correction, another approver, a Refund that matches no step, a second Refund of a Deposit in one correction); least privilege and forward-only.
      - As built after review round 1 (independent and security): a correction's Deposit application matches a Deposit step of its plan, once; `settlement_correction_refund_details` is unique on (correction, Deposit) and its deferred check needs the details to name the Refund's own Deposit; the step checks READ COMMITTED, takes its locks again and compares the whole recorded result; added tests: two Signers (BR-CREDIT-008 per Customer, a Refund to the other signed Signer with the agreement), a Late-payment Charge target, another Settlement's correction, the deferred Credit "once" check, the stage order within a correction, the Refund deny cases and the Credit Refund details check. Notes for the writers (10c-2a-3, 10e): the open-debt rule of a correction's Deposit Refund holds at its insert, so the step stays the variant's last writer of Lease debt (a later `EXTRA_CHARGE` in the same correction, as 10e plans for a paid Settlement Refund, needs a deferred re-check or comes before the step); the step's Deposit applications are dated by `app.post_deposit_application` with the clock, not a microsecond after the target's latest event (as 8e-3; slice 12 item); the Credit-before-Deposits rule locks only the steps' Credits (docs/010 choice 16).
      - As built after review round 2 (owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-43, option (a)): the lock function locks the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` right after the two draft locks, as the Settlement confirm (the move-out inspection command that supersedes an inspection locks the same row `FOR UPDATE`); added tests: an inspection correction waits while the step runs, and after it the Pet Deposit cannot pay the line as pet damage; the step refused for another person inside a live correction and at REPEATABLE READ; a BR-DEP-027 termination (staff reason refused, the Pet Deposit's rest refunded while other debt stays open); BR-CREDIT-006 after closure (Rent-first refusal, the designation and proposal bases); the DL-032 order, Deposit type and non-pet line refusals; every recorded result field in the stale test. Choice 5 confirmed by the owner, choice 16 a known limit (docs/010). Notes for the writers (10c-2a-3, 10c-2b, 10d), from the round-2 notes: a variant writes no linked Deposit application, Credit application or Deposit Refund outside the step (or adds a deferred check that the step ran); it takes the two draft locks, then the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` in the 8e-3 order (never `FOR SHARE` first; 10c-1b as committed takes no Lease lock, and 10c-2a-3 adds it when 10c-1b uses the step, owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-07-44), then calls the lock function before `app.begin_settlement_correction` and takes no lock of its own after the Lease row except its Refund method `FOR SHARE`, last (as amended by 10c-2a-3 security review round 2: it extends `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan` for its variant, as the `REFUND` branch does, so that its sources' Payments and Credits join the single id-ordered Payment and Credit passes and its corrected Invoice joins the Invoice pass; a separate pass of its own could lock Payments out of id order and deadlock with another Lease's correction or Settlement confirm); its audit event names every record the step writes (the Refund audit names neither the Settlement nor the correction).
      - Owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-44 (2026-10-07; docs/010), for the writers: (3) a correction that uses the rest of the Pet Deposit for other debt at a BR-DEP-027 termination needs a new Customer choice recorded for that correction (the recorder asks for it and stores it in the draft's `pet_remainder`); it never reuses the Settlement's choice; without it the rest of the Pet Deposit is not used for other debt; (4) when 10c-1b uses the step in 10c-2a-3, its confirm takes the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` right after the two draft locks (the 8e-3 order), with a test, and the lock function's comment and this note are updated. Round-3 optional test hardening, carried into 10c-2a-3 with its own runs: a case where only the step takes the lock function; a correction Credit application with a wrong basis refused (23514); the stale test checks that it covers every result key; `blocked_by` looks for the Lease row lock in `pg_locks` with a longer start deadline.
    - 10c-2a-3 pre-code design (2026-10-07, read only; head `20261106_102`; DL-032, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-008, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-DEP-027, BR-REFUND-014, BR-REFUND-015, BR-AUTH-010; owner answers 1 to 3 to Q-41, 1 and 2 to Q-42, 3 and 4 to Q-44). The `REFUND` correction of 10c-1b (`app.record_settlement_refund_draft`, `app.confirm_settlement_refund`, migration 100) is extended in place (same signatures, `CREATE OR REPLACE`); next migration `20261107_103`. STOPPED before code for Q-2026-10-07-46 (point 1 below).
      - Recorder: the refund input gains `plan` (the Settlement plan format of 10c-2a-2: `steps` and `pet_remainder`; staff choose every step, nothing is inferred). The 8a-1 refusal of a Deposit-paid Invoice is lifted for this recorder only (10c-1a keeps it). The release takes whole sources newest first across Deposit applications, Payment allocations and Credit applications (Q-41 answer 2) until the paid part is covered; each released source records its kind, Deposit, Payment or Credit and released amount. The recorder builds the overlay itself from the release (the line's amount owed after the release and the Adjustment; each released Payment allocation as a released-Credit key of the Invoice Customer; each Credit that gets value back; each Deposit with the value it gets back) and stores `plan`, `plan_overlay` and `plan_result` (10c-2a-2). Every `DEPOSIT_REFUNDS` step uses the draft's Refund method and note (one method and note per correction). A step 5 at a BR-DEP-027 termination needs `pet_remainder` with basis `CUSTOMER_CHOICE` and a Customer choice given for this correction (Q-44 answer 3; the recorder never copies the Settlement's choice; without it the plan check refuses the step). A partly released Deposit application is reversed whole; its rest is paid again only as a Deposit step of the plan (or by Credit first, as the plan check requires). An empty plan is valid when nothing must be paid (a Customer with no open debt on the Lease and no Deposit holding money: the Refund as today, Q-42 answer 2).
      - Confirm: the draft lock, the Settlement draft lock, then the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` (Q-44 answer 4; the 8e-3 order), the source Payments and Credits in id order, then `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan` (the steps' Payments and Credits, the Lease's Charges and Invoices, the Settlement row, each Deposit's draft lock and row) (as amended by security review rounds 1 and 2: no source pass of its own; after the Lease row every lock comes from the lock function, which takes the released sources' Payments and Credits in its single id-ordered passes and the corrected Invoice `FOR UPDATE` after the Charges; only the Refund method `FOR SHARE` comes last); the stale checks (source revision and the recorder's release); `app.begin_settlement_correction`; the release, each record linked before it is written: the Deposit application reversals (10c-2a-1, dated after the latest events of the Deposit and the target), the allocation and Credit application reversals, the credit Adjustment, the re-posted rests of allocations and Credit applications (migration 67); the Invoice status refresh; then `app.post_settlement_correction_plan` (Credit first, Deposit applications, Deposit Refunds); then one Credit Refund per released Payment allocation or Credit application for the value left in its Credit after the step, at most what this correction released to it (Q-42 answer 2; none when nothing is left); `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE`; audit events (`invoice.adjusted`, and `final_settlement.correction.confirmed` naming every reversal, re-post, released Credit, the step's Credit applications, Deposit applications and Deposit Refunds, and the Credit Refunds).
      - Tests (new module `test_settlement_deposit_refund_correction_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`): a Deposit-paid line refunded (Deposit application reversal, Adjustment, the released Deposit value refunded to the Primary Tenant; a Refund to another signed Signer with the agreement); released Deposit value paying open debt first (Credit first, then the Deposit) with only the rest refunded; a mixed line (Payment and Deposit) released newest first; Q-42 answer 2 both cases: the released Credit pays its Customer's open debt first and only the rest is refunded, and a third-party payer with no open debt on the Lease gets the Refund as in 10c-1b; a BR-DEP-027 termination needs a new Customer choice for step 5; a partly released Deposit application re-applied by a plan step; stale, READ COMMITTED, Staff cannot confirm, replay; the 10c-1b confirm holds the Lease row lock (a second connection's `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` gets 55P03) and the source order (Lease lock before the first Payment lock); the round-3 hardening carried from 10c-2a-2. 10c-1b tests whose released Credit's Customer still owes on the Lease change (narrowed expectations, docs/010 note), after a read-only search.
      - Owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-46 ((a), 2026-10-07; docs/010): the 8a-1 refusal of an Invoice Adjustment lets through only an Adjustment linked to a correction of that Invoice's own Settlement, confirmed in this transaction by its approver; the release stays newest first; no line is paid more than its new amount (already enforced at commit: `app.validate_invoice_adjustment_effect` refuses when `app.invoice_line_outstanding_at` at the Adjustment's time is below zero, and that value counts every Deposit application not reversed (migrations 41, 86, 101); a test shows it on the new path); Deposit value is freed only by the correction's own Deposit application reversal and never becomes Customer Credit (the release writes Credit only for released Payment allocations, as migration 67); tests for an older Deposit application on the same line that the release does not reach, a Deposit application on another line of the same Invoice, and an unlinked Adjustment on such an Invoice still refused.
      - STOPPED again before code (Q-2026-10-07-47): six 10c-1b tests confirm Refund corrections on a Settlement closed with the owner-written `close()` helper, which leaves both Deposits holding money (10,000 and 20,000) and the Invoice Customer owing both Damage lines (25,000); under owner answer 2 to Q-42 (released Credit first pays its Customer's open debt) and the step's rules (BR-SETTLE-008: every DL-032 Deposit holding money is applied or refunded; Credit before Deposits), each of those Refund corrections would be refused, so the 10c-1b test fixtures need more than one changed expectation.
      - Open (Q-2026-10-07-46, answered): (1) the 8a-1 refusal as built (`app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change`, relaxed in 10c-2a-1) refuses an Adjustment while any Deposit application of the Invoice, on any line, is not reversed; the newest-first release of Q-41 answer 2 does not reverse a line's older Deposit applications, nor applications on other lines of the same Invoice, so the correction's credit Adjustment would be refused (55000) in those cases; this plan entry (10c-2a, "the 8a-1 refusal passes it only when every Deposit application of the line is reversed in the same correction") and Q-41 answer 2 cannot both hold.
      - As built (migration `20261107_103`; owner answers to Q-2026-10-07-46 and Q-2026-10-07-47): as designed above. The recorder and confirm of 10c-1b are replaced in place; `app.settlement_refund_line_sources` lists Deposit applications too (newest first by recorded time, id as tie-break) and the release stops once the paid part is covered; a Deposit application is reversed whole and its rest is a plain open obligation for the plan; the new internal `app.settlement_refund_overlay` builds the overlay from the release and `app.settlement_correction_adjustment_allowed` is the Q-46 exception in `app.refuse_deposit_applied_obligation_change` (no new overpayment check: `app.validate_invoice_adjustment_effect` already refuses it, shown by a test). The confirm takes the Lease row `FOR UPDATE` right after the two draft locks (test: a second connection's `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` gets 55P03) and then every other lock through `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan`, patched in migration 103 (same signature) so that for a `REFUND` draft it takes the released sources' Payments and Credits in its single id-ordered passes with the Credit steps' (security review round 2) and the corrected Invoice `FOR UPDATE` in its Invoice pass after the Charges (security review round 1); tests: the confirm's source order, the lock function alone on an empty-plan draft (55P03 on the released Payment and on `FOR KEY SHARE` of the Invoice), and a two-connection test in which the confirm waits on a step Credit's Payment without holding the Invoice. Review round 1 also added a mixed-line test (a Payment allocation and a Deposit application released newest first; the released Credit pays before the Deposit; a Deposit-first plan refused) and refuses a remaining Pet Deposit choice reused from an earlier correction (record time, text only). The owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-48 added two tests of a Refund that includes an unpaid part (a charge partly paid after closure; a Deposit-paid line that still owes), each amount checked to the cent; committed as fe59f0e. The service command gains `steps` and `pet_remainder`; the request carries `plan`. The six 10c-1b tests are rebuilt on a real closure (owner answer to Q-47; listed in docs/010); the 10c-2a-2 round-3 hardening is carried (`blocked_by` on the Lease row, 10 seconds; the stale test covers every result key). Runs: U1, U2 3589 passed; P1 2 failed and P2 3 failed (cause A test fixture, two corrections; cause B test, one correction; cause C a host clock ahead of the database container clock while the Mac display slept, no change), P3 1928 passed; after review round 1: U3 3589 passed, P4 1 failed (cause D, the round-1 `failure` helper rolled back before the deferred checks; one correction), P5 1929 passed; after review round 2: see the review record. Record: ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10c-2a-3-deposit-refund-correction.md.
    - **10c-2a-4 — inspection supersede refused after closure** (owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-07-44, approved tightening; after 10c-2a-3; STOPPED before code, 2026-10-08, Q-2026-10-08-49: the committed 10b Damage extra charge records damage found after closure by superseding the inspection after closure): a supersede of the move-out inspection of a Lease whose Final Settlement is closed is refused (a conflict, P5S01, before anything is written; the inspection command and its insert trigger); tests: refused after closure, accepted before closure, the refusal writes nothing; the 10c-2a-2 inspection test changes (a supersede after closure is now refused; its lock part keeps a Settlement still open or moves to a Lease without a closed Settlement) with a docs/010 note. Which correction kind fixes a wrong pet-damage line after closure: (a) the Damage charge itself is wrong (no damage, or a wrong amount): `CHARGE_REDUCTION` (10c-1a) for an unpaid line, `REFUND` (10c-1b, and 10c-2a-3 for a Deposit-paid line; the released Pet Deposit value then goes through the shared step) for a paid line, `EXTRA_CHARGE` (10b) for damage billed too low; (b) the charge is right but its pet classification is wrong (the finding was or was not caused by the pet): no kind fits; every kind changes the charge, and an inspection supersede cannot be used, because it makes every line billed from the superseded inspection no longer pet damage (`app.invoice_line_is_pet_damage` reads only the current inspection's findings). Q-2026-10-07-45 asked the owner; answered (a), sub-slice 10c-2a-5.
      - Owner answer to Q-2026-10-08-49 (2026-10-08; option (a); docs/010): every supersede of a move-out inspection on a Lease whose Final Settlement is closed is refused (a conflict); damage found after closure is a new, separate inspection that supersedes nothing; a wrong finding on the inspection the Settlement relied on is fixed only by a correction (10c-2a-5 for a pet classification); a mistake in an inspection recorded after closure cannot be fixed by a supersede either (known limit; the wrong finding is simply not billed); the committed 10b test changes its set-up, keeps its name and proves the same behaviour. Read-only check before code (answer point 5), none found: "current" is decided per inspection (no other inspection supersedes it) by the Damage source check (migration 65), `app.invoice_line_is_pet_damage` (migration 86; used by migrations 89, 91 and 102), the extra charge plan and source state (migration 98), the inspection snapshot and page read (migration 60); the inspection service and the frontend pass only ids.
      - Pre-code design (2026-10-08, read only; head `20261107_103`; BR-MOVEOUT-006, BR-SETTLE-012, BR-SETTLE-013; independent and security review). STOPPED before code for Q-2026-10-08-50 (the 10c-2a-2 test change above cannot be built as written: the step runs only after closure). Migration `20261108_104`:
        - `app.move_out_inspection_record` (same signature, `CREATE OR REPLACE`, so owner, SECURITY DEFINER, settings and grant stay): after the Lease row lock `FOR UPDATE` and the existing supersede source checks, a command with `supersedes_inspection_id` reads the Lease's Final Settlement row `FOR SHARE` (the 8e-1 refusal's pattern; lock order Lease row, then Settlement row, as the 8e-3 confirm and the correction lock function) and, when it is closed, refuses with P5S01 "A move-out inspection of a Lease with a closed Final Settlement cannot be superseded" before anything is written. An inspection that supersedes nothing is not checked. The exact-repeat replay stays first (it writes nothing).
        - `app.validate_move_out_inspection_source` (BEFORE INSERT trigger, `CREATE OR REPLACE`): the same refusal after its Lease row lock, for a row with `supersedes_inspection_id`.

        - `app.post_settlement_correction_plan` (same signature; exact-text patch through `pg_get_functiondef`, so owner, SECURITY DEFINER, settings and privileges stay): right after its `app.lock_settlement_correction_plan(p_draft)` call and before it reads the plan and compares the result, when the draft's `plan.pet_remainder.basis` is `CUSTOMER_CHOICE`, it refuses (55000, the recorders' messages) a choice text equal to the confirmed Final Settlement draft's choice ("the Final Settlement's choice is not reused") or to the choice of any other confirmed `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft of the same Settlement ("an earlier correction's choice is not reused"). Every variant takes the Settlement's draft lock before the step, so two confirms on one Settlement serialize; the current draft's own CONFIRMED decision (written by `app.begin_settlement_correction` before the step) is excluded by its id.
