    - **10e-3c — the Q-2026-10-12-96 answer** (owner answer (a), 2026-10-13: "undo the newest debt payments first, then put back the part that was not mistaken money, so only the mistaken amount comes back"; small sub-slice before 10h, placed right after 10e-4): when the uses of the returned value are more than the returned amount and the Refunds paid out less than it, the Refunds are charged back whole (the 10e-3b share rule gives each its whole net), the newest Deposit or Credit applications of the uses are reversed whole until the rest of the returned amount is reached, and the part of the last reversed application that was not returned money is applied again to the same obligation from the same Credit or Deposit in the same correction (a new linked application). In the Q-96 example the $500 Refund is charged back, the $1,500 Credit application reversed and $300 applied again, so the Customer owes the $500 and $1,200 of the charge: exactly the mistaken $1,700. Lifts the 10e-3b refusal "The returned money also paid debt beyond what its Refunds paid out; reversing it waits for the owner"; the committed test of that refusal changes under the standing rule. Its pre-code design is written when it is built.
      - **10e-3c pre-code design** (2026-10-13, read only; head `20261201_127`, the 10e-3 and 10e-3b functions read from a throwaway database's catalog at `20261130_126`, unchanged by 127; migration `20261202_128` planned; BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-12-96). No owner question.
        - What exists: `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state` lists the uses of the returned value (the Credit or Deposit applications and Refunds that the earlier `REFUND_RETURN` correction, or a later `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` naming it, paid from its source), the applications newest first; its `shares` already give each Refund its whole net when the Refunds paid out less than the returned amount. `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed` refuses that case ("The returned money also paid debt beyond what its Refunds paid out; reversing it waits for the owner"). The confirm `app.confirm_settlement_return_reversal` reverses the listed Credit applications (linked `credit_application_reversal_id`), then the Deposit applications (`app.write_correction_named_deposit_application_reversals`), then writes the recoveries (`app.write_payment_return_recoveries`), then the return's `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row. Every use is a correction-linked application written by the shared DL-032 step, so each targets one Invoice line (one `customer_credit_application_details` row for a Credit) or one Late-payment Charge.
        - Source (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`): when the uses are more than the returned amount and the Refunds paid out less than it, the rest is the returned amount less the Refunds' nets; the application lists keep, newest first, only the applications whose newer applications sum to less than the rest (each reversed whole); a new key `reapply` (empty otherwise) holds one entry when the last kept application is more than what the rest still needs: its source (Credit or Deposit), the reversed application, its target kind, Invoice, line or Late-payment Charge, and the amount that was not returned money (the kept applications' total less the rest). Every other case keeps today's output plus `reapply: []`.
        - Refusal (exact-text patch of `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed`): the Q-96 refusal is removed; the shared-path refusal of a Refund of the uses with its own return stays for both cases; new fail-closed checks: `reapply` must be a list of at most one entry, its amount above 0 and below the reversed application's amount, its application among the listed ones (55000).
        - Write (exact-text patch of the confirm): right after the `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row (the source then holds exactly the re-applied amount), the `reapply` entry is applied again to the same target from the same source in the same correction, linked before it is written (`customer_credit_application_id` or `deposit_application_id`), dated after the reversal: a Credit through `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application` reading a one-step `plan` that the recorder stores in the draft payload (the `CUSTOMER_CREDIT` step format of 10c-2a-2: Credit, target kind and line or Charge, amount), a Deposit through `app.post_deposit_application` with the reversed application's target and stage, numbered after the Settlement's highest application number (a new record; owner-accepted 2026-10-13, replacing "target and order"). Build check: if the Credit writer's basis rules refuse such a one-line restoration, a small internal writer copies the reversed application's basis and designation instead (owner answer (a): "a small new writer"), with no grant. The 8a-1 and 8e-2 refusals pass by the link, as for the shared step. The result lists the re-applied application.
        - Not changed: the recorder's shape (it stores the source, now with `reapply`, and the derived `plan`), the lock function (the re-applied target and source are among the reversed applications' targets and the source it already locks), the recovery writer, the left values (sub-slice 10e-3d), the service.
        - Tests (in `test_settlement_return_reversal_migration.py`), each on the real path, to the cent: the owner's Q-96 Credit example ($300 return only X1; $1,700 return X2 with a new Refund after a $1,500 charge: the Credit applies $1,500 and refunds $500; the reversal of X2 charges the $500 Refund back, reverses the $1,500 application and applies $300 again to the same line: the Customer owes $500 and $1,200 of the charge, the Credit holds 0, each record linked, the ledger reconciles); the Deposit form of the same shape; two applications, the newest reversed whole and the older one partly re-applied; refusals (a Refund of the uses with its own return; a hand-built `reapply` outside the listed applications or of the whole application); a re-applied application written outside its correction is refused by the 8a-1 guard; least privilege and forward-only of migration 128. Committed tests changed under the standing rule (owner answer (a) to Q-96 ends that refusal): `test_returned_money_that_also_paid_debt_beyond_its_refunds_waits_for_the_owner` (becomes the allowed case; renamed with its traceability entry) and the Q-96 branch of `test_the_shared_path_refusals_fail_closed`; the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261202_128` (the 10e-4 module's `REVISION` names the migration it tests and stays, as `PAYBACK_REVISION` did).
      - 10e-3c as built (migration `20261202_128`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3c-return-reversal-reapply.md; docs/004 DB-SETTLE-005 paragraph, docs/010): as designed above. The Credit part goes through the existing Settlement Credit writer (step 1 of the stored one-step plan; its basis rules accept the restoration, so the fallback writer was not built); the Deposit part keeps the reversed application's stage and target and is numbered after the Settlement's highest application number (the design's "order"). The result and the audit event gain `reapplied`. With the review round 1 and 2 changes: the part must be a listed application of the returned Refund's own Credit or Deposit, with the same Credit or Deposit, obligation kind and (for a Late-payment Charge or a Deposit line) target, and may appear only in the Q-96 case; the reversal brings back exactly the returned amount when the uses are more than it; the confirm accepts only the plan built from its source, a payload with exactly the recorder's keys and the Settlement's Lease; the audit event is dated at the latest record (tested). Known limits (docs/004): a Credit part on a Rent or Service Invoice without designation, if the Rent-first proposal no longer gives its line; a Deposit part whose Deposit left the DL-032 path; a Credit whose Payment is no longer received; the Deposit writer dates by the clock only. Lock note (security review): the re-applied target's Invoice lines are locked by the Credit writer after the Invoice row, as by every other writer; no new lock cycle. Tests: the owner's Credit example (with crafted drafts refused), its Deposit form, two applications in three amounts (one at the exact boundary), the built-source refusals, least privilege and forward-only of 128; three earlier tests made stricter; the committed Q-96 test renamed. Next in the order: 10e-3d, 10c-2a-7, 10c-3c-1, 10c-3c-2, 10f, 10g, 10e-2d, 10e-2b-3, 10e-2b-4, then 10h.
    - **10e-3d — the Q-2026-10-12-97 answer** (owner answer (a), 2026-10-13: one fix for every case, so a later reversal always charges the right person; placed right after 10e-3c; note from the 10e-3c independent review: a part that a `RETURN_REVERSAL` applies again uses money an earlier return-only correction brought back, but it is linked to the reversal correction, so 10e-3d must count it against that earlier correction's left value too): when a later correction's DL-032 step uses a Deposit or Credit, that use counts against the held money of the earlier return-only corrections of that source, oldest return first, so their left values (the label `app.settlement_held_deposit_value` reads, and its Credit counterpart) go down. Then a held-value Refund naming an earlier return-only correction whose money a sweep paid out is refused, and that correction's reversal cannot reach money a later return brought back. The pre-code check names every committed test that reads a left value after such a sweep; an expectation that changes under this answer changes in the affected case only (owner standing rule).
      - Owner detail of 2026-10-13 (answer 1 to the 10e-3c report): process the Settlement's corrections oldest return first; count only uses made by corrections, and keep ordinary Credit spending (outside a correction) as the known limit of owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63; limit a reversal of an earlier return to that return's own left value. The pre-code design is written and committed first. A design choice that changes who pays or how much goes to ops/V1_056_QUESTIONS.md with a recommendation, and that part is not built until the owner answers.
      - **10e-3d pre-code design** (2026-10-13, read only; head `20261202_128`, functions read from a throwaway database's catalog; BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-12-97 and its detail). Split (owner allowed): **10e-3d-1** the count and the reversal limit (migration `20261203_129`), built now; **10e-3d-2** how a reversal charges money another correction paid out, which changes who pays and waited for Q-2026-10-13-100 (owner answers of 2026-10-13: point 1 (a), the reversal charges the paid-out part back to the person who received it and makes the debt that money paid owed again, together exactly the mistaken amount; point 2 (a), a correction of another Settlement of the same Customer that uses the returned Credit counts too, and when a reversal undoes a payment on that other Lease, that Lease's history shows which correction undid it; built next, after the 10e-3d-1 commit).
        - What exists: `app.settlement_held_deposit_value(E, D)` is what the return-only correction E returned into Deposit D (a reversed return counts 0) less the Deposit applications and Refunds from D of the `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` corrections that name E; `app.settlement_held_credit_value(E, C, except)` is E's return into Credit C less the Credit applications from C of E's own step and of the corrections naming E, and less those corrections' Refunds from C, leaving out `except` (`app.settlement_held_credit_left` wraps it). Readers: `app.settlement_held_value_plan` (record and confirm of a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND`: nothing left, 55000 "Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund"; `left_usd_cents`; the Deposit form's `held_left` limit of 10c-2b-4), `app.settlement_held_value_source_state` (the digest), `app.confirm_settlement_held_value_refund` (the Credit Refund cap, its own records left out) and `app.settlement_correction_refund_allowed` (the relaxed Credit Refund check, BR-CREDIT-014). A use by any other correction (a sweep: the shared DL-032 step of a correction that does not name E applies or refunds held DL-032 money, BR-SETTLE-008; a part a `RETURN_REVERSAL` applies again, 10e-3c) does not lower E's left value. The `RETURN_REVERSAL` source (`app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`) undoes only the uses of E and of the corrections naming it (`app.settlement_return_reversal_users`) and compares what the source holds now, so after a sweep the reversal of E is refused ("The returned value is no longer available; undo its other use first") or takes money a later return brought back (the Q-97 case).
        - Count (new internal `app.settlement_returned_value_left(p_settlement uuid, p_kind text, p_source uuid, p_except uuid)`, PL/pgSQL, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=pg_catalog`, no grant; one row per correction of the Settlement with a return into the source: `returned`, `used`, `swept`, `left`). It walks the Settlement's corrections oldest first (`occurred_at`, then id), leaving out `p_except`: (1) a correction's own returns into the source (its linked `VALUE_CORRECTION` rows; a reversed return counts 0) open its entry; (2) its uses of the source are its linked active Credit or Deposit applications from the source and its linked Refunds from the source at their value less their `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` rows (a later return of such a Refund is a return of its own correction, so it is not subtracted); (3) the uses take first from its own entry, then from the entry of the earlier correction it names (a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND`), then from the other open entries, oldest return first; whatever is left is the source's other money and is not counted; (4) what an entry gives to a correction that is neither its own correction nor one naming it is its `swept` part. Only uses made by corrections count; ordinary Credit spending stays the known limit of owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63, and what the source holds still caps every payout. Implementer's reading, not a free choice: a correction's own return is taken first, because the committed 10e-3b and 10e-3c reversal of a correction with a new Refund charges back exactly its own returned amount (its Refunds and step paid out its own returned money first); taking the older return first would count the same money against two returns. Example (the Q-97 case, Pet Deposit): X1 returns 1,000 (return only); X2 returns 500 with a new Refund and refunds all 1,500 the Pet Deposit holds (BR-SETTLE-008): X2's own 500 first, then X1's 1,000, swept. X1 then has 0 left, so a held-value Refund naming X1 is refused (55000, "Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund"); when X3 later brings back 500, a held-value Refund naming X1 is still refused and one naming X3 refunds the 500. The Q-96 case (Credit): X1 returns 300 (return only); X2 returns 1,700 with a new Refund and the Credit pays 1,500 of a charge and refunds 500: X2's own 1,700, then X1's 300, swept; after X2's `RETURN_REVERSAL` (X2's return counts 0, its Refund charged back whole, its application reversed) the 300 the reversal applied again is X1's money, swept by the reversal correction (the 10e-3c independent review note).
        - Patches (10e-3d-1): `app.settlement_held_deposit_value` and `app.settlement_held_credit_value` return the new function's `left` for E (same signatures, owner, definer settings and grants; whole-body replacements, because the bodies change entirely); their readers do not change, and their values go down after a sweep. Reversal limit (owner detail: "Limit a reversal of an earlier return to that return's own left value"): exact-text patches give `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state` the earlier correction's `swept_usd_cents` and make `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed` refuse a positive or missing one (55000, "Another correction paid out this return's money; reversing it waits for the owner", until Q-2026-10-13-100 is answered). Without a sweep the limit already holds: the reversal brings back the uses it undoes, so it takes from the source at most the returned amount less them, which is the left value. A `RETURN_REVERSAL` or `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` draft recorded before revision 129 whose source changes is stale at its confirm (P5S02, the existing mapping); staff reject it and record it again.
        - Committed tests that change (owner standing rule of 2026-10-11; the pre-code check of every test that reads a left value): `test_settlement_held_deposit_limit_migration.py::test_a_correction_that_names_no_earlier_correction_still_settles_held_money` shows the known limit that ends (after the sweep Ed's left value is 0, not 2,000; naming Ed is refused with "Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund" instead of "The Deposit holds no returned money to refund"; after E3's 500 a correction naming Ed is refused and one naming E3 refunds the 500); its name stays true, its docstring and traceability note follow. `test_settlement_return_reversal_migration.py::test_the_database_guards_and_a_reversal_of_uses_beyond_the_return`: the last refusal's cause becomes "Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund" (X2's Refund paid out X1's 1,000). The 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261203_129`. No other committed test reads a left value after a sweep (the held-value Refund module, the other held-Deposit limit tests, the real-path Q-65 tests and the pay-back module use only corrections that name the earlier correction), and no committed reversal test reverses a return whose money a sweep took (they reverse a correction with a new Refund, whose own uses take its own money first, or a return-only correction used only by corrections naming it).
        - Tests (new module `test_settlement_returned_value_left_migration.py`, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`), on the real path, to the cent: the Q-97 Deposit case above (left values, the refusals, X3 refunded by its own correction, X1's reversal refused with the new message and nothing written); the Credit form with the Q-96 shape (X1's left value 0 after X2, also through the relaxed Credit Refund check; after X2's reversal the part applied again is X1's swept money and X1's reversal is refused); ordinary Credit spending lowers what the Credit holds, not the left value; a `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` Credit cap still leaves out its own records; least privilege (no grant on the new function, definer settings) and forward-only (only the listed functions change). Unit tests: none expected (55000 is a conflict through the existing mapping).
      - 10e-3d-1 as built (migration `20261203_129`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-1-returned-value-count.md; docs/004 DB-SETTLE-005 paragraph, docs/010): as designed, with the review round 1 change to the count: each correction counts its net draw at its own time (what it paid out: applications, Refunds and reversed returns; less what it brought back: its reversals of applications from the source and its recoveries by a charge; never below 0), so a `REFUND` correction that releases an application and refunds the released part is not a new use; a `RETURN_REVERSAL` names its earlier correction, so its return reversal empties that entry (the design's "a reversed return counts 0" gives the same left values). A missing or non-number `swept_usd_cents` is refused as an incomplete source; any value other than 0 is refused with the Q-100 message. Review round 2 (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-101): a correction's own Deposit receipts and forfeiture reversals count as brought back, its receipt reversals and forfeitures as paid out, and its reversal of the Credit takes all the Credit still holds; the source also names `left_usd_cents`, and the check refuses a reversal that would take more than the return's left value (for example after a Refund of the uses came back through a later return). Known limits named in docs/004: ordinary Credit spending (Q-2026-10-09-63) and, until 10e-3d-2, other Settlements' corrections. Review round 3 (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-102): "own return first" shows on the real path (a return-only Credit correction whose own step pays debt while the Credit also holds an earlier return) and is tested; the Credit reversal of a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` and the receipt reversal of a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` after a return are tested on the real path; the right receipt, forfeiture and forfeiture reversal terms have no real-path test yet (slice 12 item). Not built as planned tests: "through the relaxed Credit Refund check" after a sweep and "X3 refunded by its own correction" in the new module (the second is in the changed committed held-Deposit test) and the Credit cap leaving out its own records (committed tests 1 and the third-party test run unchanged). Tests: nine in the new module (the Q-97 case, the Q-96 case with the part applied again, released money and ordinary spending with the Credit-empty refusal, the order rules, the reversal limited to the left value, own return first, the Credit reversal and the receipt reversal, the fail-closed checks, least privilege and forward-only).
      - **10e-3d-2 pre-code design** (2026-10-13, read only; head `20261203_129`, the 10e-3, 10e-3b, 10e-3c and 10e-3d-1 functions read from migrations 125, 126, 128 and 129; BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, BR-REFUND-015, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-002; owner answers (a) and (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100). Split (owner allowed): **10e-3d-2a** (migration `20261204_130` planned) undoes, in the reversal of an earlier return-only correction E, the part of E's money that another correction S of the same Settlement paid out through a Refund or an application still active and unchanged; **10e-3d-2b** follows a record a later correction changed (an application reversed by a `RETURN_REVERSAL` that applied part of it again, or released by a `REFUND` correction that wrote its rest again) to its successor; **10e-3d-2c** the cases Q-2026-10-13-103 asks about (money that left the source through a Credit reversal, a Deposit receipt reversal or a forfeiture); **10e-3d-2d** owner answer (a) to point 2 (the corrections of every Settlement in the Credit's count, and the undo on another Lease with that Lease's history). Until its sub-slice is built, each case stays refused (55000) with its own message, so nothing is undone half.
        - What exists (after 10e-3d-1): `app.settlement_returned_value_left` gives each return's `returned`, `used`, `swept` and `left`; a correction S's net draw takes from its own return first, then from the correction it names, then from the other returns, oldest first. `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state(settlement, E)` lists the uses of E's own correction and of the `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` corrections naming E (`users`): their active applications from E's Credit or Deposit (newest first) and their Refunds from it with a net (`recoveries`, each with its share, 10e-3b); `uses` totals; `reapply` (at most one entry, 10e-3c); `swept_usd_cents` and `left_usd_cents` (10e-3d-1). `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed` refuses a positive `swept_usd_cents` and a reversal that takes more than `left_usd_cents`. The confirm writes, in order, the Credit application reversals, the Deposit application reversals (`app.write_correction_named_deposit_application_reversals`), the recoveries (`app.write_payment_return_recoveries`, a share only inside a `RETURN_REVERSAL`), the `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row, then the part applied again; it reads the stored source lists, so a record placed in them is undone by the existing writers.
        - Attribution (owner answer (a) to point 1, with the rule of Q-2026-10-10-76 and Q-2026-10-12-95 that a new Refund pays the returned money first): inside S, what S paid out is taken in this order: first the money S brought back itself (released first, as the count), then S's Refunds from the source, the oldest first, then S's applications from the source, the newest first. Laid over S's draw in the count's order (S's own return, the correction S names, then the other returns, oldest return first), E's part of S's draw falls on known records: a Refund part is charged back to the person who received that Refund (a 10e-3b share, `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` linked to E's reversal, the 10e-1c charge: this Lease when that person is its signed Customer, else no Lease); an application part makes that debt owed again (the application reversed whole, and the part that was not E's money applied again to the same target from the same source, linked, as 10e-3c). In the owner's example (Pet Deposit; X1 brings back 1,000, X2 brings back 500, applies 600 to Rent and refunds 900): X2's Refund pays X2's own 500 and 400 of X1's money, its Rent application the other 600; X1's reversal charges 400 to the Refund's recipient and reverses the 600 application (600 of Rent owed again), and the Pet Deposit loses exactly X1's left value (0).
        - Count (whole-body replacement of `app.settlement_returned_value_left`, same signature and settings): the walk also returns, for each entry, the record parts it gave to other corrections: a new internal `app.settlement_returned_value_takes(p_settlement uuid, p_kind text, p_source uuid, p_earlier uuid)` (STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=pg_catalog`, no grant) returns one row per record of another correction that paid out part of E's money: the correction, the record kind (`REFUND`, `CREDIT_APPLICATION`, `DEPOSIT_APPLICATION`, or the kind of a case 10e-3d-2a does not undo: `CREDIT_REVERSAL`, `RECEIPT_REVERSAL`, `FORFEITURE`, `RETURN_REVERSAL`), the record, its amount and the part of it that was E's money; the sum of the parts is E's `swept`. Shared walk code: the left function and the takes function read one internal walk, so they cannot disagree.
        - Source (exact-text patches of `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`): (1) the takes join the lists: Refund parts as recovery entries with their share (`amount_usd_cents`) and net, application parts as listed applications (reversed whole) with a `reapply` entry for the part that was not E's money; (2) the 10e-3b and 10e-3c threshold for the own users' records becomes the part of E's money they used (`used` less `swept`) instead of the whole returned amount, so the own uses and the swept uses together undo exactly E's used money; (3) new key `swept_undone_usd_cents` (the parts placed in the lists) and `swept_open` (the takes of kinds 10e-3d-2a does not undo, each with its kind).
        - Refusal (exact-text patch of `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed`): the refusal of a positive `swept_usd_cents` becomes: `swept_usd_cents` must equal `swept_undone_usd_cents` plus the `swept_open` parts (fail closed), and any `swept_open` entry is refused with a message for its case (for example "A Credit reversal used this return's money; reversing it waits for sub-slice 10e-3d-2c"); a listed Refund part with a return of its own, a listed application reversed since, or a Refund part more than its net is refused ("undo its other use first", as the 10e-3b shared-path refusal); `reapply` may have one entry per reversed application; the left-value limit becomes: what the reversal takes from the source (the returned amount and the parts applied again less the applications and recoveries it undoes) equals at most `left_usd_cents`.
        - Write: no new writer. The confirm and the lock function read the stored lists; the build checks that the lock function's `RETURN_REVERSAL` branch locks every listed application's target and every listed Refund (else an exact-text patch adds them in its id-ordered passes); the part applied again loops over every `reapply` entry (exact-text patch of the confirm and of `app.settlement_return_reversal_reapply_steps`, one step per Credit entry).
        - Tests (10e-3d-2a, on the real path, to the cent): the owner's example (Deposit: 400 charged, 600 of Rent owed again, Pet Deposit 0, every record linked, the ledger reconciles); the Q-97 Deposit case of `test_a_sweep_counts_against_the_earlier_return_and_its_reversal_waits` (X2's Refund paid all of X1's 1,000: charged back 1,000 to its recipient) and the Credit form; an application that paid X1's money and other money on each side (reversed whole, the rest applied again); E's own uses and a sweep in one reversal (two parts applied again); refusals of the open cases and of a Refund part with its own return; least privilege and forward-only. Committed tests that change under the standing rule (owner answer (a) to Q-100 point 1 ends their refusal): in `test_settlement_returned_value_left_migration.py`, `test_a_sweep_counts_against_the_earlier_return_and_its_reversal_waits` (X1's reversal becomes the allowed case; renamed with its traceability entry) and the SWEPT case of `test_the_count_and_the_reversal_limit_fail_closed` (the new fail-closed keys); the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261204_130`. Stay refused in 10e-3d-2a (no test change): `test_a_part_applied_again_counts_against_the_return_whose_money_it_is` (a chain, 10e-3d-2b) and `test_a_payment_return_and_a_receipt_correction_count_what_they_take_from_a_return` (Q-103), each with its new message (an expectation change under the standing rule, named in the report).
        - Point 2 (10e-3d-2d, design detail when built): the walk for a Credit takes the corrections of every Settlement whose records use that Credit, in one time order (a Deposit belongs to one Lease, so its walk does not change); an undo on another Lease B whose Final Settlement is closed passes the 8e-1 and 8e-2 refusals only through a correction of B's Settlement confirmed in the same transaction, so B's history shows it. Recommended record shape (Q-2026-10-13-103 point 3): a companion correction header of B's Settlement written by E's reversal, naming E's reversal correction, with B's records linked to it.
      - Owner answers of 2026-10-13 to Q-2026-10-13-103 (docs/010): point 1 (a), money of a return that left through a bounced Payment or a wrong receipt is charged to the person who received that Refund (sub-slice 10e-3d-2c; a case that does not fit that rule is asked first); point 2 (a), money kept by a forfeiture stays refused, and every forfeiture path is checked again when 10e-2d is built; point 3 (a), a companion correction of the other Lease's Settlement, recorded and confirmed together with the reversal in one step (sub-slice 10e-3d-2d). Order: 10e-3d-2a, 10e-3d-2b, 10e-3d-2c, 10e-3d-2d, 10c-2a-7, 10c-3c-1, 10c-3c-2, 10f, 10g, 10e-2d, 10e-2b-3, 10e-2b-4, then 10h.

Implementer's readings (fail closed): a correction S that also paid out through a reversal of the Credit, a reversed return, a Deposit receipt reversal or a forfeiture is not split (its whole part is one open take of that kind; 10e-3d-2c and 10e-2d decide those cases); a Refund part more than the Refund's net now is open ("undo its other use first"); an application reversed since is open (the 10e-3d-2b message). The own uses' threshold is E's used less its swept part; the "brings back only the returned amount" check compares the own uses' part against it. No lock-function change: its `RETURN_REVERSAL` branch reads the source's lists, so every listed application's target is locked, and the returned Credit or the Lease's Deposits as before; Refunds are not row-locked (as for the own uses: a change to a listed Refund changes the confirm's source digest, so the draft is stale). Review round 1 (independent 5 should-fix, security 1, the same first one): the parts applied again are listed in the DL-032 stage order, then in the order their applications were first written (the Deposit application check refuses a lower stage after a higher one in one correction; a two-stage real-path test needs a Pet Deposit holding more after closure than the open pet-damage debt, which today's fixtures cannot make; implementer's claim for the round 2 reviewer); a Credit part applied again without a Customer designation on the newer of two reversed applications of an Invoice with Rent or Service lines is refused until the owner answers the new Q-2026-10-13-104; when two or more own corrections paid out and one of them also took another return's money (source flag `shared_with_other_returns`), the reversal waits for 10e-3d-2b, which also derives the own uses' parts from the walk (implementer's placement); a real-path test of the own uses' part below the returned amount was added; a Credit-form Refund charge-back and an own part applied again together with a swept part cannot be reached on the real path (implementer's claims for the round 2 reviewer). Review round 2: security clean; independent three should-fix (the two-stage claim and the own-plus-swept claim are refuted, with real-path set-ups in the review record; the Credit-form Refund claim is confirmed; text): STOPPED, not committed (Q-2026-10-13-105). Tests: nine in the new module `test_settlement_return_reversal_swept_migration.py` (the owner's example, scaled to fit the fixture: 2,000 returned, 800 of a Refund charged back and a 1,200 application reversed; an application with other money on each side, with the 10e-3d-2b refusal of the next return after it; two applications each applied again; the own uses and another correction's use undone together; own corrections that also took another return's money wait; a Refund part with its own return refused; the Rent-first refusal on built sources; the fail-closed parts; least privilege and forward-only). Committed tests changed under owner answer (a) to Q-100 (standing rule): the Q-97 test renamed `test_a_sweep_counts_against_the_earlier_return_and_its_reversal_charges_it_back` (the reversal charges 1,000 back), the chain test's and the payment-return-and-receipt test's messages (10e-3d-2b and 10e-3d-2c), the fail-closed test's swept cases (incomplete source) and the new keys in three hand-built sources; the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261204_130`. After review round 2 (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-105, a one-time exception): the two real-path tests the round 2 reviewer showed are added (`test_parts_applied_again_at_two_stages_follow_the_dl_032_order`: a return-only Credit correction's step lets the Pet Deposit pay at the remainder stage with an older return's money and part of E's while the hold rule keeps the rest, and a later pet-damage reclassification pays at the pet-damage stage with E's rest and a newer return's money, so the two parts applied again are written pet-damage stage first although the remainder application is older; `test_an_own_part_and_another_correction_s_part_are_each_applied_again`: a held-value Refund naming E is E's only own correction with records and also took a later return's money, and another correction took part of E's money, so one own part and one part of another correction are applied again; the set-ups were confirmed by a fresh read-only reviewer before the run, under the new run rule); `shared_with_other_returns` must be there as a boolean (five hand-built test sources gain `false`, three of them in committed tests under the owner answer, and the fail-closed test refuses it missing or not a boolean); the confirm's put-back loop refuses a source kind other than a Credit or a Deposit (not reachable: the source writes only those two kinds and the confirm compares the source it recomputes). So an ordinary Credit application (`credits.apply`, no Settlement) and a Settlement's own Credit step can never carry it, and staff cannot choose it.

The plan and the source do not change, so no committed draft or plan expectation changes.

Review round 2 note N3 belongs here too: after another return's reversal changed an own application of E, E's reversal is refused with the left-value message instead of following the change.
        - Who changes a correction's payment after closure (read): only corrections. A Settlement or correction Credit application has no one-call reversal after closure (P5S01, migration 97), and Deposit application reversals are written only by corrections. The correction kinds that reverse such a payment: a `RETURN_REVERSAL` (it reverses the application whole and, when part of it was not the reversed return's money, writes that part again on the same obligation from the same source, linked: the put-back); a `REFUND` correction (it releases part of a Credit application through a credit Adjustment, re-posts the unreleased rest and refunds the released value, migrations 67 and 100); a `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` (it reverses the Deposit applications of the reclassified line and runs the DL-032 step again); a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` or a `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` (they reverse the Credit or the receipt: sub-slice 10e-3d-2c, Q-2026-10-13-103 point 1).
        - **10e-3d-2b-1, a payment a return reversal changed** (owner answer (a) to Q-100 point 1 decides it; no new question): when the take of E's money falls on an application A that a `RETURN_REVERSAL` R reversed and put back as A' (R's source lists A in its `reapply`), E's part follows to A' with the same amount. Reason: R's put-back is A less exactly R's earlier return's part ("the part that was not returned money"), so every other return's part in A is in A' unchanged; the walk does not move it, because R's draw is A' and its reversed return less the reversed A (0 net). The successor is found from R's stored source (its `reapply` entry for A) and R's linked put-back effect at that position (a Credit put-back has the id `app.final_settlement_credit_application_id(R's draft, position)`; a Deposit put-back is R's n-th linked Deposit application effect after its reversals, n the entry's position among the Deposit entries); a successor itself changed by a later return reversal is followed again (a chain), each step checked: A' active, on the same obligation and source as A, and at least the part. E's reversal then reverses A' whole and puts back A' less E's part, exactly as for any other application part. When the successor cannot be found or checked, the part stays open with the 10e-3d-2b message (fail closed). New internal function (planned) `app.settlement_returned_value_successor(p_kind text, p_application uuid)`: the active application now holding A's money that was not a reversed return's, or NULL.
        - **The own uses from the walk** (gap ii and note N3; implementer's reading of Q-100 point 1, no new question): the own uses of E (E's own correction and the corrections naming it) are placed by the same walk as the swept parts: for each such correction U, the walk's take of E's money by U sits in U's draw after what U brought back, on U's Refunds (oldest first) and then on its applications (newest first), each changed application followed to its successor. The pooled own path of sub-slices 10e-3b and 10e-3c (all own Refunds first, then all own applications, newest first, up to "the returned amount" less the swept part) is kept unchanged when at most one own correction paid out from the source and none of its applications was changed, so every committed reversal test keeps its result; otherwise the own parts come from the walk and the flag refusal ends. The source gains `own_parts` (the same shape as `swept`) and the check holds them to the same rules as the swept parts (exact amounts, a Refund part no more than its net and without a return of its own, an application part reversed whole with exactly its rest put back), with the pooled own lists then empty. Implementer's reading to confirm in review: with one own correction the walk and the pooled path place E's money on the same records whenever that correction brought nothing back, and a correction naming E brings nothing back; the targeted run proves the committed results are unchanged.
        - Split (owner allowed): **10e-3d-2b-1a** the successor for the parts of other corrections (gap i for a return reversal's change), built first; **10e-3d-2b-1b** the own uses from the walk (gap ii and note N3), with the successor reused for own applications.
        - Lock function: unchanged (its `RETURN_REVERSAL` branch recomputes the source, so a successor is locked as a listed application and its target as before; a change between the record and the confirm makes the draft stale).
        - Tests (10e-3d-2b-1, on the real path, to the cent): the committed `test_a_part_applied_again_counts_against_the_return_whose_money_it_is` (X1b's reversal after X1's reversal put back the application) becomes the allowed case under owner answer (a) to Q-100 point 1 (expectation change under the standing rule, named in the report; the message test moves to a chain whose successor cannot be checked, built by hand on the refusal function); `test_an_application_with_other_money_on_each_side_is_applied_again` keeps its first half and its second reversal becomes allowed (X1b's 300 follows to the 1,200 put-back, which is reversed whole and 900 put back); `test_own_corrections_that_also_took_another_return_s_money_wait` becomes the allowed case (E's own 1,500 application and H's 300 application and 200 Refund placed per correction by the walk); a chain of two return reversals; the N3 case (an own application of E changed by another return's reversal); least privilege and forward-only. The 27 head-revision assertions move to the next revision.
        - **10e-3d-2b-2, a payment a `REFUND` correction or a pet-damage reclassification changed** (waits for the new Q-2026-10-13-107: where E's money goes when such a correction reverses the payment and pays the money out again; recommendation (a), the order the owner chose in Q-100: what the changer brought back pays out first, on its Refunds first, then on its applications, the newest first). Until answered, such a part stays refused with the 10e-3d-2b message. Owner answer (a) to Q-107 (2026-10-13): the order of the Q-100 answer; 10e-3d-2b-2 can be built.

      - **10e-3d-2b-2 pre-code design** (2026-10-14, read only; head `20261207_133`, the current bodies of `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`, `app.settlement_returned_value_parts`, `app.settlement_returned_value_walk` and `app.settlement_returned_value_successor` read from a throwaway catalog dump; migrations 67, 100, 103 and 105 for the changing corrections' writes; BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-107). Migration `20261208_134` planned.
        - What exists: the walk places a return E's money on the records of the correction S that paid it out (S's draw after what S brought back; S's Refunds the oldest first, then its applications the newest first), so E's part on an application A of S is a known stretch of A: from `GREATEST(before_it,start_at)-before_it` for `part_usd_cents` cents (`app.settlement_returned_value_parts` computes both bounds but returns only the part). When a later correction Q of kind `REFUND` (migrations 67 and 100 for a Credit: A reversed whole, the unreleased rest A'' written again, linked to Q, the released value refunded from the same Credit by Q's Refund; migration 103 for a Deposit: A reversed whole, then the shared DL-032 step applies or refunds the released value) or `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` (migration 105: the line's Deposit applications reversed whole, then the shared DL-032 step) reversed A, `app.settlement_returned_value_successor` gives NULL and the part is open (`CHANGED_APPLICATION`, "... waits for sub-slice 10e-3d-2b"). In the walk Q brought A back whole and paid out its own records from the same source after it (Q's Refunds the oldest first, then its applications the newest first), so Q's record stream from position 0 is exactly the money Q took back out.
        - Rule (owner answer (a) to Q-107): the money Q took back out keeps its order: Q's reversals of applications from the source, in the order Q wrote them (effect order), one after the other, are the stream Q took back out; E's stretch of A sits in it at A's start plus E's offset in A; laid over Q's record stream, each Q record it overlaps holds that part of E's money. Q-107's example: E's 1,000 the first 1,000 of a 1,500 payment, Q refunds 300 and writes 1,200 again: 300 on Q's Refund (charged back to its recipient) and 700 on the 1,200 payment (reversed whole, 500 put back, 700 of the charge owed again); E's 1,000 the last 1,000: all on the 1,200 payment (reversed whole, 200 put back).
        - New internal `app.settlement_returned_value_placed(p_settlement,p_kind,p_source,p_earlier,p_swept)` (STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=pg_catalog`, no grant): the body of `app.settlement_returned_value_parts` with one more column, `offset_usd_cents` (E's offset in the record; 0 for the open takes of a correction that also reversed the Credit, a return or a receipt, or forfeited); `app.settlement_returned_value_parts` becomes a call to it without the column (same signature, settings and privileges), so the takes and the parts cannot disagree with it.
        - New internal `app.settlement_returned_value_follow(p_settlement uuid,p_kind text,p_source uuid,p_record_kind text,p_record uuid,p_offset bigint,p_part bigint)` (PL/pgSQL, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=pg_catalog`, no grant), one row per record that holds the part now (correction, record kind, record, amount, part, open kind): an active application holds it itself (open when the stretch is not inside it); an application X a `RETURN_REVERSAL` R changed follows, one step at a time, to R's put-back for X (the n-th `reapply` entry of R's stored source is R's n-th linked application, with the checks of `app.settlement_returned_value_successor`), with the same part and the offset less the stretches of R's reversed return E_R on X that lie before it (implementer's reading of "keeps its place": the put-back is X with exactly E_R's stretch cut out, and the walk still places E_R's stretch on X, because a reversed return keeps its entry; E_R's stretches on X come from `app.settlement_returned_value_placed` for E_R, own and swept, and must add up to X less the put-back, else the part is open); an application that a `REFUND` or `PET_DAMAGE_RECLASSIFICATION` correction Q of the same Settlement reversed is laid over Q's stream as the rule says, each overlapped Refund of Q giving a Refund part and each overlapped application of Q followed again (a chain; at most 100 steps). Fail closed (an open `CHANGED_APPLICATION` part with the record where it stops): an unknown offset, a reversal by any other correction kind or by no correction, Q brought back from the source anything other than application reversals (a recovery, a receipt or a forfeiture reversal), Q also reversed the Credit, a return or a receipt, or forfeited from the source, or the stretch reaches past what Q paid out from the money it took back (money Q left in the source).
        - Source (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`; the 10e-3d-2b-1a and 10e-3d-2b-1b `swept_state` CTE replaced whole): the swept and own takes come from `app.settlement_returned_value_placed` (with the offset), each application take goes through the follow function, and the rows are summed per own flag, correction and record (two stretches can fall on one record of Q); a Refund part is open (`CHANGED_REFUND`) when the sum is more than the Refund's net, as today. The parts on Q's records are then listed, charged back and applied again by the existing lists (an application reversed whole and its rest put back, the 10e-3c and 10e-3e path; a Refund part charged back to the person who received it, the 10e-3b share). A record that would hold an own part and another correction's part fails closed through the existing "no record is a part twice" check.
        - Refusal (exact-text patch of `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed`): the `CHANGED_APPLICATION` message becomes "A later correction changed a payment made with this return's money in a way this reversal cannot follow yet; reversing it waits for the owner" (the cases left, all fail-closed backstops: a change by a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` or `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION`, sub-slice 10e-3d-2c; a put-back whose reversed return's stretch the walk does not place on it directly, such as a put-back of a put-back; money a changing correction left in the source, which no `REFUND` or pet-damage writer does today, because the shared step applies or refunds all of it). No other change; no writer, lock or grant change (the lock function's `RETURN_REVERSAL` branch recomputes the source, so Q's listed applications and their targets are locked as any listed application; a change to a listed Refund makes the draft stale through the digest).

Finding (independent review round 1, confirmed): the premise of Q-111 does not hold on the real path, because the database refuses a Credit application not later than every earlier event of its Credit (migration 41) and a Deposit application not later than the Deposit's latest balance event (migration 86, with `application_order` unique per Final Settlement), so the change is defence in depth and no stored result changes. Not changed (they never decide who pays or how much): the write order of `app.write_correction_named_deposit_application_reversals`, the 10e-3d-2a listing order of the parts applied again, the `REFUND` release order. Tests: three in the new module `test_settlement_returned_value_tie_break_migration.py` (placed, listed order and own choice; the follow stream with the round 1 reviewer's set-up; least privilege and forward-only); the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261209_135`.
      - **10e-3d-2c — money of a return that left through a bounced Payment or a wrong receipt** (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 1: "charge the person who received that Refund"; the Q-2026-10-11-78 rule). Pre-code design (2026-10-14, read only; head `20261208_134`; the walk, placed and follow functions read from migrations 130 and 134, the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` and `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` writes from the docstrings of migrations 117, 118, 120 and 122; migration `20261210_136` planned; BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-REFUND-015, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-002):
        - What exists: a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction C (a bounced Payment) recovers every Refund paid from the Payment's value that is not fully returned (a `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` of its whole net, a charge to the person who received it), reverses every Credit application and Deposit application from the Payment's Credits and Deposits, then reverses the Credits (taking all they still hold) and the Deposit receipts (each funded Deposit ends empty). A `DEPOSIT_RECEIPT_CORRECTION` C does the same for one Deposit's wrong receipt (recoveries, applications reversed newest first, the whole receipt reversed, the Deposit empty), and may then write the right receipt with its own DL-032 step (10e-2b) or forfeiture (10e-2c), all from the new money. The walk counts C's draw as what it paid out (the Credit or receipt reversal, plus any new applications, Refunds and forfeitures) less what it brought back (its recoveries, its application reversals and new receipts), so C takes from a return E exactly E's money still in the source at C's time; the placed function does not split such a C and names its reversal as one open part (`CREDIT_REVERSAL`, `RECEIPT_REVERSAL`). A part of E's money on another correction S's payment that C reversed stops open in the follow function (a reversal by another correction kind: `CHANGED_APPLICATION`), and a part on S's Refund that C recovered is above that Refund's net (`CHANGED_REFUND`). E's reversal is refused today in all three cases (the committed real-path tests `test_settlement_returned_value_left_migration.py::test_a_payment_return_and_a_receipt_correction_count_what_they_take_from_a_return`, the owner's two examples, and `test_settlement_return_reversal_changed_payment_migration.py::test_a_part_on_a_payment_a_receipt_correction_reversed_waits`).
        - Rule: in all three shapes the money went back into the source (C's recovery or application reversal) and out with C's reversal of the source, and C charged E's returned Refund R's recipient only R's net, which E had lowered. So E's reversal charges each such part back to the person who received R (a further `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` on R linked to the reversal, the 10e-1c charge to that person on R's charge Lease or with no Lease, the Q-2026-10-12-94 recipient rule), and nothing else: the charges C made to other Refunds' recipients and the debts C made owed again stay. Check with no S: the owner's Credit example (2,000 Refund, X1 300, C charges 1,700, X1's reversal charges 300: 2,000 in total); with S (Deposit receipt 1,000 wrong; R 1,000 to P; E 300; S pays 100 of debt Y and refunds 200 to P2; C charges P2 200, makes Y owed again, charges P 700 and reverses the receipt): E's reversal charges P 300, so P pays 1,000, P2 200 and Y is owed, as if E never happened; together exactly E's 300 (Q-100).
        - Fail closed (the part stays open, with the existing message reworded to "... in a way this reversal cannot follow yet; reversing it waits for the owner"): C of another variant or another Settlement; C's correction did not also reverse the source (a Credit reversal of that Credit, or a reversal of a receipt into that Deposit); for a payment part, C did not reverse that payment; for a Refund part, C's recovery of that Refund is below the part above its net; R's net after E's return reversal below the total charged back to R; R has an active return of its own other than E; R's charge Lease has a closed Final Settlement other than this one (the 10e-1c refusal; a known limit).
        - Patches (exact text unless named): the placed function names an open take of such a C as a part of R (new record kind `RETURNED_REFUND`, record id R); the follow function turns a stretch on a payment C reversed into the same kind; `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state` turns a Refund part above its Refund's net into an R part when C's recovery covers it, and sums the R parts into one recovery entry of R (its recipient, its net after the reversal); `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed`: the `CREDIT_REVERSAL`/`RECEIPT_REVERSAL` refusal ends for these shapes; the recovery scope `app.settlement_return_reversal_refund` also accepts R when the source lists it; the confirm writes R's charge-back after the `CORRECTION_REVERSAL` row (R's net is 0 before it when C recovered all of it; the build confirms how the general Refund correction check reads the net at insert); the lock function already locks the returned Refund and the Lease's invoice lock (checked at build).
        - Committed tests that change (owner answer (a) to Q-103 point 1 ends the refusals; standing rules of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-14, renamed): `test_a_payment_return_and_a_receipt_correction_count_what_they_take_from_a_return` (both reversals become allowed: P charged 300, the Pet Deposit Refund's receiver 1,000, each Refund's net 0, the ledger reconciles), `test_a_part_on_a_payment_a_receipt_correction_reversed_waits` (allowed: the part charged to R's recipient); the `REVERSED` message constant in `test_settlement_returned_value_left_migration.py` and the hand-built opened cases in the swept and own-parts modules if the message changes.
        - Tests (new module, last in `scripts/database-test.sh`), on the real path, to the cent: the owner's two examples; the worked S example (shapes 2 and 3 together); an own use's payment that C reversed; the fail-closed inputs above; least privilege and forward-only.
      - 10e-3d-2c as built (migration `20261210_136`; record ops/reviews/V1-056-slice-10e-3d-2c-return-reversal-returned-refund.md; docs/004 DB-SETTLE-005 and DB-REFUND-002 paragraph, docs/010): as designed, with five implementer's design changes confirmed by the round 1 independent reviewer (none changes who pays or how much): the general Refund correction check counts R's listed charge-back as available for the reversal of E's return (and as standing for a source Payment no longer received when it covers the whole reversal); R's charge-back takes the reversal's exact time, written right after it (the deferred Credit and Deposit history checks group events by time; R's net cannot go below 0 first); the refusal of an R with another active return is not built (the committed test the design names as allowed has one; the cap is R's net after the reversal); an own use's Refund that such a C charged back takes the walk path; the follow function hands such a stretch back with no record id. New internals `app.settlement_return_reversal_charge_back` and `app.settlement_return_reversal_paired_charge_back`; the recovery writer dates R's charge-back from the confirm's evidence and words its charge "value whose return was recorded by mistake". Committed tests renamed and allowed (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 1; run rule of 2026-10-14): `test_settlement_returned_value_left_migration.py::test_a_payment_return_and_a_receipt_correction_count_what_they_take_from_a_return` to `test_a_payment_return_and_a_receipt_correction_take_a_return_s_money_its_reversal_charges_back` (the owner's two examples), and `test_settlement_return_reversal_changed_payment_migration.py::test_a_part_on_a_payment_a_receipt_correction_reversed_waits` to `test_a_part_on_a_payment_a_receipt_correction_reversed_is_charged_to_the_refund_s_recipient`; the new module `test_settlement_return_reversal_returned_refund_migration.py` has the worked example (both shapes), the own uses' payment and Refund, an own Refund alone, a Refund part charged back only in part (open), the fail-closed inputs (hand-built sources and rolled-back owner transactions, the Q1 guard set-up included), the relaxed checks still refusing, least privilege and forward-only.       - **10e-3d-2d — a correction of another Lease's Final Settlement used the returned Credit** (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100 point 2: "a correction on another Lease of the same Customer that uses the returned Credit counts too; when a reversal undoes a payment on that other Lease, that Lease's history must show which correction undid it"; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 3: a companion correction of the other Lease's Settlement, recorded and confirmed together with the reversal in one step). Pre-code design (2026-10-15, read only; head `20261210_136`, the current bodies of the walk, placed, follow, source and refusal functions read with `scripts/catalog-dump.sh`; BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005). Split (owner allowed): **10e-3d-2d-1** the count across Settlements, with the undo on the other Lease refused until it is built (migration `20261211_137` planned), built now; **10e-3d-2d-2** the companion correction and the undo on the other Lease (its own pre-code design after the 10e-3d-2d-1 commit, because it writes a second correction header and its draft in the reversal's transaction).
        - What exists: `app.settlement_returned_value_walk(settlement, kind, source, except)` walks only the corrections of the given Settlement (`correction.final_settlement_id=p_settlement`), so a use of a returned Credit by a correction of another Settlement of the same Customer is not counted: the return keeps its left value while the Credit holds less (the Q-2026-10-09-63 limit for that case), a held-value Refund naming it can pay out money a later return brought back, and its reversal takes that money (the Q-97 problem). A Customer Credit belongs to a Customer, not a Lease; a correction's DL-032 step on another Lease B applies it to the Customer's open debt on B ("each Customer's Credit pays that Customer's open debt first" while a Deposit holds money), so the case is on the real path. A Deposit belongs to one Lease, so its walk does not change. Every left value is read through the walk (`app.settlement_returned_value_left`, `app.settlement_held_credit_value`, `app.settlement_held_deposit_value`, the placed and source functions), so one patch of the walk changes them all. The follow function already stops open (`CHANGED_APPLICATION`) at a changer of another Settlement, and the 10e-3d-2c returned-Refund part already needs a taker of this Settlement.
        - 10e-3d-2d-1 walk (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_returned_value_walk`): for a Credit, the corrections walked are those of the given Settlement and, of every other Final Settlement, those with a linked record the walk reads for that Credit (a Credit application from it, a reversal of such an application, a Refund from it, a return, return reversal or recovery of such a Refund, a reversal of the Credit), all in one order by time and id. Corrections of the given Settlement with no such record give no walk rows, as today, so every committed single-Settlement result stays.
        - 10e-3d-2d-1 refusal until 10e-3d-2d-2 (fail closed): `app.settlement_returned_value_placed` (exact-text patch) names a take of the return's money by a correction of another Final Settlement as one open part of the new kind `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` (record id: that correction), before any other kind, so none of its records is listed for undoing; the source's existing open-kind path puts it in `swept_open` (or `own_open` on the walk path); `app.assert_settlement_return_reversal_allowed` (exact-text patch) refuses it before any other open part (55000, "A correction of another Lease's Final Settlement used this return's money; reversing it waits for sub-slice 10e-3d-2d-2"). A held-value Refund naming such a return is refused by the existing left-value rule ("Nothing the earlier correction returned is left to refund") when nothing is left. The lock function is unchanged (a held-value Refund, a return reversal and another Lease's correction step all lock the Credit row; a change between the record and the confirm makes the draft stale, P5S02).

        - 10e-3d-2d-2 outline (detail in its pre-code design): X1's reversal reverses B's payment made with X1's money whole and puts back its rest, or charges a Refund part back to its recipient, as for a correction of the same Settlement; every record on B is linked to a companion correction of B's Settlement (a `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft of B's Settlement naming the reversal's draft, recorded and confirmed by the same approver in the reversal's transaction, so the 8e-1 and 8e-2 refusals pass as today and B's correction list shows it); the reversal names the companion and the companion names the reversal.

        - **10e-3d-2d-2 pre-code design** (2026-10-15, read only; head `20261211_137`; current bodies read with `scripts/catalog-dump.sh`: the walk, placed, follow, left, takes, parts and successor functions, the source and refusal functions, the recorder, confirm and lock function, `app.begin_settlement_correction`, `app.link_settlement_correction_effect`, `app.settlement_correction_linked`, the closed-Settlement guards, the Settlement and correction Credit application writer and checks, the put-back rule, `app.write_payment_return_recoveries`, `app.refund_recovery_allowed`, `app.validate_general_refund_correction`; owner answers (a) to Q-2026-10-13-100 point 2, Q-2026-10-13-103 points 1 and 3 and Q-2026-10-15-113; BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-006, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005, DB-REFUND-002).
          - What exists (after 10e-3d-2d-1): a take of a return E's money by a correction S of another Final Settlement B is one open `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` part, refused. B's records are protected by B's own rules: a reversal of a Credit application whose `final_settlement_id` is B passes only when linked to a correction of B confirmed in the same transaction (`app.refuse_final_settlement_credit_application_reversal`); an Invoice or charge on B's Lease likewise (`app.refuse_closed_final_settlement_change`); a recovery of a Refund that a correction of B paid out must be linked to a correction of B ("every closed Final Settlement the Refund belongs to links the row", `app.refund_recovery_allowed`). B's own Final Settlement confirm writes its Credit applications with `final_settlement_id` B and no correction link (ids derived from its draft); the walk does not read them (the Q-113 case). A correction's put-back goes through `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application` with its own draft and Settlement, and its checks read that draft's `source` and `plan` (variant `RETURN_REVERSAL` only).
          - Split (owner allowed; each ends in a commit; until its sub-slice is built each case stays refused with its own message, so nothing is undone half): **10e-3d-2d-2a** (migration `20261212_138` planned) the companion correction and the Credit application parts of another Settlement's corrections (reversed whole, the rest put back on its own line); **10e-3d-2d-2b** (migration `20261213_139` planned) the Refund parts of another Settlement's corrections (charged back to the person who received the Refund, through the companion), and the take of a bounced Payment's correction of another Settlement that reversed the Credit (the Q-103 point 1 rule: a part of the returned Refund, charged to its recipient by the reversal alone, nothing on B); **10e-3d-2d-2c** (migration `20261214_140` planned) the owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-15-113: B's own Final Settlement confirm's Credit applications in the walk, in the same time order, undone through the same companion.
          - Companion (2d-2a): for each other Final Settlement B with a part the reversal R undoes, R's confirm writes one `SETTLEMENT_CORRECTION` draft of B with the new variant `RETURN_REVERSAL_COMPANION`, its id derived from R's draft and B (new internal `app.settlement_correction_companion_id(p_draft uuid, p_settlement uuid)`, IMMUTABLE, as the other derived ids, no grant), through `app.insert_financial_draft` with `final_settlements.prepare` and R's reason, recorded and confirmed by R's approver (the outline: "recorded and confirmed by the same approver"; implementer's reading: the approver is its preparer, because it is written at the confirm, and it carries only what R's confirmed draft decided); payload: `variant`, `final_settlement_id` (B), `lease_id` (B's Lease), `return_reversal_draft_id` (R), `return_reversal_correction_id` (`app.settlement_correction_id(R)`), `source` (B's slice of R's stored source, in the same keys: `credit_applications`, `reapply`, from 2d-2b `recoveries`), `plan` (`steps`: B's put-back steps, as `app.settlement_return_reversal_reapply_steps` gives them). Its decision and header through `app.begin_settlement_correction` (same Property, B closed, the approver active and human, B's Settlement draft lock); every record on B linked to it before it is written. R's source names its companions; R's result and audit event name them; each companion gets its own audit event (`final_settlement.correction.confirmed`, variant `RETURN_REVERSAL_COMPANION`, B's Lease) naming R. B's correction list and Lease history then show which correction undid the payment (owner answer (a) to Q-103 point 3). A companion is never pending: it is recorded and decided in R's transaction, so no withdraw, reject or other confirm reaches it.
          - Walk (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_returned_value_walk`): a companion is part of its reversal. The corrections walked leave out companions, and each sum reads the effects of the correction and of its companions (the companion's draft names the correction), so R's net draw is the money R really takes from the source (B's records it undoes are R's money brought back), and the companion's time never ties R's. No committed result changes (no companion exists before 138).
          - Placed (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_returned_value_placed`): a taker's effects include its companions'; the `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` part stays only for a take of another Settlement's correction that has a Refund from the source (2d-2a; ended in 2d-2b); otherwise a take of another Settlement's correction is placed on its records exactly as a correction of this Settlement (its applications newest first, then followed by the follow function); its Credit reversal or return reversal stays an open part with its existing message (2d-2b ends the bounced Payment case).           - Source (exact-text patches of `app.settlement_return_reversal_source_state`): the listed Credit applications and parts applied again keep their lists and order; a new key `companions` lists, in Settlement id order, each other Settlement whose application is listed (`final_settlement_id`, `lease_id`, `property_id`, `closed`, the derived companion `draft_id` and `settlement_correction_id`). Finding (wording corrected in review round 1): a correction's draw covers, in order, what it brought back itself (the DRAW offset), its own return, then the return it names, before any other return's money, and its records are laid out Refunds first, then applications newest first; every Credit Refund writer caps its Refund by one of those three (a `REFUND` correction by what it released, a `REFUND_RETURN` by its own return, a held-value Refund by its named return's left value) or pays it from a Credit it creates itself, so no real path through a take to another correction's Credit Refund was found; the caps are enforced by the Refund writers, not by the placed function (the 10e-3d-2a round 2 reviewer confirmed this for one Settlement; the 2b-1 round 1 independent reviewer found no real path; wording softened in review round 3). In the 10e-3d-2d-2a round 1 set-up the other Lease's `REFUND` correction refunded 200 and wrote 1,300 again, and X1's 300 lands on its applications, not its Refund; the 2d-2a placed function still made the whole take one open part because the correction has a Refund. Split (owner allowed; each ends in a commit): **10e-3d-2d-2b-1** (migration `20261213_139`): the placed function places a take of another Settlement's correction on its records even when that correction also paid a Refund from the source; a part that falls on such a Refund (no real path found) is one open `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` part with the existing refusal, so nothing is undone half; the application parts are undone through the companion as in 2d-2a (no other function changes). **10e-3d-2d-2b-2**: the companion charge-back of such a Refund part and another Settlement's bounced Payment take (the Q-2026-10-13-103 point 1 rule, nothing on B); both are built when a real path is shown (the 2d-2b-1 round 1 reviewer is asked for set-ups); otherwise they stay refused and are asked as a question first. Review round 1 of 2b-1 (independent): (1) a real path to another Settlement's Refund exists through the follow function, not the placed function (set-up R1 in the 2b-1 review record: X0's money sits on the other Lease's payment, which a later `REFUND` correction of that Lease reversed, writing part of it again and refunding the rest from the Credit; the follow function stops there today as `CHANGED_APPLICATION`, fail closed, because it reads only this Settlement's changers); 2b-2 is that follow step for another Settlement's `REFUND` changer, its Refund part charged back through the companion. Warning for its design: relaxing the follow function alone would send that Refund part through `swept_paid`, which charges on this Settlement's Lease (`lease_id` and `closed_final_settlement_id` of `p_settlement`), against owner answer 4 (the other Lease when the recipient is its signed Customer); the recovery must also be linked to the companion (`app.refund_recovery_allowed` requires a correction of every closed Final Settlement the Refund belongs to). (2) Another Settlement's bounced Payment cannot take the return's money: only a `RETURNED_PAYMENT` reverses a Credit, and the return's own correction makes the first Settlement rely on that Credit, so the other Settlement's `RETURNED_PAYMENT` is refused; that part of 2b-2 is not built (its refusal stays).
            - 2d-2b-1 placed (exact-text patches of `app.settlement_returned_value_placed`): the `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` opened row of 2d-2a is removed; in the placed rows a `REFUND` record (as built, review round 1 security: any record other than a Credit application) of a correction of another Final Settlement is named `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` with the correction as its record (the 2d-1 open-part shape), so the source puts it in `swept_open` and the refusal function refuses it with its existing message.
            - 2d-2b-1 committed test that changes (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-13-103 point 3; standing rules of 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-14, renamed): `test_settlement_return_reversal_companion_migration.py::test_a_take_whose_correction_also_refunded_from_the_credit_stays_one_open_part` becomes a confirmed reversal through the companion (as built, corrected in review round 2: X1's 300 sits on the older of that correction's two payments, the 1,300 written again, at the end of the newest-first layout; that payment is reversed whole and the rest put back on its own line); the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261213_139`. A read-only search at build for other tests that expect the 2d-2a `OTHER_SETTLEMENT` part from the real path.

          - **10e-3d-2d-2c pre-code note** (2026-10-16, read only; head `20261214_140`; current bodies of the walk, placed, follow, left, takes, parts, source, refusal, confirm, result and companion source functions, `app.settlement_credit_application_owner`, `app.settlement_correction_put_back_allowed`, `app.assert_settlement_correction_credit_application`, `app.refuse_final_settlement_credit_application_reversal`, `app.settlement_held_credit_value` and `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application` read with `scripts/catalog-dump.sh`; owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-15-113; BR-SETTLE-013, BR-SETTLE-016, BR-CREDIT-014, DL-032; DB-SETTLE-005; migration `20261215_141`).
            - What exists: a Final Settlement's own confirm writes its Credit applications through `app.post_final_settlement_credit_application` with `final_settlement_id` set and no correction link (one detail each; each later than every earlier event of its Credit). The walk reads only records linked to corrections, so such a use of a returned Credit is not counted (the Q-113 case: X1 shows money left while the Credit is empty). A Final Settlement's own confirm pays no Credit Refund (its Refunds are Deposit Refunds), so its use of a Credit is its Credit applications only. A reversal of such an application passes only when linked to a correction of its Settlement confirmed in the same transaction (`app.refuse_final_settlement_credit_application_reversal`), which the companion is; `app.settlement_credit_application_owner` already names its Settlement; the companion's put-back check (`app.settlement_correction_put_back_allowed`) needs a reversed application with one detail, reversed by a reversal linked to the companion, which holds for it.
            - Walk (exact-text patches of `app.settlement_returned_value_walk`): for a Credit, each Final Settlement whose own confirm applied it (applications from the Credit with that `final_settlement_id` and no correction link) is a walker too, at the time of its first such application, in the one order by time and id with the corrections (walker id: the Final Settlement's id, because there is no correction). Its draw is the sum of those applications; it has no return of its own and names none, so it takes the other returns' money, the oldest first (`swept`). It is a walker only while some return's money is left at its place in the order; otherwise it takes nothing and gives no row, so every walk in which no return comes before a Settlement confirm keeps its rows, and every committed result stays (the committed two-Lease set-ups close the second Lease before any return).
            - Placed (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_returned_value_placed`): a take by a Final Settlement's confirm is placed on its Credit applications from the source, the newest first (time, then id; their times differ by the Credit's chronology check), kind `CREDIT_APPLICATION`; the part's correction field holds the Final Settlement's id.
            - Follow (exact-text patch of `app.settlement_returned_value_follow`): the writer of a Credit application with no correction link and a `final_settlement_id` is that Final Settlement (today such a payment stops open as `CHANGED_APPLICATION`, because its writer must be known); nothing else in the follow function changes (a later change by a correction is followed as today: a `REFUND` correction of any Settlement is followed; a companion or another variant stops open).
            - No other function changes: the source lists the application for undoing and its Settlement in `companions` (through the owner function); the refusal function's checks hold as for a correction's application; the confirm reverses it linked to that Settlement's companion and puts back any rest through the companion's step, as in 2d-2a. A part entry's `settlement_correction_id` holds the Final Settlement's id for such a part (named in docs/004; no reader joins it to the corrections table for an application part).
            - Committed tests that change: the 27 head-revision assertions move to `20261215_141`. A read-only search at build for any committed test in which a Settlement confirm applies a Credit after a return into it (expected: none; the 2d-1 and later two-Lease set-ups close the second Lease first), and for any test that compares a walk or source of such a Credit exactly.
