- **Before 10h — a returned Pet Refund-review Refund whose Pet Deposit is off the DL-032 path** (found in 10c-2b-2b; open: Q-2026-10-09-61, the owner answers before 10h with Q-2026-10-08-53 and Q-2026-10-08-57): after a pet noncompliance termination (BR-DEP-022) the returned money of a Pet Refund-review Refund paid before the termination stays held by the kept or empty Pet Deposit with no way out (and, for a `KEPT` Pet Deposit, already before closure). Options keep (a correction that links a forfeiture) and pay back (refund it again, the open debt paid first); recommendation pay back. No correction that links a forfeiture is built until the owner answers (owner, 2026-10-09).
  - Owner answer to Q-2026-10-09-61 (2026-10-10): pay back, before and after closure; the returned money had already left the Pet Deposit before the termination, so it is the Customer's money. **Sub-slice 10c-2b-5** (after 10e-2, before 10e-3): (1) before closure the Settlement preview, the plan check and the confirm treat the returned amount of a pre-termination Pet Refund-review Refund that the Pet Deposit still holds as DL-032 value (no forfeiture required for it; it first pays the Lease's open debt in the DL-032 order; the rest is refunded by the Settlement's own Deposit Refund step); the rest of the Deposit keeps its forfeiture path; (2) after closure a `REFUND_RETURN` with a new Refund and the `HELD_VALUE_REFUND` form accept such a Pet Deposit for that amount only (a relaxing exception to the "only on the DL-032 path" refusal), the open debt first; (3) if the owner approves Q-2026-10-10-73 (a), a Lease Deposit forfeiture leaves that amount out. The committed 10c-2b-2b test `test_a_returned_pet_refund_off_the_dl032_path_is_only_held` then changes its expectation under this answer (name kept, docs/010 note). Tests: both termination cases (`NOTHING_HELD` and `KEPT` Pet Deposit) before and after closure, with and without open debt; the 10d refusal of another active forfeiture stays (implementer note under Q-61: kept as a guard). Its pre-code design is written when 10e-2 is committed.
    - **10c-2b-5 pre-code design** (2026-10-10, read only, written after 10e-1a on the owner's instruction; head `20261120_116`, read from a throwaway database's catalog; BR-DEP-022, BR-SETTLE-008, BR-SETTLE-013, DL-032, DL-053; owner answers pay back to Q-2026-10-09-61 and (a) to Q-2026-10-10-73; migration `20261121_117` planned). Q-2026-10-10-75 answered 2026-10-10: point 1 (A), point 2 (b) (a return verified before the termination is paid back too). Open: Q-2026-10-10-76 (which money a later Pet Refund-review Refund pays after a return before the termination; blocks the build).
      - What exists: (1) `app.lease_deposit_settlement_path` gives each Deposit of the terminated Lease the path KEPT (an active forfeiture), DL_032 (no ground, or every forfeiture reversed), FORFEITURE_REQUIRED (a ground, no forfeiture, money held) or NOTHING_HELD; a Pet Deposit has only the BR-DEP-022 ground (`app.lease_deposit_forfeiture_rule`). (2) Every place that applies or refunds a Deposit checks the path DL_032: the preview's DL-032 lists (`app.final_settlement_preview`), the BR-SETTLE-008 sweep of the Settlement plan (`app.final_settlement_plan_result`), the Settlement Deposit Refund source (`app.assert_final_settlement_refund_source`), the Deposit application check (`app.validate_deposit_application`), the correction step and its Deposit Refund source (`app.settlement_correction_plan_result`, `app.assert_settlement_correction_deposit_refund_source`), the pet reclassification reversals (`app.settlement_pet_classification_reversals`), the refund-only form (`app.settlement_held_value_plan`) and the returned-Refund form with a new Refund (`app.settlement_refund_return_plan`, which checks before the return is written; the step's overlay then adds the returned amount whatever the path). The 10d forfeiture reversal does not read the path. (3) `app.lease_deposit_forfeiture_source` forfeits the whole held value and the confirm refuses a stale amount; the disposition insert check accepts any amount up to the held value. (4) Every Pet Refund-review Refund is paid while the Lease is signed, so before the termination.
      - Pay-back value (new internal `app.lease_deposit_payback_value(deposit)`, never NULL, no grant): for a Pet Deposit of a terminated Lease, the smaller of what it holds now and the net amount of its Pet Refund-review Refunds returned after the termination (returns verified after `terminated_at`, less their reversals); 0 for a Security Deposit (Q-75 point 2, recommendation (a)). In a forfeited Pet Deposit every cent held is pay-back money (no other money can reach it), so the same rule also covers the later return of a Settlement or correction Refund paid from pay-back money.
      - Design (A), recommended in Q-75 point 1: `app.lease_deposit_settlement_path` is replaced (same keys plus `payback_usd_cents`): a Pet Deposit with the ground whose held money is all pay-back money (held above 0 and equal to the pay-back value) has the path DL_032, also while its forfeiture stays active; FORFEITURE_REQUIRED only while it holds money beyond the pay-back value; the other cases as today. Every reader in (2) then applies, refunds and sweeps that money as DL-032 value with no change of its own, before and after closure. The forfeiture source forfeits only the held money beyond the pay-back value and refuses (22023, "The Deposit holds nothing to forfeit") when that is 0 (Q-73 (a), an approved tightening). The returned-Refund form with a new Refund also accepts a Pet Deposit with the ground (its forfeiture not reversed) when, after the return, all its held money would be pay-back money (a relaxing exact-text patch, because its check runs before the return). Consequence: after closure, pay-back money behaves as any held DL-032 money: the next correction that runs the shared step must apply or refund it (BR-SETTLE-008), as it must today for money a return-only `REFUND_RETURN` left in a Settlement Deposit.
      - Design (B), the plan text read narrowly: the path stays as today; before closure the preview, the plan check and the confirm, and after closure only the two named forms take the pay-back value as an exception; other correction steps leave it out. It needs exception code in about nine functions and a second notion of usable Deposit value.

- **Signature fixture clock (owner instruction of 2026-10-14; test set-up only; record ops/reviews/V1-056-signature-fixture-clock.md)**: the `Signature evidence is invalid` failures of the Lease signature fixture (a full rerun in four sessions; 13 failures at the end of 10e-3e run P3) come from `app.record_lease_signature`, which refuses a signing time later than the database's statement time with no tolerance (migration 24; `app.sign_lease` the same), while the fixtures took the time from the host clock (`datetime.now(UTC)`) and the database runs in the Docker VM, whose clock can lag the host clock; a lag longer than the few milliseconds between the fixture's reading and the database statement is enough, far below the about 80 ms resolution of the K1 check; the failures cluster in one stretch of a run (drift, then a resync). Fix in the test set-up only: `database_now()` in `test_lease_lifecycle_migration.py` reads `clock_timestamp()` from the database; `signature_command`'s default and every `LeaseSignCommand` and HTTP signing time in the database tests use it, and the lifecycle test's termination time too (it must not be before the signing time); the app's check is not changed.
- **Signing time from the database clock (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-14-110, 2026-10-14; small sub-slice, built first in the order of 2026-10-14; BR-AUDIT-003, API-036, API-038, UI-038, UI-040; no migration; record ops/reviews/V1-056-signing-time-database-clock.md)**: a Signer's signature time and the Lease signing time are the database's own time at the moment of the command, never a browser or device clock, so a device clock ahead of the database can no longer refuse a signing and nobody can make a signature look earlier by setting a device clock back.
  - Pre-code design (2026-10-14, read only, head `20261208_134`): (1) Routes (`backend/src/property_rental/lease_api.py`): `LeaseSignatureSource` (`PATCH /lease-signers/{signer_id}`) and `LeaseSignSource` (`POST /leases/{lease_id}/sign`) drop `signed_at`; both keep `extra="forbid"`, so a body that still sends a signing time is refused (422) and nothing is called; both routes pass `signed_at=None` to the service. (2) Service (`backend/src/property_rental/lease.py`): `LeaseRecordSignatureCommand.signed_at` and `LeaseSignCommand.signed_at` become `datetime | None` (a given time must still carry a time zone); the two calls pass `COALESCE(%s::timestamptz, statement_timestamp())`, so with `None` the database function receives the statement time that its own check compares with (`statement_timestamp()` is the same inside the called function). No database function changes: the checks stay as they are (a time later than the statement time, a time before the Signer was added, a Lease signing before a Signer's signature are still refused). The request digest carries `signed_at: null`, and the database replays a known request before it reads the time, so a retry of the same request returns the first result with its first time. Implementer's reading (named in the report): the service command keeps the optional explicit time so the committed database tests keep checking the refusals they check today through the service with explicit times (a Signer signature time later than the statement time; a Lease signing time earlier than a Signer's signature or later than the statement time; the "not before the Signer was added" check is unchanged but has no committed test, corrected in review round 2); no route passes one. (3) Screen and client (`frontend/src/leaseClient.ts`, `frontend/src/LeaseManagement.tsx`): `LeaseSignatureSource` drops `signed_at`; the screen no longer sends `new Date().toISOString()`; it shows the time the server returns, as before. (4) Committed tests that change, all covered by the answer (the request shape): the bodies of `test_lease_api.py::test_signer_signing_renewal_and_termination_are_explicit_commands` and `test_lease_api_migration.py::test_http_create_update_sign_and_replay_use_runtime_role` drop `signed_at`; the source of `leaseClient.test.tsx` TEST-FE-186 drops it (the type no longer has it); `test_lease_lifecycle_migration.py::test_commands_are_idempotent_versioned_audited_and_preserve_signer_rules` gains one line, `assert additional_signature.signed_at is not None`, so the type check accepts its arithmetic on the now optional time (found by the first type check; no value or other assertion changes). (5) New tests: `test_lease_api.py::test_signing_routes_take_no_signing_time_from_the_request` (both routes pass `None`; a body with `signed_at` is refused 422 and the flow is not called); `test_lease.py::test_signing_commands_accept_no_time_and_keep_the_time_zone_rule`; `test_lease_api_migration.py::test_http_signing_time_is_the_database_time` (on PostgreSQL through the runtime role: each Signer's signature time equals the `occurred_at` of its own `app.lease_commands` row and the Lease signing time equals the `occurred_at` of its `app.lease_signing_outcomes` row, both stamped by the database with `statement_timestamp()` (the `lease.sign` command row stores the time it was given, so it proves nothing; corrected in review round 1); a retry of either route with the same Idempotency-Key and body returns the first time and writes no row; a body with a past `signed_at` is refused 422 and the Lease version does not change); TEST-FE-674 and TEST-FE-675 in `LeaseManagement.test.tsx` (the screen sends a source of exactly the event id and the reason for a Signer signature and for the Lease signing). (6) Docs: docs/002 BR-AUDIT-003, docs/005 API-036 and API-038, docs/006 UI-040, docs/010; traceability appended. (7) Not changed: other times a screen sends (for example termination or move-in times) are outside Q-110.
  - As built (2026-10-14; record ops/reviews/V1-056-signing-time-database-clock.md; docs/002 BR-AUDIT-003, docs/005 API-036 and API-038, docs/006 UI-040, docs/010): as designed, with the review changes. Round 1 (both reviewers): the database test's Lease signing check compared the Lease time with the `lease.sign` history row, which stores the time it was given, so it proved nothing; it now compares with `app.lease_signing_outcomes.occurred_at` (the statement time), counts the `lease.sign` rows (one), and retries the Lease signing route with the same Idempotency-Key, If-Match and body after the Lease moved on (only a replay can answer 200); the three 422s pin `error.fields == ["body.signed_at"]`; the unit test also asserts that a given time changes the request digest. Round 2: text only (Q-2026-10-14-112 point 2 lists which records keep the database time; the unchanged "not before the Signer was added" check has no committed test). Round 3 (independent and security): clean. New Q-2026-10-14-112 (other device-clock times: the termination `occurred_at`, the Reservation `approved_at` and the holding deposit receipt's `received_at`; the runtime role can still pass an earlier time to the two database functions; recommendations (a) and (a); nothing waits).
- **10h — refusals after closure** (owner answers 3 and 4): the one-call allocation reversal, the one-call Credit application reversal and the move draft of a record that existed at closure on a Settlement obligation; after 10e and 10g so a returned Payment and a wrong-Unit case always have a path. 10h also closes the 6b forfeiture reversal draft after closure (owner answer 4 to Q-2026-10-06-36; owner answer 6 to Q-2026-10-10-69): once the Lease's Final Settlement is closed, the 6b recorder `app.record_lease_deposit_reversal_draft` and confirm `app.confirm_lease_deposit_reversal_draft` are refused (P5S01, a conflict), and a `deposit_disposition_reversals` row of a Deposit of that Lease is refused at insert unless a `FORFEITURE_REVERSAL` correction of that Settlement, confirmed in the same transaction, links it (sub-slice 10d); tests: a 6b draft recorded before closure and confirmed after it is refused and stays pending, and the 10d correction still passes. Its test covers a Late-payment Charge too (sub-slice 10c-3a review round 1: a one-call reversal of an allocation that paid the Charge at closure, then a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` of that Charge). Also for the owner to decide with 10h (owner answer 2 of 2026-10-13 to the 10e-4 report): the ordinary `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft (`app.record_payment_return_draft`, `app.confirm_payment_return`; sub-slice 10e-1a) still returns, without a link to the Settlement, a Payment whose only use on a closed Lease after closure is an allocation or a Credit application with nothing refunded (no closed Settlement relied on it and no recovery is charged on that Lease); the `RETURNED_PAYMENT` correction of that Settlement can return the same Payment with the records linked (sub-slice 10e-4). Closing the `PAYMENT_RETURN` path for such a Payment would be a tightening; nothing is built until the owner decides. Also decided with 10h (owner answer 2 of 2026-10-13 to the 10e-3c report): the Credit Refund outside a correction (the owner's "one-call Credit Refund": the ordinary Refund draft of slice 3a-1, `refunds.create` records and `financial.approve` confirms, with no link to the Settlement), which can pay out Credit that an earlier return-only correction brought back after closure; sub-slice 10e-3d does not count it against that correction's left value (Credit spent outside a correction is the known limit of owner answer 2 to Q-2026-10-09-63).
  - Note from the 10c-1a security review round 2: until 10h, a one-call reversal of an allocation or Credit application that paid a Settlement line at closure makes that line unpaid, and a `CHARGE_REDUCTION` can then remove it, which should have been a `REFUND` (no money leaves: the freed value stays on the Payment or Credit and leaves only through an approved Refund draft). 10h closes this path too; its tests include it (owner, 2026-10-07: the gap is accepted until 10h because no money can leave without an approved Refund draft and nothing is live; 10h must close it with a test). Also from 10c-1a: 10h decides whether the re-posted remainder of a closure-era allocation or Credit application (migration 67, written by a 10c-1b `REFUND`) counts as existing at closure. Decided (owner answer to Q-2026-10-07-39, the recommendation approved; 10c-1b review round 1): 10h counts it as existing at closure; 10c-1b already refuses the one-call reversal of a Settlement Credit application's re-posted rest.
  - For 11e (10c-1a security review round 2): the correction screens may show the approver the line's closure value (`outstanding_at_inclusion_usd_cents` of the closure snapshot) next to the current unpaid rest; the recorder's P0002 for an Invoice outside the Settlement's Lease or a line of another Invoice reaches the service as "Lease or Final Settlement was not found", which the routes should word precisely (10b's corrected-Invoice refusal too).
  - For 11e (10c-1b security review round 2): a `REFUND` correction draft's payload shows the payer, the Payment and Credit ids of the released sources and the reference note to every reader of the draft (`final_settlements.prepare` holders through the general draft reads); 11e decides what draft readers see, with the transaction id it already strips. Correction (10d security review round 2): only the correction results and replays strip it today; the general draft reads `app.financial_draft_resource_by_capability` and `app.list_financial_drafts_by_capability` still return it inside `app.financial_draft_json` for a confirmed correction draft, so 11e strips it in those reads or in `app.financial_draft_json`.
  - For 11e (10e-3d-2d-2b-2 security review round 1, note N6): a `RETURN_REVERSAL_COMPANION` draft's `source.recoveries` carries the Refund number, the recipient Customer id, the Lease id and the amounts of the other Lease's charge-back (the same data as the return reversal's own draft, same Property); 11e decides what draft readers see.
- **Times from the database clock (owner answer (a) to Q-2026-10-14-112 point 1, 2026-10-14; one small sub-slice right after 10h, before slice 11)**: the other times the screens still take from the device clock (the Lease termination `occurred_at`; the Reservation extension `approved_at`; the holding deposit receipt's `received_at`) are read case by case. A time that means the moment staff save the action is taken from the database clock, as for signing (Q-2026-10-14-110). A time that records something that happened before it was saved, like money received earlier, is not changed: it is written as a new question with a recommendation. A change that needs a tightening, or a change to committed tests beyond what the answer covers, is asked first. Pre-code design written first.

HTTP routes and screens for corrections stay in 11e.

### Slices 11a to 11e — HTTP routes and staff screens
- 11a: Lease End Date error, Unit cleaning rate, cleaning charge, hand-back month actions.
- 11b: draft list, draft detail, confirm, reject, withdraw (shared by all kinds).
  - Moved (owner answer to Q-2026-10-04-5, 2026-10-04): reject and withdraw for Reservation Deposit drafts only are built in slice 3b; the shared routes for the other kinds stay in 11b.
- 11c: Refund methods, Refund drafts, joint-Signer agreement, deposit forfeiture and Pet Deposit drafts.
  - Owner answer (2026-10-05, to the round-3 note of sub-slice 6b): a refusal caused by business state reaches the user as "conflict", not "invalid". This covers "forfeiture already reversed" and "reversal draft is stale" (sub-slice 6b) and the stale forfeiture draft (slice 6). No code change before 11c; the draft routes are built that way.
  - Note (owner answer to the 10c-2b-2b report, 2026-10-09): the return refusals after closure are business-state refusals and answer 409; 10c-2b-2b does so for a Pet Refund-review Refund (P5S01). In 11c, check the 10c-2b-1 refusal of a Refund paid by a Final Settlement or a post-settlement correction (P5202, 422 on `POST /refunds/{id}/verified-returns` and its reverse route) and move it to 409, so both routes answer the same way. Not changed before 11c.
  - Notes for the returned-Payment routes (sub-slice 10e-1a review round 1): the generic draft reads still return a confirmed `PAYMENT_RETURN` draft's decision `transaction_id` inside `app.financial_draft_json`; 11c strips it for this kind too (as 11e for correction drafts), with a test on the read after a confirm; the routes give stable reason codes for the 55000 cases (a relied-on record: use the correction; a Refund not returned; an active forfeiture: reverse it first; a waiting draft) and treat the oversize 22023 as a conflict, never showing database text; the screen tells staff not to put payer personal data in the reason, because it is copied onto every affected Customer's ledger.
  - Read item (sub-slice 10e-1c review round 1 blocker fix, owner instruction of 2026-10-11): the Refund resource (`app.refund_resource`) and the financial action context (`app.financial_action_context_page`) leave the new `recovery_obligation_id` column out, because the staff screen parser accepts exactly 17 Refund correction keys. Staff need to see the recovery: 11c adds, for a `RECOVERED_BY_CHARGE` correction, the recovery charge (its obligation, Invoice, Invoice number, Customer, Lease or none, amount and status) to the Refund read, the action context and the screens (the parser accepts the new field), with backend and frontend tests; the return results already name each charge.
  - Moved from slice 12 (owner answer 3 of 2026-10-13 to the 10e-3c report; 10e-3c security review round 3 note 2): the Settlement Credit writer's 23514 refusal of a part that a `RETURN_REVERSAL` applies again (a Credit part on a Rent or Service Invoice with no designation when the Rent-first proposal no longer gives its line) is business state, so it reaches the user as a conflict (409), like the other business-state refusals here. Not changed before 11c.
- 11d: Wrong-Unit Payment move.
  - Notes (slice 7 reviews): show the one-call reversal as unavailable for every moved (`WRONG_UNIT_MOVE`) allocation, one-Customer moves included, and point to a move draft; read move drafts only through the capability-filtered draft reads (`app.list_financial_drafts_by_capability`), because they hold acknowledgement text. Show the Credit reversal as unavailable for a Credit released from a moved allocation when the database would refuse it (the existing Credit reversal route and its action context would otherwise offer it and answer 409).
- 11e: Final Settlement (API-070 to 072), preview, confirm, corrections.
- Each: routes in the existing style, docs/005 entries, frontend client layer, screens with loading, empty, success and error states, accessible forms, backend route tests and frontend tests.
- Sec: yes for every part (CSRF, session, deny-by-default, no Staff confirm, no customer data in logs).
- Note: the app still has no working login, so there is no browser check; that waits for the login slice (DL-067).

## 4. Questions for the owner (with my recommendation)

Blocking questions are marked (B). I will not start a slice whose question is open.

1. (B, slice 1) Making the Lease draft and sign functions reject a non-month-end End Date is a tightening change. Does DL-069 ("V1-056 adds the validation") count as owner approval for this one tightening? Recommendation: yes, function check only, no table CHECK, existing rows untouched, historical fixtures narrowed with docs/010 notes.
2. (slice 1) The End Date is optional on a Lease draft. Recommendation: check it only when present, and require a month-end End Date at signing.
3. (B, slice 2) Which existing capability lets Staff record each draft kind? Recommendation: Refund drafts `refunds.create`; forfeiture and Deposit use `deposits.dispose`; Payment move the existing allocation reversal capability; Final Settlement and corrections need a preparer capability that does not exist. Options: reuse `deposits.dispose`, or approve a new `final_settlements.prepare`. I recommend the new `final_settlements.prepare` because SEC-020 lists "prepare and close Final Settlements" as its own family; this needs your answer.
4. (B, slice 2) May the same person prepare and confirm a draft (for example the Owner alone)? Recommendation: allowed, both actors recorded, because SEC-022 says the system must not require two people; you can still give Staff only the prepare capability.
5. (slice 2) Draft life: Recommendation: a draft can be rejected (approver) or withdrawn (preparer) with a reason; no expiry; confirm fails if the underlying balances changed since the draft.
6. (B, slices 3 and 6) Existing direct paths run at once today: Credit Refunds, Pet Refunds, Reservation Deposit Refunds and dispositions, and termination deposit dispositions. BR-AUTH-010 says Refunds and deposit forfeiture are drafts. Recommendation: from V1-056 every new Refund and every Deposit forfeiture (Lease and Reservation) goes through a draft; the old direct routes return a clear error pointing to the draft route; old records stay unchanged. This changes existing API behavior and existing tests. Please confirm, or limit drafts to Lease-related actions only.
7. (slice 3) Refund methods: one global list like Payment Methods, or per Property? Recommendation: global, like `app.payment_methods`.
8. (slice 5) Does the hand-back VOID or credit Adjustment need a draft and `financial.approve`? BR-AUTH-010 does not list it. Recommendation: no draft; existing `invoices.void` and `invoices.adjust` with a reason.
9. (slice 5) Partly paid hand-back Invoice: the credit Adjustment removes the unpaid rest of every line (Rent and fixed Service lines), consistent with the whole-Invoice VOID. Recommendation: yes, per line.
10. (B, slice 6) How does the system know the forfeiture ground? Termination bases today are `VOLUNTARY_EARLY`, `CUSTOMER_VIOLATION`, `SIGNED_LEASE_OTHER` and `UNPAID_INITIAL_OBLIGATION`; pet versus non-pet violation and the 30-day Rent nonpayment default are not separate bases. Recommendation: the forfeiture draft takes the ground from the termination record (governing rule and linked warnings or Serious Default event) and refuses when it cannot be derived; no new termination basis.
11. (slice 6) Existing disposition functions may accept a partial forfeiture amount. Requiring the full amount for new forfeitures rejects input that was accepted before. Recommendation: enforce full amount only in the new draft path; leave the old function as is (it is no longer reachable once Q6 is approved).
12. (slice 6) "Valid pet damage" means move-out inspection findings with `pet_related = true` that were billed as Damage charges. Recommendation: yes; nothing else counts unless staff link it with a reason.
13. (B, slice 4) Is the cleaning charge issued by an explicit staff command, or created by the system at move-out? Rules say nothing is automatic. Recommendation: explicit command; Final Settlement preview shows a blocking issue until it is issued (or the Customer never moved in).
14. (slice 4) Which Invoice carries it, and what if the Unit has no rate? Recommendation: its own one-time charge Invoice at move-out (the final Utility Invoice may be issued at a different time); a Unit with no configured rate blocks the charge until a rate is set. A rate of 0 is allowed and gives no charge line.
15. (B, slice 10) Post-settlement correction kinds. BR-SETTLE-013 names "extra charge or Refund". Recommendation: only those two kinds in V1-056 (a linked one-time charge Invoice, or a linked Refund draft). Reversal of a Settlement Deposit application or of a Credit application waits for a later owner decision.
16. (slice 7) Payment move limits. Recommendation: same Property only; the whole wrong allocation moves; if the target Invoice owes less, the rest stays as unallocated Payment value (not Credit) until the normal recognition rules apply.
17. Security reviews for slices 5 and 10 in addition to the required ones? Recommendation: yes.
18. Existing tests that assert immediate Refund or disposition behavior will need narrowing if Q6 is approved. Recommendation: narrow only the one expectation per test, keep test names, docs/010 note per test, as the run rules require.

## 5. Risks

- Lock behavior: Deposit and Refund confirms will call `app.assert_payment_excess_eligible` with NOWAIT; the 9f-4 bounded retry is reused, not changed.
- Fixture narrowing for slice 1 and Q6 may touch many tests; each is listed in the slice commit and docs/010.
- Frontend intermittent tests TEST-FE-087, TEST-FE-128 and TEST-FE-488 are recorded if they fail once, not retried.
- TEST-118 fails until slice 12; nothing is pushed before that.

## 6. Owner answers (2026-10-03)

- Q1: Yes. DL-069 is the approval for the month-end End Date rule. Check it in the database functions only, no table constraint, existing Leases untouched.
- Q3: Add the new capability `final_settlements.prepare` for Staff to prepare drafts. Do not reuse `deposits.dispose`.
- Q4: The same person may prepare and confirm a draft only if they hold `financial.approve`. Record both the preparer and the confirmer. A person without `financial.approve` can never confirm.
- Q6: Every Refund and every deposit forfeiture, Reservation ones included, becomes a draft. The old direct routes return a clear error. Narrow the affected old tests one expectation at a time, with a docs/010 note.
- Q10: Take the forfeiture reason from the termination record. Refuse when it cannot be worked out.
- Q13: Staff issue the cleaning charge themselves. The Settlement preview shows it as missing until it is issued.
- Q15: Post-settlement corrections are only "extra charge" and "Refund".
- Q2, Q5, Q7, Q8, Q9, Q11, Q12, Q14, Q16, Q17, Q18: use the recommendation written in section 4.
